- An imported Windows Server 2019 UEFI image on s7n.large.4 gets its fixed IP from DHCP with gateway and DNS 100.125.4.25, and Device Manager shows one Red Hat VirtIO SCSI controller with the system disk (BusType SCSI), eight VirtIO SCSI pass-through controllers, VirtIO Ethernet at 10 Gbps, VirtIO Serial and Balloon, all OK. (KB-4RQH, V3 live, checked 2026-11-03)
- An English Windows Server 2019 Standard Evaluation of the same build as a German one (en-us ISO 17763.3650, UI en-US, time zone W. Europe) prepared on Hyper-V Gen 2 by the same VirtIO steps with the same virtio-win MSI and imported as UEFI gets a working VirtIO network on TCP (s7n.large.4, eu-de-01) at the first start: netkvm running, adapter Up, DHCP address and gateway, no device with a problem. The German evaluation copy of KB-D7S3 failed with code 56, so the failure comes with that German evaluation install and not with the steps or the build number. It is not shown that every German UI fails: a German production server copied on 2026-11-13 installed its NIC by itself on m7n (KB-VOOL). (KB-4ZEN, V3 live, checked 2026-11-20)
- A Windows Server 2019 Gen 2 VM with three disks, prepared on Hyper-V with viostor and vioscsi Start 0 plus DriverDatabase entries and registered with is_config true and uefi, booted on s7n.large.4 in eu-de-01 with GPSSD disks: fixed IP by DHCP, no persistent route, all disks Red Hat VirtIO, data disks Offline and read-only until Set-Disk, then letters, labels and file hashes unchanged (a TCP test tenant, 2026-11-04). (KB-55ZR, V3 live, checked 2026-11-04)
- The Windows Recovery Environment of a Windows Server 2019 image without VirtIO boot drivers, on an s7n.large.4 ECS with an SSD system disk on TCP, lists no volumes in diskpart, so the system disk is not visible to it. (KB-5GEF, V3 live, checked 2026-11-03)
- An uploaded image file is converted to the internal zvhd2 format regardless of the source format, confirmed for a qcow2 upload. The registered image's disk_format field reports that internal format, not the type of the file that was uploaded. (KB-5HII, V3 live, checked 2026-07-23)
- A Windows guest prepared on Hyper-V by removing the default route with Get-NetRoute piped to Remove-NetRoute before Set-NetIPInterface -Dhcp Enabled shows Persistent Routes None after its boot on TCP, and the active default route uses the subnet gateway from DHCP. (KB-5JNH, V3 live, checked 2026-11-03)
- A Windows Server 2012 R2 image (evaluation ISO 9600.17050 with 6 updates, virtio-win 0.1.215, BIOS) imported to TCP boots on s3.large.4 and s2.large.4 (disk, VirtIO NIC with DHCP, file hashes fine) but on s7n.large.4 it stops at boot with bugcheck 0xC000021A, second parameter 0xC0000428 (STATUS_INVALID_IMAGE_HASH), and reboots in a loop; a second crash wrote the dump over the MFT and damaged the volume. (KB-5LB2, V3 live, checked 2026-11-13)
- On a German Windows Server 2019 (de-de 17763.3650, InstallLanguage 0407) moved from Hyper-V Gen 2 to TCP, the setupapi device log shows the VirtIO NIC install with oem4.inf kvmnet6.ndi ending in problem 0x38 CM_PROB_NEED_CLASS_CONFIG, then 'Waiting for device post-install' from 16:07:48 to the timeout at 16:11:33 (FAILURE 0x5b4), and Windows restarts right after; NetSetupSvc started only at the timeout and stopped 3 s later; the network class key of netkvm (Class {4d36e972...} subkey 0002) has no NetCfgInstanceId, ComponentId or DeviceInstanceID, and neither Control Network nor NetworkSetup2 Interfaces has an entry for it, while the old Hyper-V adapter still holds the name Ethernet; later pnputil /install calls end with 0x103 and change nothing. (KB-5LUM, V3 live, checked 2026-11-22)
- An ECS created from an encrypted image is encrypted with the key of the image, and an image created from an encrypted ECS keeps the key of the ECS, while an image registered from an external file is encrypted only when KMS encryption is selected at registration. (KB-5MQE, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- An ECS is captured as a private image with POST /v2/cloudimages/action carrying name and instance_id, which returns a job_id. The value entities.image_id appears in GET /v1/{project_id}/jobs/{job_id} while the job still reads RUNNING, and a 6 to 40 GB system disk reached active in about 90 seconds. (KB-5UCW, V3 live, checked 2026-07-20)
- IMS creates a system-disk image from an existing ECS with POST /v2/cloudimages/action on ims.eu-de.otc.t-systems.com, body with name, description and instance_id. The source server may be running or stopped. The response carries job_id, polled at the IMS jobs endpoint. (KB-6ELY, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-05-22)
- A Glance property PATCH that adds __support_kvm together with the hi1822 properties hiovs, hisriov and hivirtionet applies cleanly even when a property is already present. No HTTP 409 was returned. (KB-6ITH, V3 live, checked 2026-07-21)
- A Windows Server 2008 R2 SP1 disk with disk drivers injected offline (services Start 0 and critical device entries) boots on TCP only once: at that boot Plug and Play cannot install the unsigned 2k8R2 viostor package for the block controller PCI VEN_1AF4 DEV_1001, marks its Enum instance failed install (ConfigFlags 0x40, no Service), and the next boot cannot reach the disk (boot loop). A manual install of viostor in the console then fails with error 1021 (stable subkey under a volatile parent key) when the Enum instance was created at boot. Writing the Enum instance offline as a normal key (PCI VEN_1AF4&DEV_1001&SUBSYS_00021AF4&REV_00, instance 4&e0f7365&0&0828 on s3.large.4, Service viostor, ClassGUID of SCSI adapters, Class SCSIAdapter, ConfigFlags 0, HardwareID and CompatibleIDs) made the controller show as installed under Storage controllers, and the ECS booted again after a restart from inside Windows. (KB-6TXH, V3 live, checked 2026-11-18)
- The SHA-2 update does not fix the in-guest install of the virtio-win 0.1.215 2k8R2 drivers: on a Windows Server 2008 R2 SP1 guest with KB4490628 and KB4474419 installed and restarted (Get-HotFix shows both), pnputil -i -a of viostor.inf, vioscsi.inf and netkvm.inf from the 2k8R2\amd64 folders fails at once with 0xE0000247; the setup log reports "INF hash is not present in the catalog. Driver package appears to be tampered" (0xE000024B), also with the Red Hat signer in the TrustedPublisher store of the machine. Only the offline route with signature check skipped (Add-WindowsDriver on a disk that has never booted) stages these drivers. (KB-6VZR, V3 live, checked 2026-11-17)
- On a German Windows Server 2019 whose adapter got a static address and gateway by New-NetIPAddress -DefaultGateway, one netsh interface ipv4 set address source=dhcp left the default route 0.0.0.0/0 in the PersistentStore (seen after a restart by the step 7 check); a second identical netsh call removed it (EnableDHCP 1, DefaultGateway empty). In round 34 one call was enough. A check of Get-NetRoute -PolicyStore PersistentStore after the switch to DHCP is needed before the export. (KB-6WKP, V3 live, checked 2026-11-22)
- IMS direct import accepts up to 128 GB per disk. A file between 128 GB and 1 TB has to be RAW with a CreateMF.jar bitmap or ZVHD2 with quick import, and a pre-allocated monolithicFlat VMDK has to be converted before import. (KB-6Y34, V1 docs, checked 2026-06-12)
- The 2008 R2 injection recipe of round 27 also works on a large system disk (round 28, s3.large.4): a 150 GB volume from the driverless image, partition and NTFS grown to the whole volume, 137 files of 1 GiB of random data (149.7 of 150 GiB used, as full as the inventory server with 147 of 150 GB), then the injection. Windows booted, restarted itself and booted again, each time within about 4 minutes: Red Hat VirtIO SCSI controller with code 0, viostor and vioscsi running, C: 150 GiB, 137 data files plus the list, SHA-256 of two sampled files equal to the list, no dump. The volume content is the same as after the RAW pieces and dd path, which is proven for Windows system disks above 128 GB. (KB-73I6, V3 live, checked 2026-11-18)
- Private images of a tenant are listed with GET /v2/cloudimages?__imagetype=private. Each returned image reports its size in bytes in the field __image_size. (KB-7ICA, V3 live, checked 2026-07-09)
- An image is shared across tenants by the owner with POST /v1/cloudimages/members on ims.eu-de.otc.t-systems.com, naming the image and the recipient project. The recipient accepts with PUT on the same path with status accepted. Both calls returned 200 with a job_id. (KB-7JZT, V3 live, checked 2026-07-21)
- In eu-de a 10 GB Ubuntu 24.04 system disk image was exported to an OBS bucket as qcow2 with POST /v1/cloudimages/{image_id}/file in about 141 seconds, registered again from that file with POST /v2/cloudimages/action (image_url bucket:file, os_version, min_disk) in about 325 seconds, and an s3.medium.1 ECS booted from the new image. (KB-7NZQ, V3 live, checked 2026-11-20)
- IMS refuses a data image import of a fixed VHD file whose min_disk equals the virtual disk size in GB with HTTP 400 IMG.0271 (minimum disk space smaller than the source image file size), because a fixed VHD file carries a 512-byte footer after the disk; min_disk must be at least one GB above the virtual size. (KB-7OJZ, V3 live, checked 2026-11-11)
- An IMS system disk image is created from a running instance with POST /v2/cloudimages/action carrying instance_id, and the image reaches state active before the source instance may be deleted. (KB-7X4U, V3 live, checked 2026-07-29)
- An external image file is registered by uploading it to OBS, calling POST /v2/cloudimages/action with os_version set, then polling GET /v1/{project_id}/jobs/{job_id}. A 20 MiB VMDK reached active in about four minutes, and an ECS booted from the result in about 60 seconds. (KB-A4ZN, V3 live, checked 2026-06-12)
- The WatchGuard FireboxV 12.12.4 VMware OVA (222085120 bytes) holds one streamOptimized VMDK with a virtual size of 4520 MiB, an OVF without EFI firmware (BIOS boot) and VmxNet3 NICs, so the disk can go to an IMS import directly without a qemu-img step. (KB-AIRA, V3 live, checked 2026-11-10)
- A 20 MiB streamOptimized VMDK imported through IMS in eu-de needed 4 minutes 7 seconds from submission to job state SUCCESS. (KB-AMZY, V3 live, checked 2026-06-12)
- An ECS created from an ISO image registered in IMS is a temporary ECS, and the IMS user guide says disks cannot be attached to it. A backup tool's recovery ISO booted this way cannot be pointed at attached empty data disks to restore into. (KB-B475, V1 docs, checked 2026-10-09)
- In the IMS list of supported OSs for imported private images on TCP, the newest client Windows is Windows 10 64bit. Uploads of unlisted OSs may fail. (KB-BCCA, V1 docs, checked 2026-06-10)
- VirtIO drivers are installed inside the guest while it still runs on the source hypervisor, over the hypervisor console: the install swaps the NIC driver and drops a remote desktop session. The documented package source is the Fedora archive. (KB-BGSF, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-21)
- On German Windows Server 2019 (de-DE) ipconfig /all writes the DHCP line with dots before the colon, for example 'DHCP aktiviert. . . . . . . . . . : Nein'. A text that tells a reader to look for 'DHCP aktiviert: Nein' does not match the screen; name the line 'DHCP aktiviert' and its value 'Nein' or 'Ja' instead. netsh interface ipv4 show config writes 'DHCP aktiviert:' with the colon directly after the words. (KB-BKW3, V3 live, checked 2026-11-22)
- An IMS register call without os_version lands the image as Other(64 bit) with __platform Other, which loses targeted driver and configuration handling for the guest. (KB-BLW3, V3 live, checked 2026-06-12)
- Inside a Windows Server 2008 R2 SP1 guest without the SHA-2 update, pnputil -a of the 2k8R2 viostor.inf of virtio-win 0.1.215 does not stage the driver: the setupapi device log reports INF hash is not present in the catalog (0xE000024B) and the command waits for a prompt, also with the Red Hat certificate in the TrustedPublisher store of the machine. On such a guest the driver goes in offline with Add-WindowsDriver from a newer Windows host, run as SYSTEM. The TrustedPublisher store of the machine does not exist on a fresh 2008 R2, so certutil -addstore TrustedPublisher fails with 0x80070002. (KB-BXHA, V3 live, checked 2026-11-17)
- The external image chain works end to end on TCP: a streamOptimized VMDK produced with qemu-img is uploaded to OBS, registered with IMS and boots as an ECS reachable over SSH. (KB-C3NV, V3 live, checked 2026-06-12)
- An IMS system-disk image created with POST /v2/cloudimages/action and an instance_id, and a CBR backup, both sit outside the EVS disk lifecycle and survive an ECS teardown. (KB-C4YY, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- An IMS import has a floor of about four minutes regardless of file size. A 20 MiB file took 4 minutes 7 seconds. (KB-CHLR, V3 live, checked 2026-06-12)
- The same Windows Server 2008 R2 SP1 image (Hyper-V Generation 1, virtio-win 0.1.215 2k8R2 drivers added offline, BIOS) that boots on s3.large.4 also boots on TCP eu-de as ECS s7n.large.4: on 2026-11-17 the guest ran from a VirtIO SCSI disk with viostor and vioscsi Running, the VirtIO Ethernet adapter took its address by DHCP with the gateway set, and the probe answered 186 s after the ECS was ACTIVE. Windows Server 2012 R2 with virtio-win 0.1.215 failed on s7n with bugcheck 0xC0000428 in the earlier rounds, so the flavor limit seen there does not apply to 2008 R2. (KB-CHOU, V3 live, checked 2026-11-17)
- The checksum field of an image imported into IMS from an OBS file held the MD5 of the two characters {}, not a hash taken over the file. (KB-CIHR, V3 live, checked 2026-10-01)
- An IMS import with is_config set to true runs automatic configuration, which for Linux images deletes VMware Tools services and copies built-in VirtIO drivers into initrd or initramfs, with documented exceptions for the driver copy such as a separate /usr partition or CentOS 8.0 on XFS. (KB-CKIK, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- IMS fast import (Enable Fast Create, RAW or ZVHD2 up to 1 TB) performs no driver injection, unlike the normal import up to 128 GB. A Windows disk above 128 GB therefore reaches TCP only with the VirtIO drivers it already carries from the source. (KB-CM6Z, V1 docs, checked 2026-10-09)
- A dynamic VHDX file is accepted as an external image file. A 72 MiB VHDX registered to active in 104 seconds, and an ECS created from it reached ACTIVE with a DHCP address in 30 seconds. (KB-CUSR, V3 live, checked 2026-09-02)
- IMS cannot export a private image created from a Windows, SUSE, Red Hat, Ubuntu or Oracle Linux public image, and it cannot export full-ECS images or ISO images either. (KB-CVA4, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- A German Windows Server 2019 Standard Evaluation (de-de ISO 17763.3650, UI de-DE) prepared on Hyper-V Gen 2 by guide chapter 1 steps 1 to 8 and imported as UEFI boots on TCP (s7n.large.4, eu-de-01) in under a minute, restarts once by itself and runs viostor and vioscsi, but its VirtIO network card does not start: the setupapi device log of the INF folder shows netkvm 100.103.104.30200 taken from the driver store (oem4.inf) at the first start, then problem 0x38 (CM_PROB_NEED_CLASS_CONFIG) and 'Timed out waiting for device post-install' after 4 minutes; the netkvm service stays stopped, Windows has no network adapter and no address, also after a soft restart. (KB-D7S3, V3 live, checked 2026-11-20)
- A dynamic VHDX system disk of 118853992448 bytes (110.7 GiB file, Windows Server 2019, BIOS) registered from OBS with POST /v2/cloudimages/action and min_disk 120 took 2301 s until the image was active, against 500 to 750 s for 9 to 22 GB files in earlier runs; the console shows the progress in percent meanwhile. (KB-DCLN, V3 live, checked 2026-11-13)
- Convert-VHD of a fixed VHD into a dynamic VHDX keeps the blocks of deleted files: a 200 GB system disk with 11 GB in use and 100 GB of deleted files gave a 119.5 GB VHDX, and Optimize-VHD -Mode Full on the copy mounted read only freed nothing. Mounting the copy writable on the host, Optimize-Volume -ReTrim on its NTFS volume, dismounting and then Optimize-VHD -Mode Full gave 10.6 GB, and the image of that file booted on TCP with BIOS firmware with all file hashes unchanged. (KB-DFCO, V3 live, checked 2026-11-11)
- virtio-win-gt-x64.msi 0.1.302 (the VirtIO driver installer) does not install on Windows Server 2012 R2 (6.3.9600, VersionNT 603): msiexec ends with 1603 and the log says LaunchConditions 'OS must be Windows 10 or higher'; a 2012 R2 guest needs the drivers from another source. (KB-DJD3, V3 live, checked 2026-11-12)
- A Windows Server 2019 Gen 2 VHDX prepared only by script on Hyper-V (virtio MSI installed online, then viostor Start 0, its file and DriverDatabase entries written by a guest script, static address switched to DHCP) registers in 512 seconds for a 9.75 GB file and answers RDP on its fixed IP within about a minute after the ECS create job ends on s7n.large.4. (KB-DJIM, V3 live, checked 2026-11-03)
- virt-v2v makes viostor bootable offline on Windows 8 and later with a Services key (Start 0, Type 1, Group SCSI miniport, ErrorControl 1) and DriverDatabase keys for the PCI ids of the legacy and modern virtio-blk device. It uses the CriticalDeviceDatabase only when the SYSTEM hive has no DriverDatabase. (KB-DN4H, V1 docs, checked 2026-11-03)
- Signed VirtIO drivers for Windows Server 2008 R2 exist: the virtio-win ISOs 0.1.173, 0.1.189, 0.1.204, 0.1.215 and 0.1.240 carry viostor, vioscsi and NetKVM in 2k8R2 and 2k8 folders with valid production signatures (Red Hat, Inc via DigiCert or VeriSign), and the IMS list of supported OSs for external image files names Windows Server 2008 R2 Standard, Enterprise and Datacenter 64bit. (KB-DZK6, V3 live, checked 2026-11-13)
- A Windows Server 2012 R2 guest has no PowerShell Direct (the Hyper-V host needs a Windows Server 2016 or newer guest for it) and no Get-ComputerInfo (PowerShell 4.0); a lab reaches it over WinRM and reads the firmware type from the environment variable firmware_type. (KB-E56O, V3 live, checked 2026-11-21)
- The IMS user guide says a Windows ECS that will be joined to a domain needs Sysprep before the private image is created, run as sysprep.exe /generalize /oobe /unattend:Unattend.xml from the Cloudbase-Init conf folder; Windows must be activated with a remaining rearm count of at least 1, and the ECS stops after the command. (KB-E5JF, V1 docs, checked 2026-11-12)
- POST /v2/cloudimages/action on the IMS endpoint with name and instance_id answers HTTP 200 with a job_id and queues an image of the server system disk. The image covers that system disk, and data disks of the same server stay outside it. (KB-EACF, V3 live, checked 2026-06-23)
- On a Windows Server 2019 Gen 2 guest on Hyper-V, virtio-win-gt-x64.msi 0.1.302 installed silently (exit 0) and then three normal restarts changed nothing visible: no new System log errors, no device with a problem, no pending restart, no viostor or vioscsi service key, network and data unchanged. The later boot start and DriverDatabase steps then gave their expected check values. (KB-EMOQ, V3 live, checked 2026-11-11)
- The RAW-piece path also brings a data disk with more than 128 GB of real data: a 200 GB GPT/NTFS data disk with 140 GB in 140 files of 1 GB, sent as fixed VHD, split into four 50 GiB RAW pieces imported as data images (452 to 693 s), written with dd onto an empty 200 GB GPSSD volume (52 min); 40 of 40 regions MD5-equal, the NTFS volume mounts read-only on Linux and all 140 file hashes and the list hash equal the source. (KB-ETV6, V3 live, checked 2026-11-13)
- A Full-ECS image, which bundles the system disk together with attached data disks, uses POST /v1/cloudimages/wholeimages/action with the same body shape as the system disk image call POST /v2/cloudimages/action plus vault_id naming a CBR vault. (KB-F54F, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- On a German Windows Server 2019 (de-de 17763.3650) moved from Hyper-V Gen 2 to TCP, starting NetSetupSvc at once at boot (a SYSTEM startup task, about 60 s after boot, restarting it every 10 s) and freeing the adapter name Ethernet on the source do not clear the VirtIO NIC problem code 56: NetSetupSvc runs for the whole post-install wait, stops at about 4 minutes, Windows restarts by itself, and the second boot shows code 56 again with netkvm Stopped. (KB-FAFI, V3 live, checked 2026-11-22)
- An IMS import that read its source file from a Standard-class OBS bucket in the same region succeeded in eu-de with a min_disk of 10 GB. The console import form requires at least 40 GB for the same operation. (KB-FFWI, V3 live, checked 2026-06-12)
- The platform converts an external image on ingest. An image registered from a VHDX file reports disk_format zvhd2, not vhdx, so the uploaded format is not the stored format. (KB-FMM7, V3 live, checked 2026-09-02)
- A Windows Server 2019 Gen 2 disk with viostor and vioscsi Start 0 and their .sys files in System32\drivers but without the DriverDatabase entries of a guestor.inf package for VEN_1AF4&DEV_1001 and DEV_1042 boots on TCP to a blue screen and then the recovery environment; the entries can be added offline on a Linux helper with ntfs-3g mounted read-write and python3-hivex on Windows/System32/config/SYSTEM (DriverInfFiles, DriverPackages with a 48-byte Version, Configurations, DeviceIds, Descriptors). (KB-FUW2, V3 live, checked 2026-11-13)
- Inside a Windows Server 2019 ECS booted from an image prepared without any preparation script, Administrator login, the fixed IP by DHCP, one default route with no persistent route, gateway ping, DNS and all VirtIO devices with status OK were confirmed, so the typed commands reach the same result as the script path. (KB-FVA5, V3 live, checked 2026-11-04)
- Windows Server 2008 R2 SP1 Standard (evaluation 7601.17514) built on Hyper-V Generation 1 with the 2k8R2 drivers of virtio-win 0.1.215 added offline by DISM and viostor and vioscsi set to Start 0 offline after the first Hyper-V boot boots on TCP eu-de as ECS s3.large.4 from a direct VHDX import (BIOS image, import 452 s): the guest ran from a VirtIO SCSI disk with viostor and vioscsi Running, the VirtIO Ethernet adapter took its address by DHCP with the gateway set, a marker file kept its SHA-256, and the probe answered 183 s after the ECS was ACTIVE. Balloon and serial devices stay without a driver (code 28). (KB-FXTH, V3 live, checked 2026-11-17)
- PATCH /v2/cloudimages/{image_id} on IMS answers HTTP 400 IMG.0001 to a remove operation without a value field. The same remove with "value": "" deletes the attribute. (KB-FZJP, V3 live, checked 2026-10-01)
- On Windows Server 2008 R2 SP1 without the SHA-2 update, Plug and Play at the first boot on TCP finds the virtio-win 0.1.215 2k8R2 NetKVM INF through DevicePath (C:\tcp\virtio\NetKVM added offline) but verifies the package catalog with 0x80096002 (certificate of the signer invalid or not found), rates the package Not digitally signed and ends with Driver Store import failed; the VirtIO NIC stays at problem code 1 and the guest has no address. A hardware-initiated install does not take an unsigned package without a user. (KB-GEQD, V3 live, checked 2026-11-18)
- A dynamic VHDX with a virtual size of 150 GB and a file of 0.84 GB registered directly with POST /v1/cloudimages/dataimages/action (min_disk 150, no conversion, no fast import) became an active DataImage in 140 s in eu-de, disk_format zvhd2, __lazyloading true. The documented 128 GB limit of the normal import did not refuse it. (KB-GGEI, V3 live, checked 2026-11-06)
- A Windows Server 2019 UEFI VHDX with VirtIO installed online on Hyper-V (viostor and vioscsi in the driver store only, no service) registers to active on TCP in 513 seconds for a 9.75 GB file, but on s7n.large.4 with an SSD system disk it does not boot normally: about 5 minutes after the create the VNC console shows the Windows Recovery Environment screen 'Choose your keyboard layout' and RDP does not answer. (KB-GJCA, V3 live, checked 2026-11-03)
- An external image file must be uploaded to an OBS bucket in the same region as the image that is registered from it. (KB-GJIJ, V1 docs, checked 2026-07-30)
- Public image names in TCP eu-de follow Standard_Ubuntu_24.04_* for Ubuntu 24, Enterprise_RedHat_9_* for RHEL 9 and Enterprise_Windows-Server_* for Windows Server, as listed on 2026-09-25. (KB-GP36, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- An ECS rebuilt from an IMS image keeps the installed software but presents a new machine identity to a node-locked license manager, which requires a fresh activation. A license left on a deleted instance needs a vendor reset to recover. (KB-GTQH, V3 live, checked 2026-06-17)
- IMS quick import (POST /v2/cloudimages/quickimport/action) of a RAW system disk object of 214748364800 bytes without a bitmap file is refused at once with HTTP 400 IMG.0158 (raw image files need bitmaps uploaded to OBS together with the image file). (KB-GVQH, V3 live, checked 2026-11-12)
- The same Windows Server 2012 R2 image (9600.17050 plus 6 updates, virtio-win 0.1.215, BIOS) boots on the older flavor families s2, s3 and c4 (large.4) and does not boot on the newer families s7n, c7n and s9 in eu-de-01. (KB-GZPY, V3 live, checked 2026-11-13)
- An ECS on s3.large.1 in eu-de from the WatchGuard FireboxV 12.12.4 VMDK, imported with is_config false and no disk bus property, loads the Fireware kernel and then halts with 'initrd: Fatal error: can't mount root image: Invalid argument' and 'reboot: System halted' about 34 seconds into the boot. (KB-H2EX, V3 live, checked 2026-11-10)
- A system disk image of a stopped Windows Server 2019 UEFI ECS (120 GB GPSSD, about 100 GB used) created with POST /v2/cloudimages/action and instance_id took 1148 s until active; it keeps hw_firmware_type uefi and min_disk 120 of the source. (KB-HB35, V3 live, checked 2026-11-13)
- The RAW-piece path for a Windows system disk with more than 128 GB of real data also works for Generation 2 (UEFI, GPT): a 200 GB disk with 140 GB of data, sent as fixed VHD without its footer, split in OBS into four 50 GiB RAW pieces imported as data images (512 to 693 s), written with dd by a Linux helper onto the 200 GB system volume of a stopped ECS built from a uefi image (dd 52 min, 40 of 40 regions of 5 GiB MD5-equal); the volume back as /dev/vda, Windows booted, the 140-file list hash and the system file hash equal, viostor and vioscsi running, no device with a problem, the same after a soft restart. (KB-HGXQ, V3 live, checked 2026-11-12)
- Section 3.10.1 of the TCP service description (revision 09.10.2026) provides public images and GPU drivers for use on T Cloud Public only and prohibits downloading, copying, storing or using them in other environments. (KB-HQFZ, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- On 2026-10-05 the eu-de public image Standard_Ubuntu_24.04_amd64_bios_latest (also as _uefi_latest) carried __support_kvm and __support_kvm_hi1822_hiovs true, so it fits both s3 flavors (condition __support_kvm) and s9 flavors (condition __support_kvm plus hi1822_hiovs); min_disk 4 GB, min_ram 1 GB. (KB-HTPH, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-10-05)
- IMS documentation requires VMware Tools to be fully uninstalled from a Windows source image for the guest driver installation to succeed after import. (KB-HV27, V1 docs, checked 2026-06-12)
- A Windows scheduled task as SYSTEM that writes the viostor DriverDatabase entries with reg.exe on a Windows Server 2019 guest took about 2 seconds; LastTaskResult reads 267009 while it runs and 0 after, and reg.exe failures would not change that 0 unless the script exits with them. (KB-HYT4, V3 live, checked 2026-11-04)
- IMS automatic configuration of a Windows image file restores the IDE driver and DHCP and regenerates the mouse and keyboard registry keys, and it tries an offline VirtIO driver injection. That injection does not work for UEFI images (such as Hyper-V Generation 2 VMs) and can fail with Group Policy or antivirus software, so it is no substitute for drivers installed on the source. (KB-IDSJ, V1 docs, checked 2026-10-09)
- The documentation lists Windows Server 2012, 2012 R2, 2016, 2019 and 2022 among the guest operating systems that support UEFI boot mode. (KB-IHCW, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-21)
- A vanilla Windows Server 2019 Gen 2 disk (no VirtIO package, no viostor service, nothing in the driver store) boots on TCP (s7n.large.4, uefi image) after a full offline injection on a Linux helper: viostor.sys and vioscsi.sys taken from virtio-win-gt-x64.msi 0.1.302 with msiextract and copied to Windows/System32/drivers, services viostor and vioscsi created in the SYSTEM hive with python3-hivex (Type 1, Start 0, ErrorControl 1, Group SCSI miniport, ImagePath, Parameters\PnpInterface 5=1) plus the guestor DriverDatabase entries for VEN_1AF4&DEV_1001&REV_00 and DEV_1042&REV_01; a startup task in the guest then logged model OpenStack Nova, the disk with bus type Fibre Channel and viostor Running, and no crash dump was written. No NIC driver was injected, so the guest had no IP address. (KB-ISRN, V3 live, checked 2026-11-16)
- The NetSetup service trace of Windows Server 2019 (Windows Logs NetSetup service.0.etl, 17763) is a WPP trace: netsh trace convert on another Windows Server shows every event of NetSetupEngine, NetSetupApi and NetSetupSvc as Unknown with No Format Information found, so the reason of a failed network class setup cannot be read from it without the Microsoft format files. (KB-JBBO, V3 live, checked 2026-11-22)
- Image support tags are changed with PATCH /v2/images/{image_id} on ims.eu-de.otc.t-systems.com using Content-Type application/openstack-images-v2.1-json-patch, where op add, remove and replace all work. The same PATCH against /v2/cloudimages/{image_id} with application/json returns IMG.0001 about an invalid request message format. (KB-JFK4, V3 live, checked 2026-07-20)
- The whole 2008 R2 injection recipe in one pass works on a volume that has never booted on TCP (round 27, s3.large.4): a volume made from the driverless image, all offline changes on a Linux helper (viostor and vioscsi sys files, services Start 0, critical device entries, the Enum instance of the block controller with Service viostor and ConfigFlags 0, BCD recovery off, the NetKVM package in DevicePath), then set as root of an ECS from the same image. Windows booted, restarted itself from inside and booted again; both times Red Hat VirtIO SCSI controller with problem code 0, viostor and vioscsi running, no dump. The NIC is not installed by this: after the first boot Windows gives it a null driver (it shows as Ethernet Controller with code 0 but has no address and ping to the gateway fails), so the manual console install of NetKVM is still needed. (KB-JJIS, V3 live, checked 2026-11-18)
- The IMS image attribute virtual_env_type takes only the values FusionCompute, Ironic, DataImage and IsoImage. A normal virtual ECS system-disk image carries FusionCompute, so that value is not a symptom of a faulty import. (KB-JKZY, V1 docs, checked 2026-07-30)
- An IMS import from an OBS file takes hw_firmware_type uefi in the body of POST /v2/cloudimages/action. The image becomes active with hw_firmware_type uefi. (KB-JLKQ, V3 live, checked 2026-10-01)
- A Windows, SUSE, Red Hat, Ubuntu or Oracle Linux private image that was created from an external image file can be exported, unlike one built from the matching public image. (KB-JN7G, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- POST /v1/cloudimages/dataimages/action with name, image_url, min_disk and os_type registers a dynamic VHDX as a data disk image; files of 0.8 GB and 0.3 GB of 20 and 10 GB disks were active in about 140 seconds, and POST /v1.1/{project_id}/cloudservers with data_volumes carrying data_image_id creates the ECS with those disks. (KB-JU2D, V3 live, checked 2026-11-04)
- msiextract on virtio-win-gt-x64.msi 0.1.302 flattens the per-OS builds (2k16, 2k19, 2k22, 2k25, w10) of netkvm into one folder and the Windows Server 2025 build wins (inf 18828 bytes). Installed on Windows Server 2019 (17763) it binds to the NIC but does not load: System event 26 'netkvm.sys cannot find NDIS.SYS NdisRegisterPoll', Kernel-PnP 411 status 0xC0000263, device CM_PROB_DRIVER_FAILED_LOAD, adapter Not Present. Offline injection must take the files by their 2k19 File table keys from the MSI cabinet (msiinfo extract plus cabextract) or from the 2k19 folder of the ISO. (KB-KQWD, V3 live, checked 2026-11-16)
- A Windows Server 2008 R2 SP1 system disk that had booted on Hyper-V (Gen 1) and has no VirtIO driver boots on TCP (s3.large.4, BIOS image) after an offline injection on a Linux helper with python3-hivex: viostor.sys and vioscsi.sys of virtio-win 0.1.215 2k8R2 copied to System32\drivers, services viostor and vioscsi (Type 1, Start 0, ErrorControl 1, Group SCSI miniport, Parameters BusType 1 and PnpInterface 5=1) and CriticalDeviceDatabase keys pci#ven_1af4&dev_1001 and dev_1042 (viostor), dev_1004 and dev_1048 (vioscsi) with the class of SCSI adapters. Inside: disk Red Hat VirtIO SCSI Disk Device, viostor and vioscsi RUNNING. No action inside Windows before the move. (KB-KR7I, V3 live, checked 2026-11-18)
- In virtio-win 0.1.215 the 2k8R2 catalogs of viostor, vioscsi and NetKVM are signed with a SHA-1 digest and a counter-signature (Red Hat, Inc), so Windows Server 2008 R2 SP1 without the SHA-2 update can verify them. (KB-KSRG, V3 live, checked 2026-11-16)
- TCP has no FreeBSD public image and no FreeBSD price tier in eu-de. (KB-KVBP, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- A vanilla Windows Server 2019 Gen 2 disk (no guide step) gets a working VirtIO network on TCP without any action inside Windows before the move: offline on a Linux helper put the 2k19 netkvm package (netkvm.inf, .sys, .cat, netkvmp.exe, taken by their 2k19 File table keys from the cab of virtio-win-gt-x64.msi 0.1.302) into C:\tcp\virtio and create a service whose cmd.exe only starts the work script with start /b; the work script runs pnputil /add-driver netkvm.inf /install. At the next boot the adapter is OK and Up, takes its DHCP address and answers a ping to the subnet gateway. Disk drivers as in KB-ISRN. (KB-LA7N, V3 live, checked 2026-11-16)
- Image Management Service (IMS) manages the operating system images from which Elastic Cloud Servers are created: public images provided by Telekom, private images the customer creates from a server or uploads as an image file, and images shared between tenants. The public images named in the service description are SUSE Linux Enterprise Server, Oracle Linux, Red Hat Enterprise Linux and Microsoft Windows Server. The Regions and Endpoints page lists API endpoints for IMS in eu-de and eu-nl. (KB-LC4V, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- IMS refuses the direct import of a dynamic VHDX system disk file of 161937883136 bytes (200 GB virtual, 140 GB of real data, after ReTrim and Optimize-VHD Full) at once with HTTP 400 IMG.0059; compaction cannot bring a disk with more than 128 GB of real data under the file limit. (KB-LVQH, V3 live, checked 2026-11-12)
- Windows Server 2025 public images are live in eu-de. A listing of Windows public images in that region returned 22 images, all Standard edition, marked STD or STD-CORE. (KB-M3JR, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- A private image created from a running ECS inherits the source image's __support_kvm tags and its hw_firmware_type. (KB-MPY3, V3 live, checked 2026-07-20)
- A Windows guest imported to TCP after a static address was switched to DHCP with Set-NetIPInterface on Hyper-V keeps the old gateway as a persistent default route (0.0.0.0 under Persistent Routes) and the hidden Hyper-V adapter keeps its old address in the registry. The VirtIO adapter gets a new interface, the active default route uses the subnet gateway from DHCP. (KB-MW5X, V3 live, checked 2026-11-03)
- The image import path is decided per single disk file, not per VM: up to 128 GB the supported formats import directly, from 128 GB to 1 TB only RAW with a bitmap file or ZVHD2, and 1 TB is the documented maximum for one disk file. (KB-MXW6, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-21)
- After acceptance the recipient finds the image with GET /v2/cloudimages?id=<image id>, where it reports __imagetype=shared and keeps the same image id as in the owner tenant. An ECS can be created from it. (KB-N7EM, V3 live, checked 2026-07-21)
- On an IMS image import job the field entities.image_id is already filled while the job status is still RUNNING, about 15 seconds into the job, and the status turned to SUCCESS about 260 seconds in on the recorded run. (KB-NDOR, V3 live, checked 2026-07-30)
- Creating a whole-ECS image from a CBR backup through the IMS wholeimages API requires the field whole_image_type set to CBR (API reference). On 2026-09-25 a request with a server backup id and no whole_image_type was refused with HTTP 400 IMG.0198 (backup id does not match the backup type); in February the same case returned IMG.1061. (KB-NDXN, V3 live, checked 2026-09-25)
- After virtio-win-gt-x64.msi 0.1.302 is installed in a Windows Server 2019 guest running on Hyper-V, viostor and vioscsi exist only as driver store packages: sc.exe qc returns error 1060 for both and no VirtIO device exists. Whether such an image boots on TCP is not shown by this. (KB-NTQL, V3 live, checked 2026-11-03)
- Other private images are exported with POST /v1/cloudimages/{image_id}/file to a Standard OBS bucket in the same region as VMDK, VHD, QCOW2, ZVHD or ZVHD2 files, for images smaller than 1 TiB. Images larger than 128 GiB go only through fast export, which writes ZVHD2 and needs an unencrypted image. (KB-NX4W, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- Add-WindowsDriver from a Windows Server 2022 Hyper-V host into the exported disk of a Windows Server 2008 R2 SP1 VM that has run fails with Access is denied (0x80070005, setupapi.offline.log: Failed to update active driver store entry) for an administrator: the store index files drvindex.dat, INFCACHE.1, infpub.dat, infstor.dat and infstrng.dat in System32\DriverStore carry SYSTEM full control and Users read only. A disk applied fresh from install.wim and never booted took the same drivers without this error. (KB-O6R7, V3 live, checked 2026-11-17)
- Image deletion uses the Glance-native path: DELETE /v2/images/{image_id} on the IMS endpoint returns 204, while DELETE /v2/cloudimages/{id} returns HTTP 404 with APIGW.0101 "method DELETE not found". (KB-ODE7, V3 live, checked 2026-07-09)
- On a Windows system volume created from an image, background loading reached 100 percent after 438 s. The machine already answered when about 7 percent was loaded. (KB-ON4R, V3 live, checked 2026-09-02)
- A second load of the same model inside the same instance runs from the OS page cache and took about 50 minutes instead of 2.5 hours. An instance newly created from an IMS image of that system starts with a cold page cache and loads at cold speed again. (KB-P6WA, V3 live, checked 2026-05-07)
- An ECS created from a system disk image takes its flavor and system disk type from the create step rather than from the image, with a system disk at least as large as the min_disk of the image. When an ECS is created from a full-ECS image, it gets the system and data disks of the image by default and their sizes can grow but not shrink. (KB-PAHE, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- Add-WindowsDriver run as SYSTEM (scheduled task) from a Windows Server 2022 Hyper-V host into the exported disk of a Windows Server 2008 R2 SP1 VM that has run fails the same way as for an administrator: the offline setup log shows "idb: Failed to update active driver store entry, Error = 0x00000005" and "Failed to rollback driver package registration, Error = 0x00000032" for viostor, vioscsi and NetKVM. SYSTEM can create files in Windows\inf, DriverStore, FileRepository and System32\drivers of that disk and open drvindex.dat, INFCACHE.1 and infpub.dat for write; resetting the transaction log of the volume (fsutil resource setautoreset) and rewriting the index files as plain files did not help. A failed try leaves stale entries in infpub.dat, so a later Add-WindowsDriver reports success without adding the driver. So the host-side DISM route does not work for a 2008 R2 VM that has booted. (KB-PFMC, V3 live, checked 2026-11-17)
- An appliance image in qcow2 format that brings its own kernel and drivers is imported into IMS with is_config false, the API default, so the import skips automatic configuration. On Linux that step, among other changes, removes PV drivers, rewrites grub and fstab to UUIDs, deletes VMware Tools services and copies VirtIO drivers into the initrd, which can misconfigure such a guest. (KB-PLD6, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- On an English Windows Server 2019 guest, Get-NetFirewallRule -Group '@FirewallAPI.dll,-28752' lists the 3 Remote Desktop rules (User Mode TCP-In, User Mode UDP-In, Shadow TCP-In), and Enable-NetFirewallRule with that group turns all three on. (KB-PPJC, V3 live, checked 2026-11-04)
- PowerShell blocks can be run inside a Windows Server 2008 R2 Hyper-V VM that has no WinRM and no PowerShell Direct: on the mounted VHDX write Windows\System32\GroupPolicy\Machine\Scripts\scripts.ini (UTF-16, [Startup], 0CmdLine and 0Parameters) and a gpt.ini with the scripts extension GUIDs, start the VM; the startup script runs as SYSTEM under PowerShell 2.0 and can shut the VM down; then read the output offline and put the old files back. (KB-PX6U, V3 live, checked 2026-11-22)
- A Windows Server 2019 Gen 2 VHDX prepared on Hyper-V with viostor Start 0, viostor.sys in System32 drivers and virt-v2v style DriverDatabase entries for the virtio-blk PCI ids, imported to IMS with hw_firmware_type uefi and is_config true, boots to the lock screen on s7n.large.4 about 4 minutes after the ECS create. (KB-QOKP, V3 live, checked 2026-11-03)
- The Glance-native route for the same cross-tenant image share is POST /v2/images/{id}/members by the owner, followed by PUT /v2/images/{id}/members/{member_id} with status accepted by the recipient. (KB-R46O, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- IMS requires VirtIO (KVM guest OS) drivers to be installed in a Windows image file on the source platform before export. Without them, ECSs created from the image fail to start. DHCP must also be configured in the image. (KB-RB2V, V1 docs, checked 2026-10-09)
- The new image is followed with GET /v2/cloudimages?name={name} and moves through status queued, then saving, then active. For a system disk of about 80 GB, reaching active took minutes. (KB-RI7K, V3 live, checked 2026-06-23)
- On Windows Server 2008 R2 SP1 (Hyper-V Gen 1 guest, 2026-11-17 round 24d), the commands "netsh interface ipv4 set address name=NAME source=dhcp" and "netsh interface ipv4 set dnsservers name=NAME source=dhcp" switch a static NIC to DHCP at once: ipconfig then shows DHCP Enabled Yes with a lease from the subnet DHCP server. "netsh interface ipv4 show config" also lists Loopback Pseudo-Interface 1 with DHCP enabled No, which must be left alone. (KB-RIYK, V3 live, checked 2026-11-17)
- A Windows system disk with more than 128 GB of real data (Gen 1, 200 GB, 140 GB data) reaches TCP without bitmap tools: the RAW disk is split in OBS into 50 GiB RAW pieces, each imported as a data image (about 10 min each, in parallel), a Linux helper writes the piece volumes with dd onto the 200 GB system volume of a stopped BIOS Windows ECS (35 min, then 40 regions of 5 GiB MD5-equal), the volume goes back as /dev/vda; Windows booted in 2 min with all disks, letters and 140 file hashes equal, also after a soft restart. (KB-RL3N, V3 live, checked 2026-11-12)
- After an import from OBS, the private image records its source in __data_origin as file,<bucket>:<object key> and its stored size in __image_size; a 118853992448-byte dynamic VHDX gave __image_size 59219398656, about half the file. A PATCH of hw_firmware_type on such an image also works through a plain JSON patch list body. (KB-RSU2, V3 live, checked 2026-11-13)
- IMS requires DHCP in an imported Linux image file, otherwise ECS startup or networking is abnormal, and a source VM with a static IP address has to be switched to DHCP so that the new ECS takes its address from the subnet. (KB-RU6H, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- The IMS list of OSs supported for external image files names Windows Server 2022 as the newest Windows Server. Windows Server 2025 is not listed, so its import may fail. (KB-S6QF, V1 docs, checked 2026-10-09)
- The tool package for fast import that the IMS documentation links (bucket obs-20181128-ims in eu-de, DT-image-convert-tools.zip with qemu-img-hw and CreateMF.jar) is gone: a GET on the bucket through a signed call returned 404 NoSuchBucket on 2026-11-06, and the docs mirror names no other source. (KB-S7TT, V3 live, checked 2026-11-06)
- On a Windows Server 2019 guest on Hyper-V with no VirtIO device, viostor and vioscsi set with sc.exe to start= boot keep Start 0x0 in the registry through a shutdown and boot, while sc.exe qc shows DEMAND_START after that boot and the services run. sc.exe qc alone does not tell the stored start value; read the registry. (KB-SKYF, V3 live, checked 2026-11-03)
- ntfs-3g on a Linux helper ECS refuses a read-write mount of the system volume of a Windows ECS that was stopped hard (unclean file system, it falls back to read-only); after one boot and a soft stop of the Windows ECS the same volume mounts read-write. (KB-SKZ6, V3 live, checked 2026-11-21)
- A private system disk image of a running ECS (s3.medium.1, 10 GB SAS system disk, Ubuntu 24.04) was created with POST /v2/cloudimages/action and instance_id; the IMS job reached SUCCESS after about 167 seconds, without stopping the server. (KB-SZAS, V3 live, checked 2026-11-20)
- A Windows Server 2019 Gen 2 VHDX prepared on Hyper-V with viostor Start 0 and viostor.sys in System32 drivers but only the DriverDatabase entries the virtio-win MSI writes lands in the Windows Recovery Environment on s7n.large.4 with no RDP answer. The same disk with added virt-v2v style DriverDatabase entries and unused CriticalDeviceDatabase keys boots. (KB-T453, V3 live, checked 2026-11-03)
- When automatic configuration is enabled for the registration of an image file, IMS records the latest automatic change it made to a Linux image in /var/log/rainbow_modification_record.log. On a Windows image it restores the IDE driver, regenerates the mouse and keyboard registry keys and restores DHCP, so the OS gets its IP address by DHCP. (KB-T7QX, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- The public virtio-win archive named in the IMS documentation answers scripted requests with an HTML bot-check page and HTTP 200, so a scripted MSI download gets HTML; a browser download works. (KB-TDHH, V3 live, checked 2026-11-03)
- The WatchGuard FireboxV 12.12.4 VMDK imported into IMS boots to the 'WatchGuard-Firebox login:' prompt on c7n.large.2 in eu-de-01 once the image carries hw_disk_bus ide: the system disk then shows as /dev/hda in the ECS API and as /dev/sda in the guest, which is one of the root devices the Fireware initrd accepts (sda, hda, xvda, nvme0n1, not vda). (KB-THMW, V3 live, checked 2026-11-11)
- IMS refuses the direct import of a fixed VHD system disk file of 161061274112 bytes (150 GB virtual) at once with HTTP 400 IMG.0059 (size of the image file exceeds the maximum allowed), while dynamic VHDX files with a 200 GB virtual size and a 10 GB file passed; the 128 GB limit is checked on the file size. (KB-TPDJ, V3 live, checked 2026-11-11)
- A vendor appliance image published as a KVM or OpenStack x86-64 build imports through IMS and boots on TCP. (KB-TSYG, V3 live, checked 2026-07-23)
- Creating a system-disk image from a Windows ECS with POST /v2/cloudimages/action took about 25 minutes for a 200 GB disk. (KB-TWQP, V3 live, checked 2026-06-11)
- An unactivated Windows Server 2012 R2 evaluation shuts down every hour; a lab VM of it must be activated online before a long run. (KB-U3YC, V3 live, checked 2026-11-21)
- The IMS list of operating systems supported for imported private images has Windows 10 64bit as the newest client Windows, and Windows 11 is absent from it. Uploading an image whose OS is not on the list is documented as possibly failing, and the docs advise contacting customer service before the upload. (KB-ULTQ, V0 recheck due, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- Creating a whole machine image from a CBR backup is done with POST /v1/cloudimages/wholeimages/action and whole_image_type set to CBR. (KB-URBQ, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- Microsoft: duplicate machine SIDs are not a problem for domain members because Windows never exposes the machine SID outside the computer, except on domain controllers (Russinovich, 2009, NewSID retired); software that needs unique identities uses the computer name or the domain SID of the computer account. A copied domain controller is protected only if the hypervisor exposes VM-Generation ID: then it resets its invocation ID and discards its RID pool; without it the copy boots normally with the potential for a duplicate domain controller. (KB-V5EV, V1 docs, checked 2026-11-16)
- A single image is read with GET /v2/cloudimages?id={image_id} on ims.eu-de.otc.t-systems.com. The image sits in the images array, status is active once the image is usable, __image_size gives the file size in bytes as a string and min_disk is in GB. (KB-VAUK, V3 live, checked 2026-09-25)
- For Windows Server 2012 and 2012 R2 the newest virtio-win release with production-signed drivers is 0.1.215: its 2k12R2 and 2k12 catalogs carry the Red Hat, Inc. signature cross-signed to VeriSign (valid, timestamped), from 0.1.217 on they carry only the self-signed test certificate CN=Red Hat Inc., OU=Dev, O=virtio-win, which a 2012 R2 guest refuses (0x800b0109); MSIs up to 0.1.240 accept VersionNT 602, from 0.1.248 on they require Windows 10. virtio-win-gt-x64.msi 0.1.215 installs silently on 2012 R2 (exit 0) once the Red Hat signer certificate is in LocalMachine\TrustedPublisher; without it msiexec /qn ends with 1603 (0xE0000242, publisher not trusted). (KB-VBKT, V3 live, checked 2026-11-12)
- An IMS import of a 222049280-byte streamOptimized VMDK (virtual size 4520 MiB) from a Standard OBS bucket in eu-de took 443 seconds from POST /v2/cloudimages/action to job SUCCESS; the image id was in the job entities at the first poll after 21 seconds, and the image registered as disk_format zvhd2 with __image_size 206782464. (KB-VCDY, V3 live, checked 2026-11-10)
- An IMS import submitted without os_version registers the image with __os_version set to Other(64 bit) and virtual_env_type FusionCompute instead of ECS. Such an image still provisioned and booted on an s9 flavor, with degraded OS typing and driver handling. (KB-VESX, V3 live, checked 2026-06-12)
- A Windows Server 2019 Gen 2 server prepared on Hyper-V with virtio-win 0.1.302 in the driver store gets its VirtIO network card working on TCP by itself: at the first start ('Geräte werden betriebsbereit gemacht') Windows installs netkvm from the store, the service is netkvm KERNEL_DRIVER DEMAND_START (3) in group NDIS, and the NIC takes its DHCP address; only the disk drivers viostor and vioscsi need boot start and the DriverDatabase entries. (KB-VOOL, V3 live, checked 2026-11-13)
- A private system disk image of a stopped g7v.2xlarge.8 Windows Server 2022 ECS with a 100 GB ESSD system disk, made with POST /v2/cloudimages/action from instance_id with image_tags, finished its IMS job in 6 minutes 44 seconds; the server stayed SHUTOFF throughout. (KB-W4TS, V3 live, checked 2026-11-19)
- An image registered from an external file reports virtual_env_type FusionCompute and __platform Other even when os_version is passed explicitly, and instances created from it still provision and boot. (KB-W65G, V3 live, checked 2026-09-02)
- A German Windows Server 2019 Standard Evaluation (de-de ISO 17763.3650) moved from Hyper-V Gen 2 to TCP (s7n.large.4, UEFI image) keeps its VirtIO NIC at problem code 56 even with a SYSTEM startup task that runs pnputil /add-driver netkvm.inf /install from the driver store after 120 s and then disables and enables the device: pnputil exits 259 (package already oem4.inf, up to date on all targets, 0 added), netkvm stays Stopped, no adapter and no address, on two boots in a row; the disable and enable was followed by a restart of Windows by itself. (KB-WCEW, V3 live, checked 2026-11-22)
- A Windows Server 2019 ECS created from an imported system image with two imported data disk images boots with the data disks Offline and read-only under the SAN policy OfflineShared; after Set-Disk -IsOffline false and -IsReadOnly false they return with their old drive letters, labels and unchanged file hashes. (KB-WFUC, V3 live, checked 2026-11-04)
- Deleting an IMS image leaves ECS instances that were created from that image untouched and running. (KB-WK5F, V3 live, checked 2026-07-09)
- On 2026-10-06 an ECS from the eu-de public image Standard_Ubuntu_24.04_amd64_bios_latest (image build OTC_Ubuntu_24 of 2026-09-14) ran apt against archive.ubuntu.com and security.ubuntu.com, with only the OTC Tools repository on debmirror.eu-de.otc-service.com, according to its boot log ('Using classic all-in-one repo config'). That build therefore needs internet access, through NAT or an EIP, for package installs. KB-IT7E saw the debmirror as the package source on 2026-10-01. (KB-WP5X, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-10-06)
- The public image Standard_Debian_12_amd64_bios_latest on T Cloud Public (TCP) eu-de logs in with the default SSH user linux rather than debian. (KB-WS3R, V3 live, checked 2026-08-26)
- On a Windows host, editing an offline hive loaded with reg.exe load through the PowerShell registry provider (Set-ItemProperty HKLM:\...) keeps a handle open: reg.exe unload fails with Access is denied and the change is lost when the VHDX is dismounted; reg.exe add followed by reg.exe unload writes it. (KB-YDPO, V3 live, checked 2026-11-16)
- Registering an external image file as a private image takes an optional boot mode parameter with the values BIOS and UEFI. (KB-YK4H, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-21)
- On TCP, ECS instances from private images of client Windows (Windows 10, Windows 7) may be created only on a Dedicated Host (DeH). (KB-ZACD, V1 docs, checked 2026-06-10)
- Sharing a private image between two tenants takes two calls in order: the owner POSTs the recipient project to /v1/cloudimages/members, then the recipient PUTs the same image with status accepted. The recipient then sees __imagetype=shared and can create servers from it, while only the owner may modify the image. (KB-ZC5V, V3 live, checked 2026-07-21)
- Windows Server 2012 R2 with every update Windows Update offered (kernel 6.3.9600.21620, rollup of 2023-09) still does not boot on s7n.large.4 (bugcheck 0xC000021A with 0xC0000428, as without updates) but boots on c4.large.4; the crash dump pages land at the start of the system volume (over boot area and MFT), so after a second crash the NTFS volume no longer mounts. (KB-ZGOO, V3 live, checked 2026-11-13)
- IMS registers an image file already stored in OBS with POST /v2/cloudimages/action, taking name, image_url in bucket:object form, min_disk, os_type and is_config. The call returns a job_id polled at /v1/{project_id}/jobs/{job_id}, and entities.image_id appears about 15 seconds in, well before the job reaches SUCCESS. (KB-ZHKA, V3 live, checked 2026-06-12)
- Image import takes files up to 128 GB in the supported formats. Between 128 GB and 1 TB only RAW with a bitmap file or ZVHD2 qualify, and 1 TB is the documented ceiling for image import. (KB-ZL7H, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-02)
- Microsoft: every domain member has a computer account with a password and by default submits a password change every 30 days (policy Domain member: Maximum machine account password age). netdom reset MACHINE /domain:DOMAIN resets the secure channel between a computer and a domain controller; netdom resetpwd resets the machine password of a domain controller only and must run on that DC. (KB-ZMT3, V1 docs, checked 2026-11-16)
- Only unencrypted or SSE-KMS-encrypted external image files can be uploaded to OBS for an IMS import. (KB-ZOGU, V1 docs, checked 2026-07-30)
- A dynamic Windows Server 2019 UEFI system VHDX with a virtual size of 200 GB and a file of 10.4 GB registered directly with POST /v2/cloudimages/action (min_disk 200, is_config true, no conversion) became an active image in 563 s in eu-de, disk_format zvhd2, __lazyloading true, and an s7n.large.4 ECS with a 200 GB GPSSD root volume from it reached ACTIVE. The documented 128 GB limit of the normal import did not refuse it. (KB-ZQ22, V3 live, checked 2026-11-06)

## obs (168)

- OBS SDK guides are published for python, java, go, nodejs, dotnet and c. Community OBS SDK sources for python and java are published under the opentelekomcloud-community organisation. (KB-24VK, V1 docs, checked 2026-06-19)
- The OBS user guide limits a user-defined domain name bound to a bucket to HTTP requests instead of HTTPS, and the domain name points by a CNAME record to the bucket domain name, so a DNS lookup of the own domain returns the OBS bucket host. (KB-252Q, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- In an OBS bucket policy the principal is written as domain/{domain_id}:user/{user_id} with a colon between the two parts; a slash returns HTTP 400 MalformedPolicy. Actions use plain S3 names such as ListBucket, GetObject and PutObject without an obs: prefix, and Resource is the bucket name or bucket/prefix/*. (KB-2A76, V3 live, checked 2026-06-17)
- DLI does not create Iceberg tables: on 2026-09-25 the DLI SQL check refused CREATE TABLE ... USING ICEBERG with DLI.0007 and STORED BY with a Hive storage handler with DLI.0005. SQL over Iceberg on TCP goes through MRS HetuEngine or Impala instead. (KB-2MWY, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- obsutil 5.7.9 stores the access key and secret key in its configuration file in encrypted form, 44 and 64 characters instead of 20 and 40, so a prefix comparison against the plain key reports a mismatch even for the correct key. (KB-2QVO, V1 docs, checked 2026-08-25)
- A bucket policy that denies every action on the bucket and its objects to NotPrincipal one IAM user, plus an Allow for that user, leaves that user able to write, read and delete objects with an access key, while anonymous list, read and write requests get 403 AccessDenied. (KB-2SQ3, V3 live, checked 2026-09-23)
- When OBS receives a request to configure bucket event notification, it checks that the SMN topic exists and is authorized to OBS and then sends a test notification to the topic subscribers. The OBS troubleshooting page gives the console message "OBS is not authorized to use this topic" for a topic without that authorization and the fix: select OBS under "Services that can publish messages to this topic" in the SMN topic policy. (KB-2T4U, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- A bucket's default storage class applies to new uploads that omit a storage class. Objects already stored keep the class they were written with. (KB-34WC, V1 docs, checked 2026-02-04)
- The SigV4 signing region is not enforced for OBS listing: a scope signed for us-east-1 returned HTTP 200 on ListBuckets and on the path-style bucket listing in eu-de. Write requests were not tested. (KB-37YE, V3 live, checked 2026-09-15)
- OBS Warm has a minimum storage period of 30 days and OBS Cold of 90 days. These periods and the early-deletion mechanics stand in the charging-method section of the service description, not in the price table. (KB-3CKT, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-08-27)
- A signed GET on the OBS service endpoint root, addressing no bucket, returns an XML ListAllMyBucketsResult document listing the buckets of the account in eu-de. (KB-3DTX, V3 live, checked 2026-07-09)
- In SigV4 against OBS a value-less subresource is canonicalised with a trailing equals sign, as location=. Signing plain location returns 403 SignatureDoesNotMatch, which reads like a key or permission problem. (KB-3FLB, V3 live, checked 2026-09-15)
- Batch object deletion uses POST /?delete with an XML Delete body of at most 1000 keys and requires a Content-MD5 header, otherwise OBS rejects the request. (KB-3GDY, V3 live, checked 2026-07-09)
- The SFS FAQ of T Cloud Public (TCP) compares SFS, OBS and EVS: OBS is accessed by bucket address over HTTP and HTTPS and does not support online editing, an EVS disk does not support data sharing or remote access and is used only attached to a server, and SFS file systems are accessed only after an NFS mount. (KB-3MZK, V1 docs, checked 2026-11-21)
- obsutil config with the -config option only updates a configuration file that already exists. Pointed at a path with no file at it, the command returns "no such file or directory", so an existing config has to be copied to that path first. (KB-3V6A, V3 live, checked 2026-08-25)
- Deleting an OBS bucket returns HTTP 409 BucketNotEmpty while an incomplete multipart upload still exists in it, even when every object key has been removed. Aborting the pending uploads clears the condition. (KB-3WA4, V3 live, checked 2026-07-09)
- PUT /{bucket}/?customdomain={domain} returns HTTP 200 and is mandatory for a custom domain on OBS. Before the binding, requests carrying the custom Host header got 404 although the bucket name equalled that host, so the service does no implicit host matching. (KB-3ZOF, V3 live, checked 2026-07-17)
- An OBS lifecycle transition is billed at the request rate of the destination storage class, not the source class. At petabyte scale with objects around 512 KiB the one-off request charge of a transition is a headline number, not a rounding error. (KB-4E6N, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-02)
- PUT /{bucket}/?website with an IndexDocument XML body returns HTTP 200, and PUT /{bucket}/?policy with a public-read GetObject policy returns HTTP 204 on OBS in eu-de. (KB-4G6T, V3 live, checked 2026-07-17)
- Temporary OBS access keys carry a security token and are valid from 15 minutes up to 24 hours. The SDK documentation names them as the client-side alternative to permanent keys. (KB-4IUG, V1 docs, checked 2026-06-19)
- Writing to OBS with default boto3 1.43.57 stores the aws-chunked trailer framing as the object body: put_object of a 9-byte body returns success with an ETag, and head_object then reports 50 bytes. botocore 1.36 and later switch flexible checksums on by default with request_checksum_calculation = when_supported. (KB-4LB5, V3 live, checked 2026-07-28)
- On TCP an OBS object-arrival trigger for a function is created with POST /v2/{project_id}/fgs/triggers/{function_urn}, trigger_type_code OBS, which returned 201 with trigger_status ACTIVE. Event names in event_data need the s3: prefix, for example s3:ObjectCreated:*. (KB-4M2N, V3 live, checked 2026-07-29)
- Setting the Config/RMS tracker configuration with an OBS bucket as channel returned HTTP 403 with error code RMS.00010006. The same call with an SMN topic as channel succeeded on the first attempt. (KB-53ET, V3 live, checked 2026-07-17)
- In a 27-operation S3 compatibility matrix against OBS, 25 operations passed, among them ranged GET, prefix and delimiter listing, MaxKeys with ContinuationToken, StartAfter, copy, conditional GET, batch delete, object tagging, versioning, lifecycle, presigned URLs and bucket default encryption. PASS records that the API accepted the call, since the run used the corrupting checksum default. (KB-5CA3, V3 live, checked 2026-07-28)
- obsutil copies between two OBS buckets that belong to different tenants by holding one credential set as the default profile and the other as the CRR profile. The data streams through the client host and uses its CPU, memory and bandwidth, unlike a server-side copy inside one tenant. (KB-5MXI, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- obsutil ls reports a File number capped at 1000 unless the -limit parameter is raised. Object counts of a source and a destination prefix compared at the default therefore differ even when both sides hold the same objects. (KB-5ZGT, V3 live, checked 2026-06-17)
- An OBS parallel file system takes SSE-KMS only as a bucket default combining BucketKeyEnabled true with an explicit KMSMasterKeyID, all together. Objects then report ServerSideEncryption aws:kms with no per-request header. (KB-64ON, V3 live, checked 2026-07-29)
- TCP exposes no array-level immutable snapshot storage to tenants. Tenant-facing WORM comes from OBS Object Lock, which covers objects only, and from CBR backup locking, which keeps automatic backups in server, disk and SFS Turbo vaults undeletable during their retention period while manual backups stay deletable (checked 2026-09-25). (KB-66D6, V0 recheck due, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- A presigned OBS URL is bound to its object key, its HTTP method and its expiry. Reuse against another key, use after the Expires value, and a DELETE sent to a URL presigned for PUT each return HTTP 403. (KB-6AP2, V3 live, checked 2026-06-26)
- obsutil stores the multipart uploadId in a checkpoint file under its local `.obsutil_checkpoint/upload` directory and reuses that uploadId when the same copy is retried. (KB-6IAO, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-05-01)
- An OBS presigned URL in the OBS signature form carries AccessKeyId, Expires (a Unix timestamp) and a Signature over the HTTP method, Content-MD5, Content-Type, Expires, the x-obs-* headers and the bucket and object key with sub-resources such as response-content-type, so it is bound to one method and one object key while the scheme stays outside the signed string. It is signed with an AK/SK pair or with a temporary AK/SK pair plus x-obs-security-token. (KB-6K64, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- In the service description §6.4.1 OBS Standard storage is charged per GiB per month in volume tiers with the first 5 GiB free, and outbound traffic is a separate item with its own tiers. (KB-6LWE, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- An ECS agency (trust op_svc_ecs) with OBS Administrator and OBS OperateAccess granted on the domain only gave temporary keys from the ECS metadata securitykey endpoint that OBS refused (obsutil 5.7.9 and s3cmd 2.4.0, AccessDenied); with OBS Administrator also granted on the eu-de project and a bucket policy allowing the agency as principal, obsutil cp and sync and s3cmd put with an access token succeeded. (KB-6UKS, V3 live, checked 2026-11-20)
- Addressing an OBS bucket whose name contains dots in virtual-host style fails TLS SNI validation in eu-de. (KB-737E, V3 live, checked 2026-09-16)
- SSE-OBS is refused on an OBS parallel file system, both as a bucket default and as a per-object header. (KB-7BI4, V3 live, checked 2026-07-29)
- Iceberg rewrite_data_files compacts only within a partition, so it leaves a table with one file per partition as it is. Measured on OBS with 1,152,000 rows: a 6-level spec wrote 19,200 files of 2.7 KB in 140 s and compaction rewrote 0 files; a facility, hours(ts) spec wrote 96 files in 3 s. (KB-7KLV, V3 live, checked 2026-07-28)
- An OBS object is stored in the Warm or Cold class when the PUT carries the header x-obs-storage-class with WARM or COLD; STANDARD, WARM and COLD objects were written into one bucket in eu-de. (KB-7NIU, V3 live, checked 2026-11-20)
- OBS in eu-de does not accept the SDK-HMAC-SHA256 signature of the API gateway: a request signed that way gets HTTP 400 InvalidArgument 'Authorization header is invalid -- one and only one ' ' (space) required'. OBS needs its own signature. (KB-A3WA, V3 live, checked 2026-10-02)
- OBS is a global service: its permissions are assigned under Global services and a system policy granted there covers every bucket of the account. To limit a user group to specific buckets, grant it a custom policy whose resources name those buckets or put the buckets in an enterprise project and grant OBS on that project only (IAM lists OBS as supporting enterprise project authorization). In both cases leave no system policy that covers all buckets on the group, such as Tenant Administrator, Tenant Guest or OBS Operator. (KB-AGQK, V0 recheck due, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- Each PUT of an OBS bucket policy replaces the whole policy, so every statement has to be sent again. Several principals mean several statements, and one principal can hold read on the whole bucket plus write on a single prefix. (KB-AOKD, V3 live, checked 2026-06-17)
- For an MRS cluster with its Hive warehouse on OBS, set mrs_ecs_default_agency to MRS_ECS_DEFAULT_AGENCY in the create-cluster body and use an OBS parallel file system with the obs:// scheme. Nodes then get temporary credentials from the metadata service, so Spark and the Hive Metastore need no AK/SK in any config file. (KB-AU3G, V3 live, checked 2026-07-28)
- An OBS bucket policy that names a federated user by its id, or the whole account as domain/{domain_id}, did not give that identity access to the bucket when its IAM OBS roles were granted on the domain only: HEAD, list, get and put stayed 403 AccessDenied 60 seconds after the policy. (KB-BCQT, V3 live, checked 2026-10-28)
- The OBS user guide in the docs mirror of 2026-09-25 marks SMN-enabled event notifications as about to be taken offline: existing rules can still be viewed or deleted, but they cannot be modified and no new rules can be added. (KB-BICR, V0 recheck due, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- Impala 4.3.0 in MRS 3.5.0-LTS reads Iceberg tables with partition pruning, DESCRIBE HISTORY and time travel, and creates and writes its own Iceberg tables. INSERT into a Spark-written Iceberg table with a timestamptz column fails with AnalysisException (Spark stores TIMESTAMP as timestamptz), so Impala only reads those tables. (KB-BJMP, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-07-28)
- The Config resource recorder is configured with PUT /v1/resource-manager/domains/{domain_id}/tracker-config. An SMN channel was accepted with HTTP 200, while an OBS channel returned 403 RMS.00010006 about the agency missing permission on the bucket, even with OBS OperateAccess and Administrator granted and a bucket policy in place. (KB-BKP3, V3 live, checked 2026-07-17)
- FunctionGraph triggers are created with POST /v2/{project_id}/fgs/triggers/{urn} and listed with GET on the same path. A TIMER trigger takes event_data with schedule_type "Rate" and a schedule such as 5m. An OBS trigger takes a bucket, event types such as s3:ObjectCreated:Put, plus prefix and suffix. (KB-BMHI, V3 live, checked 2026-07-30)
- For an OBS static website on a custom domain the bucket name must equal the domain exactly. Such a bucket is managed through path-style URLs of the form https://obs.eu-de.otc.t-systems.com/{bucket}/..., because the dots in the name break the wildcard TLS certificate used for virtual-host style addressing. (KB-BPOF, V3 live, checked 2026-07-17)
- Three concurrent writers with four append rounds each on one Iceberg table on OBS kept all 1,200 rows and 12 snapshots with both the Hive Metastore catalog and the Hadoop catalog. Three simultaneous row-level UPDATEs on one partition all succeeded. (KB-BRPI, V3 live, checked 2026-07-28)
- With SSE-KMS the ETag OBS returns for an object differs from the plaintext MD5 and changes between attempts, so a client verifying by MD5 reports a hash mismatch although the stored data is correct. Size comparison still matches. (KB-BTOI, V3 live, checked 2026-06-15)
- A multipart upload to OBS under default boto3 fails with XAmzContentSHA256Mismatch, while the single-object path returns success and stores the framed body. (KB-BUEP, V3 live, checked 2026-07-28)
- The contractual service description rev. 09.10.2026 returns zero hits for WORM and zero for object lock, so both CBR vault locking and OBS WORM are documented features outside the service catalog. (KB-BXH5, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- An OBS bucket used as an S3 remote-state backend is configured with the backend skip_* flags, skip_s3_checksum among them. (KB-CCSA, V3 live, checked 2026-09-17)
- In an OBS bucket policy the principal reads domain/{domain_id}:user/{user_id}, with a colon between the domain part and the user part. A slash in that position returns 400 MalformedPolicy with "invalid principal". (KB-CJ4H, V3 live, checked 2026-06-17)
- A presigned OBS URL is issued on the bucket host, which in eu-de is the bucket name in front of obs.eu-de.otc.t-systems.com, with AccessKeyId, Expires and Signature as query parameters, so a user who gets the link sees the platform domain. (KB-CQHL, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- The S3-compatible OBS interface does not process If-None-Match on PUT. A presigned PUT sent with If-None-Match: * overwrote the existing object and answered HTTP 200 instead of 412, so a conditional header gives no create-only upload on that interface. (KB-D5FT, V3 live, checked 2026-06-26)
- In a recursive `obsutil cp` upload to OBS, a `.cache` directory and its files were uploaded while an `-exclude` pattern with an absolute path ending in `/.cache/*` was set. (KB-DDGQ, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-05-01)
- An OBS bucket policy is written with a PUT on the bucket and the ?policy sub-resource, signed V2. The string to sign is "PUT\n\n\n{rfc1123 gmt date}\n/{bucket}/?policy" and the header form is Authorization: OBS {access_key}:{base64 hmac_sha1 signature}. (KB-DK67, V3 live, checked 2026-06-17)
- OBS answers standard S3 clients at https://obs.eu-de.otc.t-systems.com with region eu-de, signature version 4 and virtual-hosted addressing. Listing, download and upload were verified this way with boto3. (KB-DR24, V3 live, checked 2026-06-19)
- Default server-side encryption on an OBS bucket is set with a PUT on the bucket and the ?encryption sub-resource, carrying a ServerSideEncryptionConfiguration whose SSEAlgorithm is kms. Objects written afterwards answer HEAD with the header x-obs-server-side-encryption: kms. (KB-DVX5, V3 live, checked 2026-06-15)
- After `obsutil abort obs://<bucket> -r -f`, a retry that still finds the old checkpoint file fails with 404 NoSuchUpload, because the uploadId was invalidated server-side. Deleting the checkpoint file makes the retry start a fresh multipart upload with a new uploadId. (KB-E3O4, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-05-01)
- Against OBS a boto3 client with signature_version s3v4 handles objects and the bucket policy alike: PUT, GET and DELETE of a bucket policy worked on 2026-09-25, and path-style addressing worked as well as virtual. The S3 API expects the policy in S3 syntax (Principal AWS arn:aws:iam::<domain>:root, Action s3:GetObject, Resource arn:aws:s3:::<bucket>/<key>); a policy in the native OBS syntax is answered with HTTP 400 MalformedPolicy. (KB-E73J, V3 live, checked 2026-09-25)
- OBS expects its own V2 signing scheme. A request to the OBS endpoint signed with the V4 algorithm returns 400 even when the access key is valid, which reads like a broken key. (KB-EA2F, V3 live, checked 2026-08-24)
- HetuEngine 2.1.0 in MRS 3.5.0-LTS ships an Iceberg connector (Iceberg 1.4.1) plus Delta Lake and Hudi connectors. Against Iceberg tables on OBS it ran SELECT, JOIN, GROUP BY, partition predicates, the $snapshots, $partitions and $files metadata tables and INSERT. (KB-ECWX, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-07-28)
- OBS in eu-de refuses a bucket tagging call without a Content-MD5 header (400 InvalidRequest) and accepts it with the base64 MD5 of the XML body (204). (KB-EGSV, V3 live, checked 2026-11-10)
- With the S3 backend lockfile option enabled against OBS, a concurrent-apply race was reproduced: two applies ran against the same state object and neither was blocked. (KB-EJUP, V3 live, checked 2026-07-17)
- On a normal OBS bucket Object Lock is enforced: with versioning enabled and put_object_lock_configuration set to COMPLIANCE, a versioned delete_object returns AccessDenied: The object you specified is immutable. (KB-ETIT, V3 live, checked 2026-07-29)
- OBS works as an Iceberg warehouse for Spark through the hadoop-aws S3A connector when path-style access is enabled and the endpoint is set to the regional OBS endpoint obs.eu-de.otc.t-systems.com with an AK/SK credentials provider. (KB-EYIH, V3 live, checked 2026-07-28)
- An OBS bucket delete can return 409 BucketNotEmpty while ListObjects shows zero objects, because incomplete multipart uploads still occupy the bucket. The pending uploads are listed by GET /?uploads and aborted with DELETE /{key}?uploadId=..., after which the bucket delete succeeds. (KB-F53U, V3 live, checked 2026-07-09)
- A bucket-level default SSE-KMS policy on OBS encrypts every object uploaded after the policy is set, so objects written afterwards are already encrypted at rest. (KB-F7NF, V0 recheck due, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- In the OBS docs the native API sets the default bucket storage class with ?storageClass and a StorageClass body (STANDARD, WARM, COLD), while the S3-compatible API uses ?storagePolicy with StoragePolicy and DefaultStorageClass (STANDARD, STANDARD_IA, GLACIER). Both V2 signature specs list storagePolicy among the sub-resources that go into the string to sign. (KB-FB3W, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- An OBS bucket policy is set with a PUT to the bucket root carrying the ?policy sub-resource, which answers HTTP 204 on success. (KB-FLQO, V3 live, checked 2026-06-17)
- The DLI docs disagree on the partition limit of OBS tables: the SQL reference pages on creating OBS tables give 200,000 partitions per table for both DataSource and Hive syntax, while the developer guide still gives 7,000 for DataSource and 100,000 for Hive syntax. Two other SQL reference pages give 100,000 (INSERT) and 7,000 (OVER clause) partitions per table. (KB-FQLZ, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- An object stored with a scripted call put is private: an anonymous HTTPS GET of its public URL answers AccessDenied (seen on winlab1 for six text files of 2026-11-22). A PUT on the object with the query acl and the header x-obs-acl public-read gives Everyone READ, and the same anonymous GET then returns the file with an equal SHA-256. Set the grant and test the anonymous download before a guide links to the object. (KB-G6FI, V3 live, checked 2026-11-22)
- OBS Object Lock (WORM) gives true write-once immutability on TCP, but it covers objects only; block volumes, file shares and snapshots are outside its scope. (KB-GFBA, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- An OBS-triggered FunctionGraph handler receives an event with a Records array, where the object key sits under Records[].obs.object.key. (KB-GJHC, V3 live, checked 2026-07-30)
- An IAM custom fine-grained policy granting obs:bucket:ListBucket and obs:object:GetObject on one bucket, assigned through a group at domain level, still gave HTTP 403 for OBS access with that user's access key more than 20 minutes after assignment. An OBS bucket policy granting the same access took effect within seconds. (KB-GPNI, V3 live, checked 2026-06-17)
- S3 Select against OBS returns MethodNotAllowed. awswrangler is one client that issues S3 Select calls. (KB-GRFT, V3 live, checked 2026-07-28)
- The custom domain is pointed at the website endpoint with an unproxied CNAME to {bucket}.obs-website.eu-de.otc.t-systems.com. The site answered over plain HTTP, OBS served no certificate for the custom domain. (KB-GRRS, V3 live, checked 2026-07-17)
- An OBS bucket policy in eu-de with Principal ID domain/{domain_id}:user/{user_id} and the actions ListBucket, GetBucketLocation, ListBucketMultipartUploads and HeadBucket on the bucket plus GetObject, PutObject, DeleteObject, ListMultipartUploadParts, AbortMultipartUpload and GetObjectVersion on bucket/* was accepted (204, sent with Content-MD5) and read back unchanged. (KB-GTZD, V3 live, checked 2026-11-10)
