Posting an existing name returns HTTP 500 with code 600001 and the text The datasource has exist, and DELETE /datasource/{name} answers 404, so a changed configuration is registered under a new name. (KB-GSXI, V3 live, checked 2026-07-30)
- No MySQL JDBC driver ships with HetuEngine, and HSConsole exposes no endpoint for uploading one. The PostgreSQL driver is bundled. A MySQL JDBC data source registers, and the catalog then stays unloaded with MySqlDriverProvider Failed to add driver jar. null in the coordinator log. (KB-HEOG, V3 live, checked 2026-07-30)
- The Ranger service definition for obs on MRS returned resources ['path'] and accessTypes ['read','write'], with rowFilterDef and dataMaskDef both empty, so the definition carries no masking and no row filtering over storage paths. (KB-HO7G, V3 live, checked 2026-07-30)
- GET /v2/{project_id}/clusters/{cluster_id}/job-executions with a limit parameter lists jobs and returns total_record together with job_list. Single job detail includes queue, launcher_id, app_id and tracking_url. (KB-HSOB, V3 live, checked 2026-07-30)
- On a secured MRS cluster Ranger ships hive and trino service definitions, each with eight mask types and row filtering, and the Hive plugin is wired and enforcing. (KB-ICR4, V3 live, checked 2026-07-30)
- A HetuEngine data source created through the API stores the baseProperties field connection-password encrypted, and the coordinator then fails authentication with FATAL: Invalid username/password,login denied. The same credentials placed inside connection-url authenticate, and land in clear text in the catalog properties file in HDFS. (KB-IL2F, V3 live, checked 2026-07-30)
- The Trino Hive connector shipped inside HetuEngine 2.1.0 on MRS carries the partition projection classes and the full property set, with the properties spelled partition_projection_enabled and partition_projection_type. (KB-ITCD, V3 live, checked 2026-07-30)
- On MRS cluster creation a node disk below 480 GB is rejected with MRS.00005022. The check fires on the root_volume field of the create call rather than on the data volume. (KB-IVZR, V3 live, checked 2026-07-28)
- A HetuEngine compute instance (coordinator and workers as YARN service applications) is created headless with hetu-cli --catalog hive --tenant default, after installing the client from the HetuEngine component directory and copying hetuserver-client.properties from the HSBroker etc directory. No web UI step is involved. (KB-IZY2, V3 live, checked 2026-07-28)
- On MRS 3.5.0-LTS the views returned for Yarn, Hive and HDFS are view1 on Yarn (resource root, type parentQueue, auths Submit and Admin), hive_data_operation_priv on Hive (database default, auths select/insert/create/delete/grant_of_*) and view1 on HDFS (hdfs://hacluster/, type Folders, auths read/write/execute). (KB-JB6H, V3 live, checked 2026-07-30)
- The HetuEngine dynamic catalog scanner picks up a newly registered data source in about 60 seconds, with no service restart. (KB-JP3P, V3 live, checked 2026-07-30)
- hetu-cli discovers the coordinator through HSBroker on port 29861. When HSBroker answers 500 the CLI resolves --server to an empty value and every command ends with Missing required parameter for option '--server', which reads like a usage error; the coordinator address can be read from ZooKeeper instead. (KB-JPCL, V3 live, checked 2026-07-30)
- The MRS cluster manager REST API answers on the cluster float IP, on port 28443 and on 9022. Requests to a node's own address, to the loopback interface or to the FQDN return status 000, which reads as the API being switched off. The float IP is in the OMS configuration file as float_ip. (KB-K35K, V3 live, checked 2026-07-30)
- On MRS 3.5.0-LTS Hive takes Athena-style external table DDL unchanged apart from the LOCATION scheme, which is obs:// instead of s3://. Creating a partitioned Parquet external table, adding a partition, running MSCK REPAIR TABLE and listing partitions all succeeded through beeline within seconds. (KB-K5ID, V3 live, checked 2026-07-30)
- The Trino connector in MRS implements partition projection, configured with Trino's own property names. (KB-K6IQ, V3 live, checked 2026-07-30)
- In the HetuEngine client package trino.tar.gz the plugin/* entries are symlinks into trino/shareLib/. Extracting only the plugin path produces dangling links, which makes connector classes look absent; the classes sit under the shareLib path. (KB-KKXD, V3 live, checked 2026-07-30)
- When creating a user through the MRS cluster manager API the field userType must be HM. (KB-KM3X, V3 live, checked 2026-07-30)
- POST /web/api/v2/permission/users creates a Manager user on MRS and returns HTTP 204. Repeating the call for a user that already exists returns HTTP 500 with error 12-5000048. (KB-KPR2, V3 live, checked 2026-07-28)
- MRS rejects job submission when the IAM user name contains spaces, with the message "This operation cannot be performed because your username contains spaces." The message is written only to the job log in the service-owned OBS bucket. (KB-KXYS, V3 live, checked 2026-07-30)
- MRS 3.3.0-LTS.1 and MRS 3.5.0-LTS ship Spark 3.3.1, the Spark version DLI also runs, so a jar built against Spark 3.3.1 needs no Spark version change to run as an MRS spark-submit job. MRS 3.6.0-LTS moves to Spark 3.5.6. (KB-L4MM, V0 recheck due, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- MRS wrote to an SSE-KMS encrypted parallel file system over the obs:// scheme with the default MRS ECS agency end to end: HDFS put and cat, a partitioned Iceberg table, insert, row-level UPDATE and kept snapshot history, with all 14 data objects carrying the customer KMS key. (KB-L6IL, V3 live, checked 2026-07-30)
- The HetuEngine CLI creates its compute instance itself on the first connection. (KB-LRZR, V3 live, checked 2026-07-28)
- Iceberg on TCP runs on three catalogs: the MRS Hive Metastore, which HetuEngine reads, a JDBC catalog on RDS for PostgreSQL where no MRS cluster exists, and a HadoopCatalog on OBS, which upstream treats as single-writer because S3-compatible storage gives no atomic compare-and-set. (KB-M63N, V3 live, checked 2026-07-28)
- GET /v1/hsconsole/clusters lists the HetuEngine compute instances with id, state, coordinator webui URL and worker counts. POST /v1/hsconsole/clusters/{id}/start (also stop, restart, delete) manages a single instance. (KB-MON7, V3 live, checked 2026-07-28)
- MRS SQL runs asynchronously over POST /v2/{project_id}/clusters/{cluster_id}/job-executions, which returns 202 with job_submit_result.job_id. GET on the same path with the job id returns job_detail carrying job_state, job_result, job_progress and the submitted, started and finished times. (KB-MXUR, V3 live, checked 2026-07-30)
- On an MRS cluster, obs:// access to a parallel file system bucket works through a credentials provider already configured in core-site.xml, so no AK/SK has to be placed on the nodes. (KB-MZ77, V3 live, checked 2026-07-28)
- The sftp subsystem is disabled on MRS cluster nodes, so scp fails with "subsystem request failed on channel 0". Piping over ssh with a redirect into a file on the node works. (KB-NC6T, V3 live, checked 2026-07-28)
- Iceberg rewrite_data_files compacts within a partition, so over-partitioning is not repairable afterwards. A 6-level spec over 1,152,000 rows produced 19,200 partitions holding one 2.7 KB file each, 51.3 MB total, 139.9 s to write, and rewrite_data_files rewrote 0 files. (KB-OFL4, V3 live, checked 2026-07-28)
- HetuEngine 2.1.0 is based on Trino 399, which was released on 6 October 2022. (KB-OI6W, V1 docs, checked 2026-07-30)
- MRS cluster creation creates its own security group for the cluster nodes. An operator host reaches the cluster after an ingress rule for that host is added to this group. (KB-OIOO, V3 live, checked 2026-07-28)
- Impala 4.3.0 on MRS carries iceberg-api 1.2.1 and iceberg-hive-runtime 1.2.1 and handles read, partition pruning, DESCRIBE HISTORY, time travel with FOR SYSTEM_VERSION AS OF, CREATE TABLE STORED BY ICEBERG and INSERT. (KB-OOAR, V3 live, checked 2026-07-28)
- MRS Spark defaults spark.sql.storeAssignmentPolicy to LEGACY, which the DataSource V2 write path refuses; Iceberg writes run with the value set to ANSI. (KB-OUMI, V3 live, checked 2026-07-28)
- An MRS 3.5.0-LTS cluster of 5 nodes takes about 23 minutes from the create call until it is usable. (KB-PTJN, V3 live, checked 2026-07-28)
- core-site.xml on an MRS node already sets fs.obs.security.provider to the MRS credentials provider, and the node metadata service returns live temporary credentials, so an OBS-backed Iceberg warehouse in the Hive Metastore runs with no credentials written into any configuration file. (KB-PWYS, V3 live, checked 2026-07-28)
- MRS Spark on YARN reads Iceberg tables on OBS that were written from a machine outside the cluster, including a schema-evolved column and an evolved partition spec, and writes back with INSERT and MERGE INTO. Snapshot lineage stays intact across engines. (KB-Q4IP, V3 live, checked 2026-07-28)
- The MRS v2 job API has an asynchronous shape: submit a job, poll it by job id, then read the result object. HetuEngine and Impala themselves are reached over synchronous JDBC. (KB-R32Q, V1 docs, checked 2026-07-30)
- The MRS Manager REST API answers on the cluster floating IP address, on port 28443 and identically on port 9022, with basic authentication. Probes against the node hostname, its FQDN or the loopback address returned no HTTP status code. (KB-RDCL, V3 live, checked 2026-07-30)
- On the MRS Manager API, GET /web/api/v2/clusters/1/tenants returned HTTP 405 on the bare collection. The tenant list is read from /web/api/v2/clusters/1/tenants/queue_config/super_scheduler, and /tenants/calc_resource/scheduler_mode returns SUPER or CAPACITY, which is needed to build a tenant creation body. (KB-RMRE, V3 live, checked 2026-07-28)
- On an MRS cluster with HetuEngine the HSConsole REST API listens on port 29880, bound to the business IP of the HSBroker node. It answers plain HTTP and accepted calls without credentials from inside the cluster. (KB-RQV2, V3 live, checked 2026-07-28)
- remove_orphan_files rejects any older_than window shorter than 24 hours, by upstream Iceberg design. A timestamp in the future trips the same check, because the window is measured against the current time. (KB-RUF5, V3 live, checked 2026-07-30)
- For MRS 3.x, including 3.5.0-LTS, the MRS user manual sets a floor of 64 GB for master node memory (most pages say greater than 64 GB, the Doris page at least 64 GB), so 16 GiB and 32 GiB master flavors do not qualify. The MRS 3.5.0-LTS clusters built on eu-de used 64 GiB flavors, m3.2xlarge.8 for master and c4.4xlarge.4 for core nodes. (KB-RY3G, V0 recheck due, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- impalad on an MRS cluster listens on the core nodes, ports 21050 and 21000, not on the master node. (KB-SGOR, V3 live, checked 2026-07-28)
- On MRS, a HetuEngine query user needs the YARN queue permission and membership in the hetuuser group, and HetuEngine is restarted after that grant. (KB-SHRY, V3 live, checked 2026-07-28)
- spark-sql caches Iceberg table metadata for the life of a session. The files and snapshots metadata tables read right after a maintenance call still return the pre-maintenance numbers; a fresh session showed the compaction result, 5 data files before and 2 after. (KB-SYST, V3 live, checked 2026-07-30)
- MRS cluster nodes run sshd with the SFTP subsystem switched off, so scp to a node returns an error and file transfer goes through a pipe over ssh. (KB-TGSO, V3 live, checked 2026-07-28)
- A secured MRS cluster serves Ranger admin over HTTPS on port 6182 of the RangerAdmin node. An insecure cluster serves it over HTTP on port 6080. (KB-U4XJ, V3 live, checked 2026-07-30)
- With Kerberos enabled, Spark on YARN in MRS creates an Iceberg table on OBS, inserts into it, runs a row-level UPDATE and keeps snapshot history. (KB-UALF, V3 live, checked 2026-07-30)
- Probing an MRS master for the Ranger admin API on ports 21400, 21401 and 6182 got no answer, and port 6085 is bound to the loopback interface only. (KB-UE7F, V3 live, checked 2026-07-30)
- The MRS node metadata service answers "401 Please configure Cloud Service Agency first" when the create-cluster body omitted mrs_ecs_default_agency. The wording resembles a Kerberos restriction, but the cause is the missing field. (KB-UKGD, V3 live, checked 2026-07-30)
- An Iceberg data source registered in HetuEngine after the service restart answers with Catalog not found. The order that works writes the catalog first and restarts the service afterwards. (KB-UM5W, V3 live, checked 2026-07-28)
- GET /web/api/v2/clusters on the MRS Manager returns the cluster id (usually 1), the auth mode and the installed packs. GET /web/api/v2/clusters/1/services lists every service with its role instances and health, and the per-service call reports runningStatus values GOOD, STARTING or STOPPED. (KB-UOVS, V3 live, checked 2026-07-28)
- A HetuEngine user must also belong to the hetuuser group, otherwise the call returns error 10000013 asking for that group. Group membership changes take effect only after a HetuEngine restart. (KB-UWS2, V3 live, checked 2026-07-28)
- A comma inside the description field of an MRS Manager role or user creation request is rejected with 12-4000004 Invalid description. (KB-UZL5, V3 live, checked 2026-07-30)
- A response of 000 from MRS Manager on port 28443 means the probe went to the wrong address. Manager binds to the cluster float IP, not to the address returned by hostname -i on a node. (KB-V4OT, V3 live, checked 2026-07-30)
- HetuEngine picks up a newly registered data source only after the service is restarted, and that restart has to follow the catalog registration in HSConsole. (KB-VHYQ, V3 live, checked 2026-07-30)
- An MRS 3.5.0-LTS node group requires a root_volume of at least 480 GB. (KB-VJES, V3 live, checked 2026-07-28)
- The MRS client environment script bigdata_env references unset variables, so a shell script running with set -u aborts silently when it sources that file. Running with set -o pipefail alone avoids the abort. (KB-VTJC, V3 live, checked 2026-07-28)
- On the MRS v2 job-executions API the job_type values HiveSql, HiveScript, SparkSql and SparkSubmit were seen working in eu-de on 2026-07-30. (KB-VZ4R, V3 live, checked 2026-07-30)
- Killing the default-coordinator and default-worker YARN applications on MRS leaves the HetuEngine compute instance in FAULT with zero nodes, and it stays there. While no instance exists HSBroker answers HTTP 500 on every endpoint, so hetu-cli has no discovery path; recovery goes through the HSConsole Start or Restart action, or a Manager service restart. (KB-W56N, V3 live, checked 2026-07-30)
- On an MRS 3.5.0-LTS cluster with Ranger and without Guardian, GET /service returned exactly one service, of type obs and name obs, holding zero policies. (KB-W5DT, V3 live, checked 2026-07-30)
- Without Guardian installed on an MRS cluster, creating any Ranger obs policy fails with Init obs client failed. (KB-WENQ, V3 live, checked 2026-07-30)
- On 2026-11-19 the TCP price API held eu-nl records for MRS and for CSS, and the service description of 2026-10-09 lists both in its EU-NL columns, while the regions-and-endpoints documentation lists an endpoint for MRS and CSS in eu-de only. (KB-WQVX, V3 live, checked 2026-11-19)
- Each MRS node group accepts only a subset of flavors. On MRS 3.5.0-LTS in eu-de master_node_default_group accepted m3.2xlarge.8.linux.mrs and c4.4xlarge.4.linux.mrs and rejected every 16 GiB and 32 GiB flavor tried; core_node_analysis_group accepted only c4.4xlarge.4.linux.mrs out of six. (KB-X5PJ, V3 live, checked 2026-07-29)
- On MRS 3.5.0-LTS Spark with an Iceberg warehouse on OBS the five Iceberg maintenance procedures rewrite_data_files, rewrite_manifests, expire_snapshots, rewrite_position_delete_files and remove_orphan_files all run successfully. (KB-XBDB, V3 live, checked 2026-07-30)
- MRS 3.5.0-LTS ships Spark 3.3.1 with the vendor-built Iceberg runtime iceberg-spark-runtime-3.3_2.12-1.4.1-h0.cbu.mrs.350.r11.jar on the default classpath, in the Spark client jars, the shared Spark directory and the JobGateway job jars, so Iceberg jobs run with no --jars argument. (KB-XIWQ, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-07-28)
- The first kinit of a newly created MRS Manager user forces a password change, so old password and new password are both supplied at that first login. (KB-XKVV, V3 live, checked 2026-07-30)
- On a Kerberos-secured MRS cluster the built-in admin identity carries only the ficommon group and the Manager_administrator_180 role, so Spark jobs fail with ACL check failed on root.default and Hive access fails with Permission denied on /user/hive. (KB-YJJP, V3 live, checked 2026-07-30)
- The HetuEngine coordinator runs in a YARN container as user omm, so connector configuration files need read and traverse rights for omm on every node. Where they lack them, the scanner logs Invalid configuration property and reports the file as missing although it is present. (KB-YWYI, V3 live, checked 2026-07-30)
- The MRS cluster create call accepted cluster_version MRS 3.5.0-LTS and rejected MRS 3.3.0-LTS.1, a value listed in the API reference. (KB-Z3UG, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-07-28)
- The MRS Manager REST API together with hetu-cli covers the full HetuEngine compute instance flow, so the steps can be scripted. (KB-ZIOE, V3 live, checked 2026-07-30)
- On a secured MRS cluster the Ranger role instances are listed with GET /web/api/v2/clusters/1/services/Ranger/instances on Manager port 28443, which returns role instances with numeric ids. A RangerAdmin instance is then reached through the Manager proxy on port 9022 under /component/Ranger/RangerAdmin/{instance_id}/{ranger_path}. (KB-ZODF, V3 live, checked 2026-07-30)
- A user created through POST /permission/users on MRS Manager has to carry userType HM. The values HumanMachine and Machine-Machine are rejected with 12-4000013 Invalid user type. (KB-ZVIB, V3 live, checked 2026-07-30)

## vpc (98)

- A custom VPC route table in eu-de accepts 0.0.0.0/0 and other destinations with next hop type ecs (server id), eni (the primary NIC port id of a server) and vip (the address of an existing virtual IP); type vip with an unknown address answers VPC.2800 "could not be found", type vip with a plain NIC address answers VPC.2801 RouteTypeNexthopNotMatch. A subnet is associated with POST /v1/{project_id}/routetables/{id}/action and {"routetable": {"subnets": {"associate": [subnet_id]}}}; PUT on that path answers 404. (KB-2FEV, V3 live, checked 2026-11-22)
- In eu-de a VPC and a VPC security group can both be renamed in place through the API (update with a new name), both answered 200 and the ids stayed the same, so ECS and rules that refer to them need no change. (KB-3BYZ, V3 live, checked 2026-11-10)
- In eu-de a virtual IP read with GET /v1/{project_id}/ports/{port_id} reports status DOWN even while it is in use: an EIP bound to it reads ACTIVE with that port as port_id. DOWN on a virtual IP port is no sign of an idle or broken resource; judge it by the EIP and the allowed address pairs of the server NIC. (KB-3IMR, V3 live, checked 2026-11-09)
- Once the WAF engine port is gone, the security group and the subnet delete cleanly. A VPC delete can still answer 409 VPC.0104 once while the subnet settles and then succeed on the next attempt. (KB-3TES, V3 live, checked 2026-09-16)
- The VPC api-ref requires a plain IP address for the ip_address of an allowed address pair under the hardware SDN networking plan, and a dedicated security group for a pair whose mask is shorter than /24. (KB-3YKZ, V1 docs, checked 2026-06-12)
- Updating an existing subnet with primary_dns and secondary_dns is rejected with VPC.0201 'secondary_dns fail when primary_dns is empty', although primary_dns is in the body. An update that sets the dnsList array returns 200, and running guests pick it up on DHCP renew. (KB-4AB2, V3 live, checked 2026-07-17)
- TCP security group rules are not allow-only. Each rule has an action of allow or deny and a priority, read through the v3 API, and deny rules exist live in eu-de (KB-OVQP). The FAQ that calls security groups a whitelist describes allow rules only. (KB-4DQW, V1 docs, checked 2026-10-02)
- The VPC quota and its used count for a project are returned by GET /v1/{project_id}/quotas with type vpc. (KB-4DTY, V4 kept live, checked 2026-07-17)
- In the VPC API a subnet's id and its neutron_subnet_id are two different values; the subnet id equals its neutron_network_id. A port references both: its network_id holds the VPC subnet id and its fixed_ips subnet_id holds the neutron_subnet_id (neutron_subnet_id_v6 for an IPv6 address) (eu-de, checked 2026-09-25). (KB-4N3E, V3 live, checked 2026-09-25)
- In eu-de a single VPC is read with GET /v1/{project_id}/vpcs/{vpc_id}. The response wraps the object under the singular key vpc, while the collection call returns the list under vpcs. (KB-4NWB, V3 live, checked 2026-09-25)
- A subnet created over the API takes DNS server addresses and dhcp_enable as explicit fields. The values the API applies when those fields are omitted differ from the ones the console sets for a subnet. (KB-55UQ, V3 live, checked 2026-08-18)
- The description of a security group rule is free text of up to 255 characters without angle brackets and is independent of the rule body. Whether a rule allows or denies is in its action field (allow or deny, default allow), which the v3 API returns and the v1 and v2.0 reads do not, so a rule read back as protocol null and remote_ip_prefix 0.0.0.0/0 matches all IPv4 traffic in its direction but is an allow-everything rule only if the v3 API shows action allow (eu-de, checked 2026-09-25). (KB-5TKG, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- A VPC subnet in eu-de with no servers still carries two system ports in GET /v1/{project_id}/ports: device_owner network:dhcp with an empty device_id, and network:router_interface_distributed whose device_id is the VPC id. A check for leftovers before deleting a subnet can ignore those two owners. (KB-6FJO, V3 live, checked 2026-10-05)
- In eu-de PUT /v1/{project_id}/publicips/{publicip_id} with the body {"publicip": {}} unbinds an elastic IP from the virtual IP port it was bound to: HTTP 200, and an immediate GET reads status DOWN and port_id null. No separate unbind call is needed. (KB-6XFR, V3 live, checked 2026-11-10)
- A VPC subnet accepts masks down to /29: on 2026-09-25 in eu-de a /29 and a /28 subnet were created and turned ACTIVE. A /30 create answered HTTP 200, but the subnet never turned ACTIVE and was gone within a minute. The VPC user guide says /29, the API reference says the mask cannot be greater than 28, and the live result follows the user guide. (KB-7ISN, V3 live, checked 2026-09-25)
- The DNS server addresses of a VPC subnet on T Cloud Public (TCP) can be modified after creation and a subnet takes at most five DNS server addresses. (KB-7LTC, V1 docs, checked 2026-11-12)
- dhcp_enable has to be true on a VPC subnet for cloud-init in the guest to get an address and inject the SSH key. With it false the instance comes up without an address. (KB-7MAW, V3 live, checked 2026-08-18)
- An unfiltered GET /v2.0/ports?limit=500 returned a body with no ports key at all. (KB-7NBH, V3 live, checked 2026-09-16)
- In eu-de an active/standby failover of a virtual IP between two Linux ECS (VIP listed in both NIC pairs and both NIC addresses in the VIP port pairs; the new master adds the address, sends gratuitous ARP every second and enables its NAT rules, the old master removes them) moved HTTP to the VIP EIP, DNAT to a backend and SNAT of a workload subnet routed to the VIP without a lost probe at a 2 second interval, twice in both directions; no API call on the VIP or the EIP was needed for the switch. (KB-7NLT, V3 live, checked 2026-11-22)
- Subnet delete uses the nested path DELETE /v1/{project_id}/vpcs/{vpc_id}/subnets/{subnet_id}, while create posts to the flat path /v1/{project_id}/subnets. (KB-7RKE, V1 docs, checked 2026-08-18)
- The response of POST /v1/{project_id}/security-groups already lists the four default rules with their ids (egress IPv4 and IPv6 to anywhere, ingress IPv4 and IPv6 from the same group); deleting each with DELETE /v1/{project_id}/security-group-rules/{id} leaves a group with no rule at all, and one added ingress rule (for example TCP 3389 from one /32) is then the only rule, so a server in it can open no outbound connection. (KB-7WVA, V3 live, checked 2026-11-13)
- A security group created through the VPC API in eu-de with only a name and a VPC id comes with four rules: ingress from members of the same group for IPv4 and IPv6 and egress to anywhere for IPv4 and IPv6, so no outside address reaches its servers until an ingress rule is added. (KB-A2BE, V3 live, checked 2026-11-10)
- Intra-subnet ICMP ping failed where the security group carried only a rule with no protocol set, while TCP over the same path worked. The VPC user manual lists All, TCP, UDP, GRE and ICMP as Protocol values. The observation and the manual disagree and no re-test has been made. (KB-ANXW, V3 live, checked 2026-09-02)
- A subnet created with POST /v1/{project_id}/subnets without DNS fields has an empty dnsList, so guests get no DNS resolvers: traffic by IP works, name resolution fails. The console fills in default resolvers, the API does not. Pass primary_dns and secondary_dns when creating subnets by API or IaC. (KB-BANM, V3 live, checked 2026-07-17)
- Security group rules on TCP carry an action of allow or deny with a priority from 1 to 100, which only the v3 API returns. Traffic that matches no inbound rule is denied implicitly. Deny rules exist live in eu-de (KB-OVQP). Network ACL and firewall rules have their own allow and deny actions. (KB-BNGZ, V1 docs, checked 2026-10-02)
- A VPC subnet gateway address is a settable field under Advanced Settings at subnet creation, where the .1 address is only the example. It is fixed once the subnet exists. (KB-BY5T, V1 docs, checked 2026-08-26)
- The VPC user guide calls the subnet-level stateful allow and deny ACL a Firewall, while the VPC v3 API names the same object network_acl. (KB-BZZQ, V1 docs, checked 2026-06-08)
- A VPC port has source and destination checking on by default, so an appliance routes or NATs traffic only after the check is disabled on every transit NIC, which is done by adding a wildcard allowed-address-pair entry to the port. (KB-C4X3, V3 live, checked 2026-07-23)
- A VPC create is polled to status OK and a subnet create to status ACTIVE before the next resource in the chain is created. (KB-D2OW, V3 live, checked 2026-08-18)
- POST /v1/{project_id}/publicips with publicip type 5_bgp and port_id of the server's NIC (from GET /v1/{project_id}/cloudservers/{id}/os-interface) creates and binds an Elastic IP in one call (PENDING_CREATE, ACTIVE about 10 s later); tags go on it with POST /v2.0/{project_id}/publicips/{id}/tags and a body {"tag": {"key": ..., "value": ...}}, one call per tag. (KB-DEAH, V3 live, checked 2026-11-13)
- In eu-de the VPC listing is GET /v1/{project_id}/vpcs and returns the networks under the response key vpcs. Each entry carries id, name, cidr and status. (KB-DR57, V4 kept live, checked 2026-09-25)
- In eu-de, when the VIP port lists the NIC addresses of two servers in allowed_address_pairs, traffic to the VIP and to its EIP reaches both servers, not only the one that holds the address: the backup of an active/standby pair must drop VIP traffic itself (a Linux backup with ip_forward and NAT rules on processed copies of it). (KB-DXYJ, V3 live, checked 2026-11-22)
- opentelekomcloud_vpc_subnet_v1 exposes two different ids: .id is the VPC subnet id, while .network_id is the value that NAT gateways, CCE, ELB, RDS and SFS expect. Passing the wrong one fails late and reads like a permission problem. (KB-DYU2, V3 live, checked 2026-09-16)
- A single security group rule is removed with DELETE /v1/{project_id}/security-group-rules/{rule_id} on the VPC v1 API in eu-de, which answers HTTP 204. (KB-EVGE, V3 live, checked 2026-06-08)
- When a VPN route for a CIDR already exists, creating a VPC subnet with that CIDR returns HTTP 200 and the route table then holds a local and an eni route for the same destination, with no error or warning. This contradicts the documented rule of one route per destination. (KB-F44Z, V3 live, checked 2026-08-26)
- The VPC of a resource is read from a different field per service: ECS metadata.vpc_id, CCE spec.hostNetwork.vpc, ELB vpc_id, NAT gateway router_id, RDS vpc_id. An EIP is matched over its load balancer or over the private address inside the VPC CIDR. (KB-F5QT, V3 live, checked 2026-09-17)
- VPC peering judges overlap on the declared VPC CIDR, not on the subnets in use. Two VPCs whose declared ranges overlap cannot be connected by a peering connection, and the documentation answer is to replan the network. (KB-FD3K, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-16)
- PUT /v3/{project_id}/vpc/firewalls/{id}/associate-subnets takes the body {"subnets": [{"virsubnet_id": ID}]} at the top level; a body wrapped in firewall answers 400 SYS.0400 Invalid request body: No keys['subnets']. (KB-FHYK, V3 live, checked 2026-11-20)
- VPC quota can be read before a create with GET /v1/{project_id}/quotas?type=vpc on vpc.eu-de.otc.t-systems.com. The 200 response carries quotas.resources[] entries with type, used, quota and min. (KB-GAQV, V4 kept live, checked 2026-08-18)
- A newly created security group already carries an egress allow-all rule and an ingress rule for members of the same group. (KB-GRIL, V3 live, checked 2026-08-18)
- Creating a VPC subnet whose CIDR duplicates an existing VPN route returns HTTP 200 in eu-de, and the route table then stably holds that destination twice, once as local and once as eni, although the documentation states duplicate destinations are rejected. (KB-GSLG, V3 live, checked 2026-08-26)
- A VPC peering between two VPCs of the same tenant becomes active on creation, without a separate accept step. Routes have to be added on both sides, in Terraform with opentelekomcloud_vpc_route_v2, type "peering" and nexthop set to the peering id. (KB-H7KG, V3 live, checked 2026-09-16)
- The VPC API reference on TCP exposes no sub-network-interface endpoints. The VLAN-based supplementary network interface capability exists on the platform but is consumed by the CCE Turbo CNI only, so there is no customer-usable equivalent of ENI prefix delegation on ECS. (KB-HLIZ, V1 docs, checked 2026-06-12)
- Two VPC route conflicts are rejected: creating a VPN connection whose peer subnet equals an existing VPC subnet returns VPN.0219, and adding a peering route for a destination already routed to a VPN returns 400 DestinationAlreadyExist. (KB-HNE7, V3 live, checked 2026-08-26)
- GET /v2.0/ports reports port_security_enabled true on a port that has an any-source allowed address pair with prefix /0, so port_security_enabled does not tell whether the source and destination check is off; read allowed_address_pairs instead. (KB-HPNY, V3 live, checked 2026-11-05)
- In eu-de, with the source and destination check on (no 1.1.1.1/0 pair) a Linux firewall ECS could not DNAT inbound traffic to a backend in another subnet: the forwarded packets with the external source address stopped within 4 seconds of removing the pair and flowed again within 2 seconds of adding 1.1.1.1/0, twice. In the same state, SNAT for sessions started by a workload subnet routed to the firewall and replies of a network behind the firewall reached by a type ecs route kept working. (KB-I6FW, V3 live, checked 2026-11-22)
- The VPC v1 API lists security groups together with their rules at /v1/{project_id}/security-groups in eu-de, returning security_groups[].security_group_rules[]. Each rule carries id, direction, ethertype as IPv4 or IPv6, protocol where null means any, port_range_min, port_range_max, remote_ip_prefix and remote_group_id. (KB-IJZM, V4 kept live, checked 2026-06-08)
- GET /v1/{project_id}/subnets also returns legacy neutron networks, recognisable by status UNKNOWN, scope center and a missing vpc_id field. A record without vpc_id is not a VPC subnet. (KB-KGJY, V3 live, checked 2026-09-17)
- A security group created with POST /v1/{project_id}/security-groups in eu-de already holds an IPv4 ingress rule from its own group; posting that rule again answers HTTP 409 VPC.0602 SecurityGroupRuleExists with the id of the existing rule, so two ECS in the same new group reach each other on all ports without an extra rule. (KB-KJAE, V3 live, checked 2026-11-22)
- On T Cloud Public (TCP) in eu-de network ACLs are read with the VPC v3 call GET /v3/{project_id}/vpc/firewalls, which returns HTTP 200 with the response keys firewalls and page_info. (KB-KMWL, V4 kept live, checked 2026-07-02)
- The VPC quota of a project is read with GET /v1/{project_id}/quotas against the VPC endpoint in eu-de. The response carries one record per type, for example type vpc with a used and a quota value. (KB-KQ3Z, V4 kept live, checked 2026-08-26)
- A subnet created with POST /v1/{project_id}/subnets and no DNS fields ends up with an empty dnsList, and its instances get no resolver. The console fills in the regional resolver addresses, the API does not. (KB-KS3J, V3 live, checked 2026-07-17)
- A port whose device_owner still points at an already deleted VM blocks deletion of its subnet and VPC, and a tenant cannot remove such a port itself. Clearing it required a support ticket to force-delete the stale port or the VPC. (KB-KZJU, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- A VPC subnet is created with dhcp_enable true by default. (KB-L6FG, V1 docs, checked 2026-08-18)
- A security group rule is added with POST /v1/{project_id}/security-group-rules and removed individually by rule id with DELETE on the same path. A newly added rule takes a few seconds to become effective. (KB-LERK, V3 live, checked 2026-07-29)
- In allowed_address_pairs a pair whose prefix is /0 with any non-zero address is accepted as the any-source wildcard, while a literal 0.0.0.0/0 is rejected by request validation. (KB-LSXG, V3 live, checked 2026-07-23)
- In a bridge VPC, /32 routes to each source database address over cross-tenant VPC peering plus one /24 route to the destination VPCs over intra-tenant peering carry DRS traffic in both directions. Longest prefix match decides, and DRS replication instances receive addresses different from the source database. (KB-LTUU, V3 live, checked 2026-02-04)
- After a persistent connection is cut, a new connection under the new rule only forms once the connection tracking entry has timed out: 600 seconds for established TCP, 30 seconds for TCP in FIN-WAIT and for ICMP, 180 seconds for other protocols with traffic in both directions and 30 seconds with traffic in one direction. (KB-LUT7, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- Adding a VPC peering route for a destination already covered by a VPN route returns 400 DestinationAlreadyExist. (KB-LXWR, V3 live, checked 2026-08-26)
- POST /v1/{project_id}/subnets leaves primary_dns and secondary_dns empty when the fields are omitted, while the console form prefills them. An instance on such a subnet reaches ACTIVE and answers SSH, and name resolution in the guest fails. (KB-MM4Z, V3 live, checked 2026-08-18)
- A security group rule description is free text and does not define what the rule does: a rule with protocol null, no ports and a remote prefix covering all addresses matches all traffic in its direction and allows it only when its action is allow. The action (allow or deny, with a priority from 1 to 100) is returned by the v3 API but not by the v1 and v2.0 reads, so audits must read action, priority, protocol and remote_ip_prefix from the v3 API, never the description (eu-de, checked 2026-09-25). (KB-MOMQ, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- A security group filters inbound traffic to an ECS while egress is open by default, so an outbound transfer from the instance to an external host over SSH runs without any security group change. (KB-MQP4, V3 live, checked 2026-06-16)
- Subnets set up through the console in eu-de carry primary_dns 100.125.4.25 and secondary_dns 100.125.129.199, the platform resolvers. A subnet created through the API or Terraform should set both explicitly. (KB-N2BY, V4 kept live, checked 2026-10-05)
- VPC firewall rule changes never cut existing connections: adding, changing or deleting a firewall rule and associating or disassociating a subnet leave all inbound and outbound persistent connections up. The new rules apply to new connections only. (KB-N6UD, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- Every VPC route table carries system routes for 100.64.0.0/10 and 198.19.128.0/20 and a loopback route for 127.0.0.0/8. System routes cannot be modified or deleted. (KB-NLXW, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- The default VPC quota per tenant is 10, and creating an eleventh VPC returns VPC.0114 OverQuota. Current use is readable with GET /v1/{project_id}/quotas, where the subnet quota is 100. Raising the VPC quota goes through a support request. (KB-NPZK, V3 live, checked 2026-07-23)
- Traffic over an active VPC peering needs a route entry in the route table on each of the two sides, with the peer VPC CIDR, a peer subnet CIDR or a peer ECS IP address as destination. (KB-O6IU, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- Security group rules on TCP are not allow-only. The v3 API and the console carry an action of allow or deny and a priority, and deny rules exist live in eu-de (KB-OVQP). The older FAQ wording on a whitelist mechanism and aggregated rules predates this and does not cover deny rules. Restricting egress can use a deny rule in the security group or another control such as Cloud Firewall, NAT or routes. (KB-OIUA, V1 docs, checked 2026-10-02)
- A VPC peering whose two VPCs belong to different tenants becomes active only after the owner of the accepting VPC accepts the request. (KB-OOK5, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- TCP security groups can hold deny rules. A live read of the v3 security group rules API in eu-de on 2026-10-02 returned two enabled ingress rules with action deny on one test tenant (priority 4 and 5, IPv4, all protocols) next to allow rules. The other test tenant held 205 rules, all allow. The v3 API returns action and priority on every rule. (KB-OVQP, V3 live, checked 2026-10-02)
- The allow-any rule that references a security group's own id appears twice in a listing, once with ethertype IPv4 and once with IPv6. The two entries form one pair covering both address families, not a duplicated rule. (KB-PF6Q, V3 live, checked 2026-06-08)
- Security groups track connections: a changed inbound rule applies to existing traffic at once, while outbound changes only affect new connections. Any added, changed or deleted rule and any instance added to or removed from the group clears the inbound persistent connections of all instances in the group, while outbound persistent connections stay. (KB-PI2D, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- VPC peering connections are listed with GET /v2.0/vpc/peerings, a v2.0 path that carries no project id segment. The response key is peerings and each entry has name, status, request_vpc_info with vpc_id, and accept_vpc_info with vpc_id and tenant_id. (KB-PWJP, V4 kept live, checked 2026-09-25)
- POST /v1/{project_id}/subnets stores primary_dns and secondary_dns as empty when the request omits them. The console prefills both fields, the API does not, so a subnet built by API or Terraform can hand out no resolver at all. The region resolver addresses have to be set explicitly. (KB-Q5W2, V3 live, checked 2026-08-18)
- A VPC v1 subnet is created with or without availability_zone in the body; without it the subnet answers without an availability_zone field and is not bound to a zone. (KB-QFNG, V3 live, checked 2026-11-20)
- GET /v1/{project_id}/security-groups with the query vpc_id=<id> returned an empty list in eu-de although groups used in that VPC exist, because the listed security groups carry no vpc_id value; filter the unfiltered list or by name instead. (KB-QV4M, V3 live, checked 2026-11-10)
- In eu-de two subnets deleted with DELETE /v1/{project_id}/vpcs/{vpc_id}/subnets/{subnet_id} were gone within seconds, their dhcp and router-interface ports included, and the VPC could be deleted right after. A GET on the deleted VPC answers 404 VPC.0105 with the Neutron message RouterNotFound, so a VPC is a Neutron router underneath. (KB-RD6U, V3 live, checked 2026-10-05)
- In eu-de a virtual IP (POST /v1/{project_id}/privateips with device_owner neutron:VIP_PORT) carries traffic to a server only after the VIP port itself lists the server NIC address in allowed_address_pairs (PUT /v2.0/ports/{vip_port_id}); with the VIP only in the allowed_address_pairs of the server NIC, the VIP and an EIP bound to it stayed unreachable for more than 15 hours, and after the VIP port pair was set the VIP answered within about 8 seconds. (KB-SKOF, V3 live, checked 2026-11-22)
- GET /v2.0/ports?network_id={id} returned 0 ports for a subnet that held three ports, one of them attached to a running VM. The same call without the filter, matched client-side on network_id and on fixed_ips subnet_id, returned all three. (KB-SLWK, V3 live, checked 2026-09-16)
- In eu-de GET /v1/{project_id}/subnets returned 14 entries both without a limit and with limit=2000, but one call omitted a subnet that a direct GET /v1/{project_id}/subnets/{subnet_id} returned as ACTIVE, and the later call listed it. An inventory must not mark a subnet missing from one listing alone; confirm it with a direct GET by id. (KB-UHKQ, V3 live, checked 2026-11-09)
- Virtual Private Cloud (VPC) provides isolated virtual networks inside a tenant: each VPC carries its own IP address ranges, subnets, virtual routers, security groups and network ACLs, and the customer can create one or more VPCs. VPC flow logs capture the traffic of the network interfaces of generation 7 and generation 9 servers and forward it to Log Tank Service for storage and analysis. The Regions and Endpoints page lists API endpoints for VPC in eu-de and eu-nl. (KB-UTUP, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- The resource opentelekomcloud_vpc_secgroup_v3 creates the IPv4 and IPv6 allow-all egress rules by itself. Declaring an additional catch-all egress rule on the same security group returns 409 VPC.9902 "Security group rule already exists" and stops the apply. (KB-VBVB, V3 live, checked 2026-09-16)
- GET /v1/{project_id}/quotas returns the per-resource quotas of a project, including the VPC quota. On a test tenant in eu-de the call reported the VPC quota at 10 of 10 used. (KB-VCIT, V3 live, checked 2026-08-18)
- On T Cloud Public (TCP) in eu-de a subnet object returned by the VPC v1 API can carry an optional availability_zone attribute (the AZ specified at creation) and omits the key when none is set (checked 2026-09-25). The value does not limit where the subnet can be used: a subnet is defined by its VPC and its CIDR range and is not bound to a single availability zone. (KB-VLGS, V3 live, checked 2026-09-25)
- Anti-spoofing on a VPC NIC drops packets whose source or destination address differs from the NIC's fixed IP, which affects any routing, firewall or VPN gateway instance. It is opened per NIC with PUT /v2.0/ports/{port_id} on vpc.eu-de.otc.t-systems.com setting allowed_address_pairs, returning HTTP 200. (KB-W7H2, V3 live, checked 2026-07-23)
- In eu-de the bandwidth of an EIP with share_type PER and charge_mode traffic was raised from 10 to 1000 Mbit/s in place with a bandwidth update (size and name in one call); it answered 200, kept charge_mode traffic and the EIP stayed bound. (KB-WGS5, V3 live, checked 2026-11-10)
- In eu-de the quota for type vpc returned by the VPC quota endpoint is 10 VPCs per project. (KB-WGTH, V3 live, checked 2026-08-26)
- Subnets are AZ-agnostic: an ECS placed in eu-de-03 was created on a subnet labelled eu-de-01 without problems. (KB-XGKU, V3 live, checked 2026-07-20)
- A security group rule with action deny and priority 1 is created with POST /v3/{project_id}/vpc/security-group-rules (multiport, remote_ip_prefix, ethertype) and reads back next to allow rules of priority 100. (KB-XKHS, V3 live, checked 2026-11-20)
- A default route to a NAT gateway in a VPC shows in GET /v1/{project_id}/routetables/{id} as type nat with the gateway id and in GET /v1/{project_id}/vpcs as a route with the private IP of the gateway port as nexthop, but GET /v2.0/vpc/routes lists only the peering routes of that VPC, so that list alone does not show the default route. (KB-XQLC, V3 live, checked 2026-11-04)
- The tag call POST /v2.0/{project_id}/subnets/{subnet_id}/tags/action takes the subnet.id of the v1 subnet create answer (not neutron_subnet_id), and POST /v2/{project_id}/os-vendor-volumes/{volume_id}/tags/action tags a disk right after a Cinder create (POST /v2/{project_id}/volumes) while it is still in state creating; both tags read back with GET .../tags. (KB-XU74, V3 live, checked 2026-11-09)
- A VPC create in a project that has reached its VPC quota fails with a quota error, not with a syntax or validation error. (KB-Y72H, V3 live, checked 2026-08-18)
- The description of a security group rule is free text and has no effect on filtering. A rule described "Deny all traffic" with protocol All, port range 0 to 0 and remote_ip_prefix 0.0.0.0/0 is an ordinary allow-all rule. (KB-YGSM, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-02)
- A VPC route table on T Cloud Public (TCP) cannot hold two routes with the same destination, even when the next hop types differ, and the system route created for a VPC subnet CIDR cannot be modified or deleted. (KB-YHLQ, V1 docs, checked 2026-08-26)
- VPC peering is not billed. (KB-YHSH, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-09-16)
- The default VPC route table quota is 200 routes per table, which caps a static-routing pod network on ECS at roughly 200 nodes before a quota increase. (KB-ZKI3, V1 docs, checked 2026-06-12)
- In eu-de POST /v1/{project_id}/privateips without device_owner creates a plain reserved private IP (device_owner empty), not a virtual IP; an EIP bound to it reaches no server. A virtual IP needs device_owner neutron:VIP_PORT at creation, the field cannot be changed later. (KB-ZO2E, V3 live, checked 2026-11-22)
- In the opentelekomcloud Terraform provider 1.37.8, opentelekomcloud_vpc_secgroup_rule_v3 uses the argument names ether_type and multi_port, carries an explicit action field, and takes a port list or a range in one field. The v2 spellings ethertype and multiport are rejected by terraform validate, and port_range_min and port_range_max are absent from the v3 schema. (KB-ZULT, V3 live, checked 2026-09-16)

## pricing (64)

- The price API returns rows for flavors marked sellout in cond:operation:az and for ramped-down services. Orderability follows from the per-AZ state, not from the presence of a price row. (KB-4ABK, V3 live, checked 2026-09-25)
- No public service description of the Swiss offering (eu-ch2) was found on 2026-10-08: the TCP service description revision 17.08.2026 covers EU-DE and EU-NL only, the Swiss product pages link that same general document, and the operator's Swiss site offers a price calculator in CHF and a technical flyer of 2022 but no service description or price list. What is offered in eu-ch2 has to be confirmed with the Swiss entity before it is quoted. (KB-4FW6, V0 recheck due, docs, checked 2026-10-08)
- Answers of the public price API can carry response.cachedAt, the time of a server cache in server time, two hours ahead of UTC on 2026-09-24. That day an unfiltered query answered from a cache of 2026-09-07 and a query for sn=ecs from one of 2026-09-17, so a price read today can come from a cache more than two weeks old. (KB-5LLN, V3 live, checked 2026-09-24)
- On 2026-10-14 the TCP price API, serving its cache of 2026-10-10, gave the 4- and 8-GPU p5s (H100 PCIe) sizes a lower per-GPU open Linux rate than the 1- and 2-GPU sizes, as the list before 2026-09-10 did. The single per-GPU rate the API carried on 2026-09-10 now sits in the p5s rows of the service description revision 09.10.2026 instead. (KB-5RCL, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-10-14)
- A price API result for one sn can contain records of another product: the sn=dc result for rn=eu-de carried one stray DAS row, distinguishable by its productName field. (KB-6A7H, V3 live, checked 2026-08-25)
- In the c9 price table of the TCP service description the flavour name is printed on the Oracle row, and the open Linux row directly above it carries its rate without a flavour name. A search combining a c9 flavour name with the words open Linux matches nothing. (KB-6HHT, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-09-11)
- The Financial Dashboard has a board "Consumption View (Hourly)" (provided by T Cloud Public) with hourly stacked bars per tenant, per category and per project; its tooltip lists the cost of one hour per flavor and OS, for example "GPU Accelerated g7v.2xl.8 Windows", without a split by server. At about 15:03 UTC on 2026-11-18 the latest hour shown was 13:00, so an hour appears there about two hours later. (KB-6UN6, V3 live, checked 2026-11-18)
- A stopped g7v.2xlarge.8 Windows ECS in eu-de is not billed for its flavor from the second of the stop: in the hourly Financial Dashboard data the flavor row (product OTC_G7V2XL8_WI) of a server stopped at 16:01:51 Berlin time ended with 111 seconds in that hour and had no row in the next hours, while its two ESSD disks went on billing in full under their own resource ids. This settles the conflict of KB-NAAF in favour of the service description and the ECS documentation. (KB-7MM3, V3 live, checked 2026-11-18)
- A g7v.2xlarge.8 Windows ECS in eu-de is charged for its flavor to the second, with no rounding to a full hour: in the hourly Financial Dashboard data a run of ten and a half minutes counted only those ten and a half minutes, from the os-start call (3 seconds before ACTIVE) to SHUTOFF. Each run is its own interval; the disks go on without a gap. (KB-7SVG, V3 live, checked 2026-11-19)
- The TCP price API returns rows for services that are no longer offerable. By 2026-09-02 this had been observed for SFS Common I/O, BMS and DIS, so a price-API hit alone does not show that a service can be ordered. (KB-7VPP, V3 live, checked 2026-09-24)
- Every record of the public price API has an id, a SKU code, while opiFlavour is empty on some: in eu-de on 2026-09-24 on 11 of 461 rds records, 1 of 11 eip records and 6 of 27 coss records. Every ecs, deh, obs, elb and nat record carried one. The id is the key to look a record up by. (KB-AEVL, V3 live, checked 2026-09-24)
- In the TCP service description the commercial limit per business partner (section 3.1.1.1, footnote 2) and the footnote 1 marker for no guaranteed capacity are set only on the flexible-purpose families x1, x1e, x1m, x2, x2e, x2m and x2ne. The s9, m9 and c9 rows carry neither marker. (KB-AH5P, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-09-11)
- The price API covers the s2, s3, s7n and s9 ECS families, so a flavor missing from the price API can still be listed in the service description catalog. (KB-AI2E, V3 live, checked 2026-07-15)
- On the TCP price model page a Reserved package can be upgraded at any time to a larger instance type or a newer generation (for example c3 to c4 or s2 to c3) through the Service Desk. The term end stays the same: the time already used is billed to the day at the old rate (daily rate of the monthly price times the days passed) and the new package runs to the original end, also billed to the day. Reserved Upfront allows no such switch; it is billed as the full amount for 8,760 hours (365 days x 24 hours) at the end of the month of the order and then shows on each invoice without a charge. (KB-B2A7, V1 docs, checked 2026-11-07)
- Under the TCP service description a Reserved or Reserved Upfront term of 12, 24 or 36 months needs an agreed minimum order volume. It is a monthly usage contingent for the selected flavor type, not fixed to one instance: one or several instances of that type use it, and it is billed even when it is not or not fully used. Use beyond the minimum order volume and all use after the term are billed in the Open Elastic model. (KB-B2JD, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-11-07)
- A query of the price API that matches nothing answers HTTP 200 with stats.count 0 and result as an empty list, while a query that matches answers result as an object keyed by service name. A service name the API does not know answers HTTP 500 with code Error, so an empty answer and a refused query can be told apart. (KB-BVUH, V3 live, checked 2026-09-24)
- A private system disk image of a Windows Server 2022 ECS with a 100 GB system disk is about 14 GB and is billed as OBS Standard Space (product OTC_OBST_SP) under the image id from the start of the image job, in the hourly Financial Dashboard. (KB-CFPQ, V3 live, checked 2026-11-19)
- The TCP price model page says Reserved is not available for every flavor and names terms of 12 or 24 months, while the service description of 09.10.2026 (the contract) names 12, 24 and/or 36 months. The price row of the flavor decides: the service description tables show n.a. and the price API shows zero for a term that is not offered, for example no 36-month term for g7v.2xlarge.8 in eu-de. (KB-COBA, V1 docs, checked 2026-11-07)
- A g7v.2xlarge.8 Windows ECS in eu-de is billed for its flavor from ACTIVE to SHUTOFF without rounding to the hour, also for a run of about half a minute and for a run of just over one hour: each was billed only for the seconds between ACTIVE and SHUTOFF in the hourly Financial Dashboard (runs on 2026-11-19). (KB-DK5Z, V3 live, checked 2026-11-19)
- The TCP price API gives per record PAYG in EUR per hour, R12, R24 and R36 as the Reserved price in EUR per unit and month, and RU12, RU24 and RU36 as the Reserved Upfront total of the term divided by its months; zero means the term is not offered. The API page labels the fields without a unit; the service description lists Reserved in euro per month and Reserved Upfront as one euro amount for the whole term, and the API values match it that way for g7v.2xlarge.8 Windows in eu-de. (KB-DXYK, V3 live, checked 2026-11-07)
- A price change is pre-announced inside the current revision of the service description. A section 6 price row can carry a "from DD.MM.YYYY:" subline: the value under the marker is the new hourly price, the value on the row above is the one in force until that date. (KB-FBVR, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-07-05)
- Money values returned by the public price API use English number formatting: comma as thousands separator, dot as decimal separator, six decimal places, and the currency code appended after the value. (KB-FE6O, V3 live, checked 2026-06-11)
- Windows ECS are priced in a separate, higher tier per flavor than the Open Linux tier: on 2026-09-24 the Windows rate was higher on all 306 flavors of the six ECS service names in eu-de that carry both. The OS of every VM decides which price row applies. (KB-FIBF, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-09-24)
- Price rows in the service description can carry a "from <date>:" subline that announces a future value, so a parser reading only the main row returns the superseded value. (KB-FZRN, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-07-01)
- The en variant of the price API writes every amount as a number with a decimal point, a comma as thousands separator and the currency code after it, while the de variant writes a decimal comma. A reader has to know which variant it reads, because a comma followed by three digits is ambiguous on its own. (KB-H5VQ, V3 live, checked 2026-09-24)
- A pre-announced price change can move different rows by different ratios. In the change effective 01.07.2026, 81 of 90 affected ECS price points moved by factor 1.2500 and the nine s9 Windows rows by 1.1103, the compute and the Windows license component moving separately. (KB-HOSK, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-07-05)
- A query to the TCP price API with sn=ucs returned HTTP 500 rather than an empty result set. (KB-HSN2, V3 live, checked 2026-09-01)
- Reserved terms and discount depth are family-dependent: the 36-month term is offered for part of the ECS families. (KB-HSTO, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- The unit field of the price API is free text with about 20 values, among them h, GB/month, Item/month, h/month and an empty unit. isMRC is true on some records whose unit is h, so neither field alone tells an hourly rate from a monthly one. (KB-HYBW, V3 live, checked 2026-09-24)
- The service description lists flavor x1e.slarge.2 with 3 vCPU and 6 GB RAM, a specification the flavor name does not reflect. (KB-IDKJ, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-07-01)
- The Financial Dashboard has a consumption view by tag with hourly and daily granularity and key/value selection, backed by the Business.tag_daily and Business.tag_hourly tables. Tagged resource costs appear in the invoice CSV. The invoice document stays per tenant and not every product is taggable. (KB-JDBN, V1 docs, checked 2026-07-17)
- Ordering Reserved on TCP: it is ordered in the eShop or through Telekom Sales, and a booking through a sales contact or the Support Center needs a lead time of five working days before the term starts. Reserved is invoiced monthly and allows a switch to the next higher flavor or reserved class during the term; Reserved Upfront is invoiced for the whole term in the first month after provision and allows no switch. (KB-KM2G, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-11-07)
- In the hourly Financial Dashboard the OBS Standard Space row (OTC_OBST_SP) of a private image carries GB-months: its hourly quantity matched the image size in GiB divided by 720, and the first row carried a full hour although the image job started 20 minutes into that hour (image of 14344941568 bytes, 2026-11-19). (KB-KZ3B, V3 live, checked 2026-11-19)
- On the TCP price model page a Reserved term starts at 0:00 on the order day. A package ordered during a month is billed only for the rest of that month, and the last month is billed only for its part. Packages are not renewed automatically. The contingent of a month is the days of that month times 24 hours (summer and winter time counted); the unused part of a month expires and cannot be used later in the term. (KB-LWOH, V1 docs, checked 2026-11-07)
- An Elastic IP bound to a stopped ECS in eu-de is billed on as product OTC_ELASTIC_IP under its own resource id, by the fraction of the hour, from the minute of its creation, in the hourly Financial Dashboard. (KB-M7ZW, V3 live, checked 2026-11-19)
- Open conflict on TCP, open with Telekom Sales since 2026-11-07: the price model page for computing says GPU flavors are billed also when stopped, while the service description (revision 09.10.2026, section 6.1.1) lists only i3, d2 and all Dedicated Host types as billed when stopped and the ECS documentation says a stopped G6, G7, G7v or P5e is billed only for its disk. Until Telekom Sales answers, price by the service description and the ECS documentation, and say so on every GPU line. It matters most for GPU machines that run part time: if stopped GPU flavors were billed, each would cost a full month by the hour and the part-time pay-per-use model would not hold. A lab run cannot settle it; it needs a written statement of the Service Desk or Telekom Sales, or the Financial Dashboard of a tenant whose stopped GPU machine was billed. (KB-NAAF, V1 docs, checked 2026-11-07)
- The public price API returns money values in English number format: a comma separates thousands and a dot separates decimals, followed by the currency code. A parser that reads the comma as a decimal separator returns values a thousand times too low. (KB-NCPE, V3 live, checked 2026-06-11)
- How to price Reserved on TCP: count the pooled hours of all instances of one billing item (flavor plus operating system) in a month, not the hours of each instance. The unit count is booked for the whole term, so choose it once against the months of the term; in each month n units cover n times the hours of that month and the rest runs Open Elastic. One more unit pays off when the rest of a month is above the monthly Reserved price divided by the hourly Open Elastic price of the same item. Use the real month length or state the 730-hour average, since Reserved is a flat monthly amount and a short month lowers the saving. Show the agreed minimum order volume as an open point on every reserved line, and a term the price row does not offer as not offered. (KB-NPRJ, V1 docs, checked 2026-11-07)
- The Compute section of the Financial Dashboard shows "Compute spendings (details)" as daily stacked bars, one series per flavor and OS (for example "GPU Accelerated g7v.2xl.8 Windows"), with Mean and Total per series; it does not split a series by server. Viewed at about 15:00 UTC on 2026-11-18, the last bar was 2026-11-17, so a day appears there the next day at the earliest. (KB-PBIC, V3 live, checked 2026-11-18)
- The price API switches to the announced value on the effective date. For the flavor families the API covers, the from-column of the service description and the API value agree to six decimals. (KB-PL7K, V3 live, checked 2026-07-05)
- The TCP service description charges the storage space of stored private images as OBS Standard Object Storage Space and makes no charge for image management, retrieval, requests and outbound traffic; storage is billed in GiB by the hour, divided by the hours of the calendar month. The older IMS user guide FAQ text that images do not occupy the user's resources does not describe this charge. (KB-PLV4, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-11-19)
- The eu-ch2 slice of the public TCP price API is not the Swiss price list: on 2026-10-08 it held 75 records marked EUR, 15 of them (KMS API calls, the five OBS Standard Space tiers, eight SMN rows) with exactly the numbers the Swiss calculator shows as CHF, and 60 rows that the Swiss calculator does not carry at all (API Gateway, HSS, WAF domain, FunctionGraph, LTS, DNS queries, OBS cold and warm space, outbound traffic, a firewall and VPN row), most of them priced zero. It carries no ECS, EVS or RDS row for eu-ch2, and its values were unchanged between 2026-09-24 and 2026-10-08. (KB-PRGT, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-10-08)
- The service description lists UCS rates in section 6.8.5, billed per hour and per CPU in the cluster, with the same rate for clusters inside and outside the tenant and the eu-nl region marked as not applicable. (KB-QIV4, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-09-01)
- Prices of the Swiss offering (region eu-ch2) come from a price calculator of its own on the operator's Swiss site (calculator client 2), in CHF: on 2026-10-08 it had 19 modules with 255 records, among them 146 ECS rows (s3 flavors; Open Linux, Oracle Linux, RedHat, SUSE, SUSE for SAP and Windows tiers), 40 RDS rows, Direct Connect, Dedicated Host, CCE, EVS (SAS and SSD), OBS, EIP, ELB, NAT, private DNS, VPC, VPC endpoint, KMS, SMN, CBR, SDRS, AOM and dedicated WAF, with pay-as-you-use and 12-month reserved packages. The main TCP calculator lists eu-ch2 in its region picker but its module picker returns no module for that region. (KB-QWED, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-10-08)
- In the price API, sn=ecs covers only the s2, s3, s7n and s9 families. The vss.* entries in that payload are EVS disk SKUs rather than a compute family, and the other ECS families sit under other service names of the price API. (KB-RMW2, V3 live, checked 2026-09-24)
- The price API returns rows and the documentation mirror keeps full user guides and API references for services that are no longer offered, VBS and CSBS (end of life since 01.01.2026) among them, so presence in either source does not show that a service is orderable. For BMS in eu-de on 2026-09-25, the legacy BMS flavors are all abandon or sellout, while the bms rows of the price API now cover the orderable Physical ECS flavor c7t.28xl. (KB-SCLG, V3 live, checked 2026-09-25)
- In the public price API the service name sn=dcaas returns HTTP 500, while sn=dc with rn=eu-de returns the full Direct Connect price list including bandwidth tiers and ports. The same service can be served under an abbreviation different from the one used elsewhere. (KB-SEDA, V3 live, checked 2026-08-25)
- On TCP each kind of VM (flavor plus operating system) is one billing item: the usage of the single VMs is recorded per second, summed over the calendar month and billed in hours, so several Windows VMs of one flavor add up in one item. A flavor with Windows and the same flavor with Linux are separate items, but several VMs of one flavor with different open Linux derivatives are not separated. A Reserved package draws on one such item. (KB-SFB4, V1 docs, checked 2026-11-07)
- The public price API returns a changing set of ECS records: 502, then 508, then 400 within one month, with the x1 family and the s3 R36 reserved fields disappearing between calls. (KB-SFUF, V3 live, checked 2026-07-01)
- The TCP service description, revision 09.10.2026, sets a compensation of half of the remaining payments for ending a reserved package before its minimum term (for reserved upfront: the pro-rata residual value), but the rule sits in section 7.2.6.2 under the special conditions for migration under the Data Act; no general early-end rule was found outside that section. (KB-SHDM, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-22)
- Reserved price rows can be left out of a pre-announced increase. In the change effective 01.07.2026 the R12, R24 and R36 cells carried no from-date marker and their values did not move. (KB-SUE5, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-07-05)
- The TCP price model page for computing says a Reserved package is a monthly credit of days x 24 hours for one instance type, not tied to one instance: several instances of that type may use it for part periods in the same month, use beyond it is billed in the Elastic mode, and the unused part of a month expires. Its example: two s2.xlarge.8 instances run at the same time and the use beyond the monthly 672 hours is billed Elastic. (KB-UD2I, V1 docs, checked 2026-11-07)
- The TCP price API and the contractual service description can carry different rates for the same flavor family. On 2026-10-14 the API, serving its cache of 2026-10-10, held the earlier p5s GPU table with a lower per-GPU rate on the 4- and 8-GPU sizes, while the p5s rows of the service description revision 09.10.2026 held one per-GPU rate on every size. (KB-UVSS, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-10-14)
- A Reserved or Reserved Upfront booking on TCP is a billing contingent for a flavor type: it allocates no resources in the customer tenant and holds no capacity for a stopped instance. (KB-VCXP, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-21)
- The public TCP price API answers at https://calculator.otc-service.com/en/open-telekom-price-api/ with JSON and needs no key. It takes sn for the service name and rn for the region. limitFrom pages through the records and limitMax sets how many come per page: 500 without it, while limitMax=10000 returned each region in one answer on 2026-09-24. (KB-W7ZS, V3 live, checked 2026-09-24)
- The break-even of one Reserved unit of g7v Windows in eu-de, the monthly reserved rate divided by the on-demand hourly rate, is 720.7 h for R12 and 643.1 h for R24 on g7v.2xlarge.8, 675.1 h and 577.3 h on g7v.4xlarge.8 and 683.1 h and 573.1 h on g7v.6xlarge.8, so an R12 unit of g7v.2xlarge.8 Windows pays off only for a pool that runs about the whole month. (KB-X22H, V3 live, checked 2026-11-19)
- A stopped GPU ECS of the G7v family (g7v.2xlarge.8 with Windows, product OTC_G7V2XL8_WI) is billed no flavor from the second of the stop: the billed hourly rows end at the stop time to the second (a stop at 16:01:51 gave a last row of 111 seconds in the 16:00 hour and no flavor row after it), while its EVS disks keep billing in full per hour under their own resource ids. Live result for the open conflict KB-NAAF: for G7v the ECS documentation holds, the price page note on stopped GPU flavors does not. (KB-XDRX, V3 live, checked 2026-11-18)
- The public price API held these record counts on 2026-09-24: eu-de 3386 records under 100 service names, eu-nl 2638 records under 64 names and eu-ch2 75 records under 14 names. (KB-XEGQ, V3 live, checked 2026-09-24)
- A record in the public price API does not show that a service can still be ordered: rows stay served for services that have reached end of life, so orderability has to be confirmed against the service catalog. (KB-XELM, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-09-24)
- Record ids of the price API are specific to a region: on 2026-09-24 none of the 6099 ids of eu-de, eu-nl and eu-ch2 appeared in two regions, so an id also fixes the region. (KB-XJDA, V3 live, checked 2026-09-24)
- SKU ids seen in the price API for network and object items include OTC_OBST_SP_2 (OBS Standard storage), OTC_OBST_REQ (OBS requests per 1000), OTC_LBV3_B_2AZ_20 (dedicated ELB, 2 AZ, 20 LCU), OTC_ELASTIC_IP and OTC_VPC_UP_S_2 (egress tier). (KB-YAT6, V3 live, checked 2026-09-24)
- In eu-de on 2026-09-24 the price API held the x1 family under sn=ecsflex in the size ratios 2, 3 and 4 only, 18 flavors, and held no m3 flavor under any of the six ECS service names. (KB-YUVN, V3 live, checked 2026-09-24)
- A reservation is bound to the instance type it was bought for and does not follow a downsize of the instance. (KB-ZJ4B, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-07-01)
- Records of the price API with idGroupTiered set carry the bounds of their tier in fromOn and upTo. On 2026-09-24 there were 355 such records in 18 groups in eu-de and eu-nl, most of them outbound internet traffic (group OTC_VPC_UP) and OBS outbound traffic. (KB-ZNJK, V3 live, checked 2026-09-24)

## elb (54)

- Creating the pool with listener_id and leaving default_pool_id off the listener makes the pool depend on the listener in Terraform, so destroy removes the pool first and the listener second, matching the order the ELB v3 API accepts. (KB-2BL5, V3 live, checked 2026-09-16)
- Only a dedicated load balancer can send traffic to servers outside its own VPC. With IP as a backend enabled it adds servers by private IPv4 address in a VPC linked by VPC peering or in an on-premises data centre linked by Direct Connect or VPN. (KB-36IY, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- A server added to a dedicated load balancer by its IP address takes at most 50,000 concurrent connections. (KB-3LFE, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- Load balancers are listed with GET /v2/{project_id}/elb/loadbalancers. The response holds loadbalancers[], and each entry carries name, id, operating_status, provisioning_status and vip_address. (KB-4XYI, V3 live, checked 2026-09-25)
- The eu-de price API bills the shared load balancer by the hour under record OTC_LBAAS (Shared Elastic Loadbalancer), while dedicated load balancers are billed by LCU records OTC_LBV3_B_* per number of AZs, each with a base record at zero. (KB-5G6I, V3 live, checked 2026-11-20)
- GET /v3/{project_id}/elb/loadbalancers/{loadbalancer_id} for a dedicated load balancer that no longer exists answers HTTP 404 with error_code ELB.8904, 'loadbalancer <id> could not be found'. Seen after a terraform destroy that stopped halfway: the load balancer was gone in TCP while the Terraform state still listed it. (KB-7QDZ, V3 live, checked 2026-10-06)
- The public price API carries a record named Shared Elastic Loadbalancer for eu-nl (seen on 2026-09-25), although section 6.5.3 of the service description shows n.a. for EU-NL and the ELB documentation allows only dedicated load balancers in eu-nl, so that record is no proof of availability. (KB-B4SW, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- When an elastic IP is passed into opentelekomcloud_lb_loadbalancer_v3 by id, the attribute public_ip[0].address stays null, so an output that reads it fails the apply at the last step with every resource already created. (KB-BETP, V3 live, checked 2026-09-16)
- IP as a backend cannot be switched off once it is enabled. In the v3 API the load balancer field ip_target_enable can only be updated to true. (KB-BFDT, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- An ELB HTTPS listener presents a server certificate whose content and private key the owner uploads in PEM format, and with SNI one listener serves several domain names with their own certificates, so the TLS handshake shows the owner's domain and certificate. (KB-BNR5, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- Servers added by IP address cannot receive the client source IP address. For HTTP and HTTPS listeners the ELB guide points to the X-Forwarded-For header instead and it names no alternative for TCP and UDP listeners. (KB-CJTJ, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- On 2026-09-25 GET /v3/{project_id}/elb/loadbalancers on elb.eu-de returned the field ip_target_enable in the load balancer object, so the state of IP as a backend can be read back through the API. (KB-CQMN, V3 live, checked 2026-09-25)
- DELETE /v3/{project_id}/elb/pools/{id} returns HTTP 204 while the listener that the pool belongs to still exists, which frees the listener for a later delete. (KB-DIPH, V3 live, checked 2026-09-16)
- A shared load balancer cannot route traffic to backend servers in another VPC and cannot be used across accounts. The shared ELB API requires each member to sit in a subnet of the VPC of the load balancer. (KB-DVMF, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- In the opentelekomcloud provider a changed certificate body on an ELB v3 certificate resource is planned as "updated in-place": the plan carries no replacement marker, and an apply would swap the certificate the listener serves. (KB-DWDN, V3 live, checked 2026-09-17)
- On 2026-10-05 GET /v3/{project_id}/elb/flavors in eu-de listed six dedicated L7 flavors with their QPS: L7_flavor.elb.s1.small 4,000, s1.medium 16,000, s1.large 80,000, s2.small 8,000, s2.medium 40,000 and s2.large 160,000, plus L4 flavors s1 and s2 in small, medium and large. Terraform's l7_flavor takes the flavor id, which the data source opentelekomcloud_lb_flavor_v3 finds by name. (KB-E2KU, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-10-05)
- A shared ELB v2.0 in eu-de with an HTTP listener on port 80, a ROUND_ROBIN pool and an HTTP health monitor marked an ECS backend ONLINE within about 20 s of the monitor create, once the web server on the backend answered. The backend security group let port 80 in from the subnet range and from 100.125.0.0/16; which of the two the health checks use was not proven. (KB-EGHR, V3 live, checked 2026-10-12)
- The shared ELB v2.0 API in eu-de accepts AK/SK calls signed SDK-HMAC-SHA256 without an X-Project-Id header: the creates of load balancer, listener, pool, member and health monitor answered 201, the deletes 204, and a read after the delete answered 404. (KB-ERDD, V3 live, checked 2026-10-12)
- A backend member of a dedicated load balancer in eu-de that has nothing listening yet shows operating_status OFFLINE with reason_code CONNECT_FAILED in GET /v3/{project_id}/elb/pools/{pool_id}/members, and the HTTPS listener then answers 502. In a run on 2026-10-06 the server was created at 12:43 UTC, cloud-init reached modules:final at 12:44:51, nginx was being installed at about 12:45:06 and the member read ONLINE with the site answering 200 at 12:45:28: about two and a half minutes from terraform creating the server to a working site. (KB-ESMU, V3 live, checked 2026-10-06)
- The ELB v3 certificate create at POST /v3/{project_id}/elb/certificates accepts both RSA and ECDSA certificates. (KB-FA4K, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-05-26)
- DELETE /v3/{project_id}/elb/listeners/{listener_id} on a dedicated load balancer answers 409 ELB.8907 'pool <id> is using this listener' while a pool is bound to the listener, also when the pool has no members left. Deleting the pool first frees the listener. In Terraform, bind the pool with listener_id on the pool resource instead of default_pool_id on the listener: then destroy deletes the pool before the listener; with default_pool_id it tries the listener first and stops. (KB-GTIT, V3 live, checked 2026-10-06)
- A dedicated load balancer binds an existing elastic IP through public_ip { id = ... } and an SNAT rule binds one through floating_ip_id, so addresses allocated in a separate stack keep their ids and their DNS record across destroy and re-apply of the consuming stack. (KB-GZM3, V3 live, checked 2026-09-16)
- Shared load balancers cannot be created in eu-nl. Only dedicated load balancers can be created there, on the console or through the API. (KB-H34Q, V0 recheck due, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- T Cloud Public (TCP) offers load balancing at layer 4 and layer 7 through Elastic Load Balancer (ELB): network load balancing for TCP and UDP and application load balancing for HTTP and HTTPS, as shared or dedicated load balancers, with dedicated ones spread over up to three availability zones. (KB-HZXL, V1 docs, checked 2026-10-11)
- L7 rules of a policy are listed with GET /v3/{project_id}/elb/l7policies/{policy_id}/rules. Each rule carries id, type such as PATH, HOST_NAME or HEADER, compare_type such as STARTS_WITH or EQUAL_TO, value, conditions and invert. (KB-ICS6, V3 live, checked 2026-09-25)
- On 2026-09-25 GET /v3/{project_id}/elb/flavors in eu-de returned 17 dedicated load balancer flavors over five types: L4 (6), L7 (6), L7_elastic (3), L4_elastic_max (1) and L7_elastic_max (1). This is what the listing showed on that date. (KB-J3DZ, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- A shared ELB with an EIP, a TERMINATED_HTTPS listener on 443 (uploaded self-signed certificate) and an HTTP listener on 80, both forwarding HTTP to one backend ECS on port 80, answered a client on the Internet with HTTP 200 and the backend page on both listeners and presented the uploaded certificate (subject CN); while the backend web server was still starting it answered 502 Bad Gateway, and a member without a health monitor shows operating_status NO_MONITOR. (KB-JYKA, V3 live, checked 2026-11-21)
- A dedicated ELB v3 load balancer create expects vip_subnet_cidr_id to hold the neutron subnet id; with the VPC subnet id the VIP is not placed. The mapping comes from GET /v1/{project_id}/subnets/{vpc_subnet_id}, whose response carries neutron_subnet_id. (KB-KG3S, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-05-26)
- A dedicated load balancer sends its health checks, and for HTTP or HTTPS listeners the forwarded traffic, from IP addresses in its own backend subnet, so the backend servers' security group must allow that subnet on the service and health check ports. A shared load balancer uses 100.125.0.0/16 instead. (KB-KHNT, V1 docs, checked 2026-10-06)
- ELB v3 returns l4_flavor_id and l7_flavor_id as UUIDs, resolved through GET /v3/{project_id}/elb/flavors, which returned 17 records on 2026-09-17 with names of the form L4_flavor.elb.s1.small and L7_flavor.elb.s1.small. (KB-KK7Q, V3 live, checked 2026-09-17)
- Through the v3 API such a server is added as a member with a private IPv4 address and without subnet_cidr_id. The API reference allows it only in backend server groups with protocol TCP, HTTP or HTTPS (the console guide also names UDP) and answers ELB.8939 while ip_target_enable is false. (KB-KKT4, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- The ELB v3 API rejects deletion of a listener while a pool still points at it, with 409 ELB.8907 "pool <id> is using this listener". The stack applies and serves traffic, the failure appears at teardown. (KB-KZUN, V3 live, checked 2026-09-16)
