- The ELB FAQ and the v3 API also allow IP backends in a VPC of another region that is linked by a Cloud Connect cloud connection. Cloud Connect is listed under preview and beta versions in the service description, section 3.12.6. (KB-LD5O, V0 recheck due, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- For an L7 rule created with conditions and no top-level value, the v3 API returns value as an empty string and the match values in the conditions array, one per entry, in an order of its own: two conditions came back reversed on 2026-09-25. A rule created with a value and conditions returns that value. (KB-LJKN, V3 live, checked 2026-09-25)
- In the opentelekomcloud Terraform provider an HTTPS listener takes its server certificate through default_tls_container_ref, which must hold the id of an opentelekomcloud_lb_certificate_v3 resource created with the fields certificate, private_key and domain. TLS terminates at the load balancer listener. (KB-MPJZ, V3 live, checked 2026-09-16)
- The v3 rule listing and the v3 single-rule-by-id call return identical rule data for an L7 rule, conditions included. (KB-NAR6, V3 live, checked 2026-09-25)
- On T Cloud Public (TCP) in eu-de the ELB v3 call GET /v3/{project_id}/elb/certificates returns HTTP 200 and delivers the certificate list under the response key certificates. (KB-OACX, V3 live, checked 2026-07-02)
- Security group rules of servers added by IP address must allow traffic from the backend subnet of the load balancer, otherwise health checks fail. The ELB FAQ asks for at least 16 free IP addresses in the subnet where the load balancer resides to enable IP as a backend. (KB-OEB6, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- A dedicated load balancer placed in eu-de-01 or eu-de-02 reaches backend servers sitting in eu-de-03 across availability zones inside the same VPC. (KB-OVNG, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- Rebuilding an ELB in another project includes creating the backend pools and their members again. (KB-OVTY, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- ELB L7 policies are listed with GET /v3/{project_id}/elb/l7policies using limit (default and maximum 2000) and marker paging; the response holds l7policies[] plus page_info with current_count and previous_marker; next_marker appears only when the server offers another page. In eu-de on 2026-09-25 a short page did not mean the end of the list: with limit=2 the first page held 1 of 3 policies and carried no next_marker, while the default limit returned all 3. (KB-P5PW, V3 live, checked 2026-09-25)
- POST /v3/{project_id}/elb/certificates expects the body wrapped in a certificate object. A flat body returns HTTP 400 "Invalid request body: Error map format". The inner fields are name, type (server), domain, certificate and private_key. (KB-PJVB, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-05-26)
- GET /v3/{project_id}/elb/availability-zones on eu-de returned eu-de-01 and eu-de-02 on two separate tenants. The service description states up to three availability zones, which is a contractual maximum and not the zone list this endpoint returned. (KB-QQBS, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-10-05)
- An AS group over eu-de-01 and eu-de-02 in one subnet, bound to two shared ELB pools with HTTP health checks, had both instances ONLINE in both pools 153 s after the group start when the web server ran as a systemd unit (systemd-run from cloud-init). (KB-QZZ5, V3 live, checked 2026-11-20)
- A forwarding policy of a dedicated ELB takes one rule per rule type: on 2026-09-25 a second PATH rule on the same policy was refused with HTTP 409 ELB.8907 (already has rules with type PATH), while a HOST_NAME rule next to it was accepted. (KB-ROUV, V3 live, checked 2026-09-25)
- On 2026-07-02 GET /v3/{project_id}/elb/availability-zones in eu-de returned exactly two zones, eu-de-01 and eu-de-02, both in state ACTIVE. Dedicated load balancer placement on that date followed this listing. (KB-SBUW, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- A shared ELB server certificate is uploaded with POST /v2.0/lbaas/certificates and a flat body (name, type server, certificate, private_key in PEM); the answer carries id at top level and a field certificate with the PEM. POST /v2.0/{project_id}/elb/certificates answers APIGW.0101. A TERMINATED_HTTPS listener on 443 with default_tls_container_ref set to that id was created (201). (KB-T6R4, V3 live, checked 2026-11-20)
- Each L7 policy returned by the v3 listing carries id, name, action, listener_id, redirect_pool_id, redirect_url_config and priority. (KB-TQG5, V3 live, checked 2026-09-25)
- A TERMINATED_HTTPS listener on ELB requires the TLS certificate to be uploaded into the target project before the listener is created. (KB-U47A, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- A shared load balancer listener created with POST /v2.0/lbaas/listeners and protocol TERMINATED_HTTPS without default_tls_container_ref answers 400 ListenerProtocolTERMINATEDHTTPSNeedCertID; an HTTP listener on port 80 on the same load balancer is created at once. (KB-W6JW, V3 live, checked 2026-11-20)
- The resource opentelekomcloud_lb_loadbalancer_v3 exposes the public address of a dedicated load balancer as public_ip[0].address. (KB-YFA5, V3 live, checked 2026-09-16)
- For multi-condition L7 rules the v2 rules endpoint returns an empty value string and its response body carries no conditions array, so the match value of such a rule is not present in the v2 response; the v3 response carries it in conditions. (KB-YNV3, V3 live, checked 2026-09-25)
- Dedicated load balancers and public NAT gateways are offered in eu-nl. On 2026-09-25 the ELB v3 flavor list of eu-nl returned the same 17 dedicated flavors as eu-de, the ELB AZ list returned eu-nl-01, eu-nl-02 and eu-nl-03, and the public NAT API (GET /v2.0/nat_gateways) answered in eu-nl. (KB-ZABN, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- Elastic Load Balancer (ELB) distributes incoming traffic across several backend servers, checks their health and works with Auto Scaling. It is offered as shared and as dedicated load balancers for application load balancing at layer 7 and network load balancing at layer 4; a dedicated load balancer has its own resources, can span up to three availability zones and comes in fixed and elastic flavors. The documentation states that eu-de offers dedicated and shared load balancers and eu-nl offers dedicated ones. (KB-ZYJM, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)

## other (50)

- HetuEngine in MRS 3.5.0-LTS does not support registering an Iceberg catalog as shipped, because HSConsole has no ICEBERG data source type. Workaround: repoint the template of an unused type in HSConsole etc/datasource.properties, restart HetuEngine, then POST the catalog to /v1/hsconsole/datasource on port 29880. (KB-2KDP, V3 live, checked 2026-07-28)
- The base service description, revision 09.10.2026, section 4.2, names a single availability figure: a monthly platform availability target of 99.95%, which applies separately to Elastic Cloud Server, Object Storage Service and Elastic Volume Service. For all other services it states that there is no minimum availability. (KB-35HK, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- FunctionGraph scales to zero when idle and idle time is not charged. (KB-3FHX, V1 docs, checked 2026-08-27)
- FunctionGraph is a managed serverless service that hosts and runs event-driven functions: the customer writes code and sets the trigger conditions, and the service manages the compute resources and scales automatically with the number of requests. Functions are written in Node.js, Python, Java, C#, PHP, Go or a custom runtime and are triggered by API calls or cloud service events; reserved instances keep functions warm against cold starts. The Regions and Endpoints page lists the FunctionGraph API endpoint in eu-de. (KB-3L75, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- Managed Services are optional operations services that Telekom provides on T Cloud Public (TCP) in three packages, Managed Services Basic, Standard and Advanced, covering up to 20, 50 or 150 managed services (operating systems, databases and applications). The portfolio catalogue names applications (Nginx, Tomcat, Apache, firewall, VPN, GitLab), RDS for SQL Server, MySQL and PostgreSQL and self-run SQL Server, MySQL, Oracle, MariaDB and PostgreSQL, managed Linux and Windows Server operating systems and managed containers; larger or custom scopes are offered individually. (KB-4N6Q, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- MRS Spark defaults to spark.sql.storeAssignmentPolicy=LEGACY, which DataSource V2 rejects with 'LEGACY store assignment policy is disallowed in Spark data source V2'. Set --conf spark.sql.storeAssignmentPolicy=ANSI for any Iceberg INSERT. (KB-4Y2C, V3 live, checked 2026-07-28)
- DORA Article 28(8) requires financial entities to put in place exit strategies for ICT services supporting critical or important functions, with comprehensive and documented exit plans that are tested and reviewed periodically, and with transition plans to move the services and data to another provider or back in-house. (KB-6YSY, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- A word-boundary search for nifi across the documentation mirror returned zero hits, so no managed Apache NiFi service is documented. (KB-7KEU, V0 recheck due, docs, checked 2026-10-14)
- Enterprise Agreement 1.0 (EA10) is an optional extended service contract from Telekom in the service classes Silver, Gold and Platinum, ordered separately on top of the standard service. All classes add a consulting and administration service; Gold and Platinum add IT architecture consulting, an availability report and service meetings; Platinum adds dedicated service and delivery management and a manager on duty service. Service levels, response times and credits are contract terms and are not stated here. (KB-7U4I, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- Data Admin Service (DAS) is a cloud database management platform in preview for managing databases on a web console, with database development, operations and intelligent diagnosis. The service description of 09.10.2026 announces it as expected to be available from 1 May 2027, and as a preview version it is meant for testing. (KB-AB67, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- Secure Mail Gateway (SMG) lets applications hosted on T Cloud Public (TCP) send email to the internet through a gateway service, so the sending servers' own IP addresses do not land on spam blacklists; outbound SMTP on ports 465 and 587 is opened only towards this gateway. The documentation names anti-virus and anti-spam checks, three mail servers spread over eu-de-01, eu-de-02 and eu-de-03, and a possible limit of 100 mails per minute per customer, which the service description also reserves. (KB-BGAJ, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- FunctionGraph runs in eu-de only: on 2026-09-25 GET /v2/{project_id}/fgs/functions answered HTTP 200 on functiongraph.eu-de.otc.t-systems.com and HTTP 404 APIGW.0101 on the eu-nl endpoint. (KB-BGBR, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- Consulting Services (CS) are optional consulting services from Telekom in the T Cloud Public (TCP) environment, commissioned separately through the T Cloud Public Support Center, that go beyond the support services of the standard contract. (KB-CXIW, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- The public platform is branded T Cloud Public (TCP) after the 2025 rename. The service description carries the title "Service Specifications & Additional Terms and Conditions T Cloud Public". (KB-DFXG, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-07-15)
- On the shared default DLI queue, statements stayed in LAUNCHING for over 15 minutes and the synchronous submit returned DLI.0019 Get sync job result timeout; the job id is in the error text. Capability tests need a dedicated queue or elastic resource pool. (KB-ETTV, V3 live, checked 2026-07-28)
- Section 7.3 of the TCP service description, revision of 2026-10-09, freezes a tenant for the two weeks before its termination takes effect and erases all its resources when it does, and section 7.5 requires the customer to back up its application data on its own responsibility before that grace period. (KB-F3A4, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- In a HetuEngine Iceberg data source on MRS, hive.config.resources needs absolute paths; relative paths make reads fail with 'EndPoint is not set'. (KB-FAPL, V3 live, checked 2026-07-28)
- Marketplace is an optional service of T Cloud Public (TCP): customers subscribe to and use services from third-party providers and Telekom units through it, and sellers publish software offers through the Marketplace Seller Center, on request. A product offering combines the service type, which is Cloud Container Engine, the license type (open source, free or trial, bring your own license, or private offer) and the seller's software. (KB-FFRB, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- The TCP service description rev. 09.10.2026 covers the public cloud offering and lists no dedicated-cloud, edge-site or on-premises variant. Other delivery models of the provider are separate offerings outside this dataset. (KB-FIKE, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- Uploading core-site.xml as a file-typed property of a HetuEngine data source in MRS corrupts the file, and reads then fail with WstxIOException: Invalid UTF-8 start byte 0x81. (KB-FLDL, V3 live, checked 2026-07-28)
- T Cloud Public (TCP) has no IoT device access endpoint in eu-de: on 2026-10-20 the hostname iotda.eu-de.otc.t-systems.com did not resolve and IoTDA API calls got no answer, while SMN and ModelArts in eu-de resolved and answered. The service description of 09.10.2026 lists no IoT service. (KB-H76B, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-10-20)
- Tag Management Service (TMS) adds key-value tags as metadata to cloud resources and manages them centrally across services and regions; tags are created by hand or predefined and imported in CSV format, and resources are searched and filtered by tag. Costs can be assigned to tags in the invoice documents and in the Financial Dashboard. The Regions and Endpoints page lists API endpoints for TMS in eu-de and eu-nl. (KB-HFN2, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- MRS 3.5.0-LTS ships a vendor-built Apache Iceberg 1.4.1 Spark runtime (iceberg-spark-runtime-3.3_2.12) on the default Spark 3.3.1 classpath, so Iceberg jobs need no extra --jars. The MRS component table and the documentation do not list Iceberg. (KB-HQC7, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-07-28)
- DLI in eu-de accepts Spark 2.4.5 and 3.1.1 only; a job submitted with Spark 3.3.1 is refused although the documentation lists that version. Moving a Spark 3.3 workload to DLI therefore means a Spark downgrade. (KB-HYY7, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-07-30)
- An RFS stack delete ended in DELETION_FAILED when the provider got Internal Server Error on the VPC delete, while a direct VPC delete succeeded; a stack whose agency is already deleted cannot destroy and is removed with POST /v1/{project_id}/stacks/{name}/deletion and retain_all_resources true. Every RFS call needs the header Client-Request-Id. (KB-JOTF, V3 live, checked 2026-11-20)
- DORA Article 30(3)(f) makes exit strategies a mandatory term of contracts for ICT services supporting critical or important functions, in particular an adequate transition period in which the ICT third-party service provider keeps providing the service while the financial entity moves to another provider or in-house. Article 30(2)(d) requires every ICT services contract to provide for access, recovery and return of the data on termination or on the provider's insolvency, resolution or end of business. (KB-K5U4, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- In MRS 3.5.0-LTS, impalad runs on the core nodes and listens on ports 21050 and 21000, so Impala clients connect to a core node. (KB-K6OT, V3 live, checked 2026-07-28)
- Config (Resource Management Service, RMS) lets the customer centrally view all resources of a tenant with their details and relationships. The documentation adds a resource recorder that keeps change records and snapshots in an OBS bucket and sends change notifications through Simple Message Notification, compliance evaluation of resources against Config rules, and advanced queries in ResourceQL. The Regions and Endpoints page lists API endpoints for Config in eu-de and eu-nl. (KB-KHDV, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- Section 7.2.3 of the TCP service description, revision of 2026-10-09, gives a customer who notifies a migration under the Data Act at least 30 days ahead a 30-day transition period. The customer may extend it once, and it never exceeds seven months. (KB-KT7I, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- Announced availability dates in the service description move between revisions. UCS was 01.04.2026 in rev. 08.06.2026 and 01.09.2026 in rev. 17.08.2026, and CSMS moved from 01.07.2026 to 01.10.2026 and then to 01.04.2027. Rev. 09.10.2026 keeps both dates, although the UCS date has passed. (KB-LQBE, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- Data Replication Service (DRS) migrates and synchronizes databases online in real time, to simplify data migration and reduce migration effort; the customer gets tools to query the migration progress, check migration logs and compare migrated items. The Regions and Endpoints page lists API endpoints for DRS in eu-de and eu-nl. (KB-LSZV, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- API Gateway (dedicated) (APIG) is a managed API hosting service whose gateways run dedicated to the customer's tenant; it covers the whole API lifecycle of creating, debugging, publishing and taking APIs offline. Request throttling, authentication, environments with environment variables, signatures, version rollback and API monitoring with alarms are configured per API. The Regions and Endpoints page lists the APIG API endpoint in eu-de. (KB-MIJ4, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- Base support in the service description answers within 4 hours on a best-effort basis. Critical cases are handled 24/7, all other cases Monday to Friday from 8 to 17. (KB-MM3J, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- DORA Article 2(2) calls only the entities of points (a) to (t) of Article 2(1) financial entities, so ICT third-party service providers, listed separately under point (u), are not the addressees of the exit duty in Article 28, and Article 28(1)(a) keeps financial entities fully responsible for compliance when they use ICT services. (KB-NLXT, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- hetu-cli creates a HetuEngine compute instance in MRS without the web UI. The user needs a Manager role with Submit and Admin on YARN queue root.default (without it hetu-cli reports 'user does not belong to the specified tenant') and membership in the hetuuser group, which takes effect only after a HetuEngine restart. (KB-NVXS, V3 live, checked 2026-07-28)
- Enterprise Support Agreement 2.0 (ESA20) is an optional extended support package from Telekom, ordered separately. The basic package includes the Financial Dashboard and a consulting and administration service; dedicated service delivery management and a service delivery manager on duty are optional components. Service levels, response times and credits are contract terms and are not stated here. (KB-NZSQ, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- Optical Character Recognition (OCR) automatically recognizes printed and handwritten characters in images and converts them into editable text. It is used through open APIs, and the documentation describes general OCR that extracts text from images of tables, documents, receipts and forms and returns it in JSON format. The Regions and Endpoints page lists the OCR API endpoint in eu-de. (KB-OBHQ, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- Financial Dashboard (FD, former Enterprise Dashboard) gives the customer an overview of the resources used and their costs in a separate portal reached through MyWorkplace, with predefined and custom diagrams, CSV and API export, and email notification when customer-set cost thresholds are reached. It comes as Small, which is included in the standard scope, Medium and Large; Medium and Large add support for several T Cloud Public tenants and Large adds organization management. (KB-QAFU, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- The DORA oversight framework of Chapter V Section II applies to ICT third-party service providers that the ESAs designate as critical under Article 31(1)(a), and under Article 31(9) the ESAs publish and update the list of critical providers yearly. (KB-QILM, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- T Cloud Public (TCP) has no CDN endpoint: on 2026-10-20 neither cdn.eu-de.otc.t-systems.com nor cdn.otc.t-systems.com resolved and a CDN API call got no answer. The service description of 09.10.2026 lists no CDN service. (KB-T7LO, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-10-20)
- A DCS Redis 5.0 single-node instance with 128 MB (redis.single.xu1.tiny.128) was RUNNING 42 s after the create call. (KB-TVUA, V3 live, checked 2026-11-20)
- Oracle-optimized (OO) instances are virtual servers with an Oracle-optimized operating system and ZFS storage classes built for Oracle databases, offered in two availability zones of the EU-DE region. The service supports Oracle Enterprise Edition with the customer's own license (BYOL) only, needs a Direct Connect connection and an initial network setup in the customer tenant, and high availability is built by the customer with instances in different availability zones and database replication the customer sets up. (KB-U7RP, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- Database Security Service (DBSS) audits databases: it records user access to the database in real time, detects SQL injection attacks and high-risk operations, sends real-time alerts and produces audit and compliance reports. The documentation describes out-of-path audit of RDS databases and of databases the customer runs on Elastic Cloud Servers, among them TaurusDB, MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQL Server. DBSS is offered in eu-de (KB-22YR, KB-JM53). (KB-UUZF, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- Tag Management Service in eu-de: POST /v1.0/predefine_tags/action with action create or delete manages predefined tags (204), and POST /v1.0/resource-tags/batch-create with project_id, resources [{resource_id, resource_type vpc}] and tags put a tag on a VPC that GET /v2.0/{project_id}/vpcs/{id}/tags then lists; POST /v2.0/resources/filter answered APIGW.0101 (not published). (KB-VFZW, V3 live, checked 2026-11-20)
- Section 7.2.4 of the TCP service description, revision of 2026-10-09, points to the documentation and a switching information page to support the customer's exit strategy, and section 7.2.5 makes the customer responsible for performing the migration, exporting its content and choosing and configuring the new provider, with no liability of the operator for the success of the migration. (KB-VNL6, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- The Keystone service list of a TCP account registers a service heat of type orchestration (enabled) next to rfs, but GET /v3/endpoints lists no endpoint for heat in eu-de while rfs has https://rfs.eu-de.otc.t-systems.com/v1/; a call to the orchestration service gets no answer. (KB-WFID, V3 live, checked 2026-11-22)
- Distributed Database Middleware (DDM) is a MySQL-compatible distributed middleware service for relational databases that removes capacity and performance limits of Relational Database Service (RDS) instances for large data volumes at high access rates. The documentation names automatic database and table sharding across several data nodes, read/write splitting and scaling of compute nodes and of shards. The Regions and Endpoints page lists the DDM API endpoint in eu-de. (KB-WVTD, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- RFS in eu-de runs Terraform through an IAM agency whose trust_domain_name is op_svc_iac (op_svc_rfs answers 404 Could not find domain). The template must name the provider source opentelekomcloud/provider/opentelekomcloud (the registry name opentelekomcloud/opentelekomcloud fails with Failed to query available provider packages) and the provider block needs auth_url, else the deploy fails with auth_url must be specified; with auth_url, region and tenant_name a VPC stack reached DEPLOYMENT_COMPLETE in 21 s. (KB-XCMK, V3 live, checked 2026-11-20)
- Alert Service is an optional preview service that tracks the customer's spending on T Cloud Public (TCP) and is reached through MyWorkplace. The service description of 09.10.2026 still lists it as expected to be available from 1 October 2026, and as a preview version it is meant for testing. (KB-XKXQ, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- The public price API carries Managed Services under their own service names: the package base fee under sn=ms-base-fee (Base Basic, Standard, Advanced, ids OTC_MS_T_B/S/C) and managed CCE clusters under sn=ms-man-cce (ids OTC_MS_I_CC*), each priced per item and month. The names ms, mgs, msp and managedservices return HTTP 500. (KB-XQES, V3 live, checked 2026-10-22)

## ai (49)

- A 400 validation error is a FastAPI error list that echoes the submitted request body, so the prompt can end up in client error logs. (KB-234F, V3 live, checked 2026-09-21)
- Visual RAG runs under /v1 (files and vector stores) with text-embedding-bge-m3 for text and tsi-embedding-colqwen2-2b-v1 for page images. GET /v1/vector_stores answered on a Category 1 key. (KB-2PS3, V3 live, checked 2026-09-21)
- The Whisper prompt parameter had no effect. verbose_json returns only text and usage, so there are no timestamps, and there is no diarization. (KB-2ZQZ, V3 live, checked 2026-09-08)
- Self-service fine-tuning was removed from the docs, and every model reports allow_fine_tuning false. Fine-tuning remains an optional quoted service. (KB-356T, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-09-17)
- AI Foundation Services (AIFS) is a T-Systems product, also called LLM Hub. Its service description names it LLM-Serving-Service. (KB-3QGT, V1 docs, checked 2026-10-08)
- Category 1 metadata in the API says deployment_region "otc-germany" and deployment_country "Germany, EU", but the service description commits only to EU/EEA or an adequacy country. (KB-3VHO, V3 live, checked 2026-09-17)
- The API showed end-of-life dates (for example 2026-10-01 on two Qwen3 models) while the docs retirement page listed nothing as scheduled. Read EOL dates from the API. (KB-52IK, V3 live, checked 2026-09-17)
- The meta_data of /v2/models carries end_of_life_date, replacement_exact_model_name, model_lifecycle_stage, input_modalities, output_modalities, max_sequence_length, max_output_length, is_externally_hosted, deployment_region, deployment_country, parameter_mappings and license. is_externally_hosted tells Category 1 from Category 2. (KB-5HKP, V3 live, checked 2026-09-17)
- Evaluation models are free with a plan, run in the EU/EEA only and carry an explicit liability exclusion for production or real-data use. The list is at https://docs.llmhub.t-systems.net/plans/test. (KB-62LJ, V0 recheck due, docs, checked 2026-08-19)
- The Category 1 backend is vLLM; system_fingerprint starts with "vllm-". (KB-7PHA, V3 live, checked 2026-09-21)
- In v1.24 the service description removed the section on the chat frontend (Playground), so the Playground is no longer a contracted feature. (KB-B5UT, V1 docs, checked 2026-10-08)
- A customer who sends personal data must sign a data processing agreement (Telekom template), available on request. (KB-BVS7, V1 docs, checked 2026-06-12)
- Category 2 models in v1.24 (20): on Azure Sweden GPT-5.6 Sol, Terra and Luna, GPT-6.1 Sol and GPT-6 Luna; on Azure GPT-Image-2; Ada-Text (Azure France); on GCP Mistral Medium 3; on GCP and Azure Mistral Medium 3.5, Claude Opus 5, Opus 5.5, Sonnet 5, Sonnet 5.5, Fable 5 and Fable 5.1; on GCP Gemini 3 Flash, 3.1 Pro, 3.5 Flash, 3.8 Flash and 3 Pro Image. (KB-CN3H, V0 recheck due, docs, checked 2026-10-08)
- Category 1 models are operated by Telekom on T Cloud Public / Industrial AI Cloud. Data is processed in the EU/EEA and/or in countries with an EU adequacy decision. (KB-EZ6O, V0 recheck due, docs, checked 2026-10-08)
- A stream always ends with a usage chunk whose choices list is empty, even without stream_options.include_usage. A client that reads choices[0] on every chunk fails on it. (KB-FTZX, V3 live, checked 2026-09-21)
- Tool calling works on every Category 1 chat model except Qwen3.6-35B-A3B-FP8. response_format json_object is not honoured on GLM-5.2, Nemotron 3 Super and Qwen3.6. (KB-GGYA, V3 live, checked 2026-09-17)
- The /queue/... endpoints return a finished completion with no job id. They answer synchronously and do not work as a batch API. (KB-GMCD, V3 live, checked 2026-09-21)
- Requests and answers are by default not stored permanently and not used for training. A cache keeps processed requests for a limited time; it is on by default and the customer can switch it off. (KB-GWKA, V1 docs, checked 2026-10-08)
- Category 2 models are not operated by Telekom. Requests go directly to the third-party provider, processing can take place outside the EU/EEA, the provider can use sub-processors worldwide and the customer accepts the provider's terms and data protection rules. (KB-HC2L, V1 docs, checked 2026-10-08)
- There is no latency or time-to-first-token target; the docs say the SLA covers API availability only. (KB-J2X4, V1 docs, checked 2026-08-21)
- A wrong key returns 401 "Incorrect API key was provided". A deactivated key returns 401 "Your API Key is deactivated...". A request without an Authorization header returns 403 "Missing authentication". (KB-JLYF, V3 live, checked 2026-09-26)
- Every request authenticates with the header "Authorization: Bearer <API key>". The OpenAI SDK works when OPENAI_API_KEY and OPENAI_BASE_URL are set. (KB-JNV5, V1 docs, checked 2026-06-12)
- Whisper has a 448-token context per request. Audio longer than about 2.5 minutes came back with duplicated text or a lost final segment, so long audio must be split. (KB-JWXS, V3 live, checked 2026-09-08)
- Prefix caching matches exact token prefixes only. T Cloud models use the request fields save_cache and cache_salt; Anthropic models use cache_control. The docs call cache counters diagnostics, not a billing statement. (KB-K6QW, V1 docs, checked 2026-08-21)
- A free trial key from the self-service portal gives access to all available models, without an SLA; the provider can limit or deactivate it. (KB-L5JU, V0 recheck due, docs, checked 2026-08-25)
- Published Category 1 rate limits (Essential / Professional / Agentic): GPT-OSS-120B 400k / 1M / 2M TPM and 100 / 200 / 300 RPM; Gemma 4, Mistral Small 4, Qwen3.6 and Nemotron 3 Super 50k / 100k / 300k TPM and 50 / 100 / 150 RPM; GLM 5.2 400k / 1M / 2M TPM and 100 / 200 / 300 RPM; Whisper large-v3 218 TPM, turbo 345 TPM, 300 RPM in every plan. (KB-LT6G, V0 recheck due, docs, checked 2026-10-08)
- The API base URL is https://llm-server.llmhub.t-systems.net/v2 (path /v2, not /v1) and it is OpenAI-compatible. (KB-MAIA, V3 live, checked 2026-09-21)
- Optional services, each quoted after a feasibility check: LLMs on a dedicated instance, fine-tuning, installation in the customer's own environment, additional LLMs or embedding models, and further support. (KB-MJ5A, V0 recheck due, docs, checked 2026-06-12)
- The service description names only public internet access: HTTPS requests or the Python package openai, with the handover point at the API endpoint in the Telekom data centre. It names no VPN, Direct Connect, peering or private endpoint. (KB-NF43, V1 docs, checked 2026-10-10)
- The platform injects save_cache true on several models (GLM-5.2, GLM-5.3-Flash, Mistral Small 4, Gemma 4, gpt-oss-120b), and on GLM-5.2 also cache_salt "global". With save_cache false sent explicitly, GLM-5.2 reported no cache hit. (KB-NX4L, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-09-17)
- The docs plan pages label the GPT models "Azure Sweden", Ada-Text "Azure France" and Claude, Gemini and Mistral Medium "GCP Europe". (KB-PASA, V0 recheck due, docs, checked 2026-08-25)
- Chat completions accept n, logprobs with top_logprobs, seed, stop, the penalty parameters and a strict json_schema. (KB-PHSS, V3 live, checked 2026-09-21)
- AIFS is not a service in the T Cloud Public service description. It has its own service description and its own contract. (KB-PKO4, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-01)
- Silence in the audio produced invented, billed text (a known Whisper effect); trim silence before sending. (KB-QEGN, V3 live, checked 2026-09-08)
- Dedicated LLM Serving (single-tenant GPUs) is not sold on the Marketplace, needs a tailored offer and covers open-source models only. (KB-QMAT, V0 recheck due, docs, checked 2026-08-25)
- Since v1.22 the service description no longer says Category 1 data is "not visible to Telekom or third parties"; the v1.20 text said that. (KB-SXWH, V1 docs, checked 2026-08-21)
- The docs plans page shows a fourth plan, Enterprise (custom), that the service description does not list. (KB-T7WI, V0 recheck due, docs, checked 2026-08-21)
- Rate limits apply per contract and are shared by all keys under it. They are maximum ceilings on a best-effort basis and not part of the SLA. (KB-VA7X, V1 docs, checked 2026-10-08)
- On a Category 1 key, /v2/chat/completions, /v2/completions, /v2/audio/transcriptions, /v2/coding/chat/completions and /v2/responses (tested with gpt-oss-120b) work. (KB-VBP2, V3 live, checked 2026-09-21)
- Models hosted on T Cloud Public send no rate-limit headers; clients must count usage themselves. (KB-VCL5, V3 live, checked 2026-08-25)
- Category 1 models in v1.24 (12): Gemma 4 31B, Mistral Small 4, Qwen3.6 35B, NVIDIA Nemotron 3 Super, GLM 5.2, GLM 5.3 Flash, Qwen 3.8 27B, GPT-OSS-120B, jina-embeddings-v2-base-de, text-embedding-bge-m3, Whisper Large v3 and Whisper Large v3 Turbo. (KB-VOCY, V0 recheck due, docs, checked 2026-10-08)
- Whisper is billed by transcript tokens, all in completion_tokens, with prompt_tokens 0. Dense German speech produced about 152.6 tokens per audio minute. (KB-W2D6, V3 live, checked 2026-09-08)
- The plans Essential, Professional and Agentic can be bought on the T Cloud Public Marketplace as "LLM Serving Service (TCP Models)". The listing says it includes only models hosted on T Cloud Public; models on Azure or GCP need a separate offer. (KB-W6A3, V0 recheck due, docs, checked 2026-08-25)
- GET /v2/models lists the models of the key. GET /v2/models/{id} returns 405. (KB-WKNA, V3 live, checked 2026-09-17)
- The self-service portal https://apikey.llmhub.t-systems.net shows the keys and their models, creates and manages keys and shows token use per key and model. The customer admin gets a login and adds users, who create their own keys. (KB-WMLW, V1 docs, checked 2026-10-08)
- The docs changelog announces model and feature changes only. Contractual changes such as the SLA cut from 99.9 % to 99.5 % appear only in the service description. (KB-WTCR, V0 recheck due, docs, checked 2026-08-21)
- The SLA is 99.5 % monthly availability in the minimum-spend plans, measured only Monday to Friday 09:00 to 17:00 without German public holidays. Service credits are 5 %, 10 % or 25 % of the minimum spend. Outages of third-party LLM providers are excluded. (KB-XVXB, V0 recheck due, docs, checked 2026-10-10)
- gpt-oss-120b spends max_tokens on reasoning first. With a small limit the answer has content null, the text sits in message.reasoning and finish_reason is "length". (KB-XXO6, V3 live, checked 2026-09-21)
- Input TPM is enforced, sometimes below the published figure (Gemma 4 and Mistral Small 4 throttled at 35,000 against 50,000). Output TPM and Whisper TPM were not enforced. An unenforced limit can be switched on without notice. (KB-Z3AO, V3 live, checked 2026-09-17)

## cce (45)

- Cloud Container Engine and Cloud Container Engine Turbo (CCE, CCE Turbo) provide managed Kubernetes clusters in which the customer runs, manages and monitors containers, with the compute, storage and network resources reserved on T Cloud Public (TCP). CCE Turbo adds passthrough networking (Cloud Native 2.0) and better scheduling compared with CCE Standard; the preassembled cluster flavors hold up to 50, 200, 1,000 or 2,000 nodes, among them a high-availability flavor. The Regions and Endpoints page lists API endpoints for CCE in eu-de and eu-nl. (KB-22XD, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- A new CCE cluster comes with 17 storage classes, among them `csi-disk`, `csi-nas`, `csi-obs`, `csi-sfsturbo` and older flexvolume ones, and none of them carries the `storageclass.kubernetes.io/is-default-class` annotation. A PersistentVolumeClaim that does not name a class stays Pending. (KB-27TG, V3 live, checked 2026-09-16)
- CCE Turbo clusters (Cloud Native Network 2.0) are documented and orderable on TCP through cluster category Turbo, containerNetwork mode eni and an eniNetwork block in the create-cluster API, and add per-pod security groups and ELB passthrough. (KB-2HZA, V0 recheck due, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- GP SSD v2 (GPSSD2) is valid for CCE persistent volumes through the everest CSI driver on TCP, eu-de, and is set in the StorageClass. (KB-3DQF, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-09)
- A live price API query for cce returned only s1 and s2 cluster management items, with no line for the Turbo variant. (KB-3UFL, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-09-24)
- CCE publishes https://<cluster-uid>.cce.eu-de.otc.t-systems.com in status.endpoints as external_otc. The name resolves to the platform API gateway and serves a valid certificate even when no elastic IP is bound to the cluster API. (KB-4Z2W, V3 live, checked 2026-09-16)
- GET /api/v3/clusterupgradepaths on the CCE endpoint needs no cluster id and returns the upgrade targets per cluster version and platform version, a call the CCE API reference in the docs mirror does not list. On 2026-09-25 it offered v1.30 clusters on platform version cce.14.70 or cce.14.72 an upgrade to v1.31.14-r32, and older v1.30 platform versions only the patch v1.30.14-r72. (KB-5BJH, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- CCE nodes join a cluster with no internet egress, because bootstrap and add-ons come from internal endpoints. A pod pulling an image from a public registry then stays in ImagePullBackOff. One NAT gateway with an SNAT rule covers the whole node subnet. (KB-5IOB, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-08-18)
- The CCE Kubernetes version policy in the docs mirror (commit of 2026-08-31) lists v1.30 to v1.34 as in commercial use, with end of service in November 2026 for v1.30, June 2027 for v1.31, October 2027 for v1.32, March 2028 for v1.33 and July 2028 for v1.34. v1.29 reached end of service in June 2026. (KB-5TID, V0 recheck due, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- A CCE cluster with container_network_type overlay_l2 rejects an Ubuntu 22.04 node pool on versions v1.31 through v1.34 with 403 CCE.01403001, "Not support Ubuntu 22.04 in overlay_l2 cluster". EulerOS 2.9 and HCE OS 2.0 are accepted there, spelled that way in the API body. (KB-6O6J, V3 live, checked 2026-09-16)
- On a CCE worker the kubelet reports `nodefs` of about 10 GiB, with allocatable ephemeral-storage 9.8 GiB, even on a 50 GiB system disk. Images and container layers sit on the data disk as `imagefs`, 88 GiB on a 100 GiB volume. Everything under /var/lib/kubelet, including every emptyDir, uses the small nodefs. (KB-75DH, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-09-17)
- In a CCE cluster with the VPC or tunnel network, traffic from a pod to an external overlay relay leaves through the node, so a relay port such as UDP 9993 for ZeroTier must be allowed in the node security group only if its default allow-all outbound rule was hardened. In a CCE Turbo cluster the ENI security group governs pod egress instead. (KB-76SZ, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- GET /api/v3/projects/{project_id}/clusters returns the CCE clusters of a project and answers with an empty list when none exist. (KB-7CH3, V3 live, checked 2026-09-16)
- The Python kubernetes client method CoreV1Api.read_namespaced_pod_log with the default _preload_content=True returns a str holding the repr of the response bytes (b'...\n...'). Passing _preload_content=False and decoding resp.data gives the raw log text. (KB-AUZM, V3 live, checked 2026-09-17)
- On 2026-09-25 the CCE upgrade path API listed cluster version v1.35 (latest patch v1.35.3-r4) in eu-de and eu-nl, and most CCE add-on templates declared support for v1.35, although the version policy in the docs mirror ends at v1.34. No v1.34 platform version had v1.35 as an upgrade target yet. (KB-BB4E, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- ECS instances serving as CCE worker nodes carry the string tags CCE-Cluster-ID and CCE-Dynamic-Provisioning-Node. The CCE node field status.privateIP equals the Kubernetes node name, which joins the ECS view to the CCE view. (KB-BHHR, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-09-17)
- The node security group that CCE creates already allows the node port range 30000-32767, so the load balancer reaches a NodePort Service over the node address with the default rules. (KB-C5QV, V3 live, checked 2026-09-16)
- A managed CCE cluster can have worker nodes with no SSH access. Host-level software is then installed from inside the cluster by a privileged pod that runs in the host namespaces. (KB-CGCI, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-02-04)
- On CCE a 10 Gi PersistentVolumeClaim with the csi-disk storage class bound in about one minute. While the volume is being provisioned, exec into the pod answers "container not found". (KB-CR6R, V3 live, checked 2026-09-17)
- CCE creates the SWR pull credential default-secret in every namespace of a cluster, so a Pod or Job pulling a private image from SWR only needs imagePullSecrets: [{name: default-secret}]. (KB-E6ID, V3 live, checked 2026-09-17)
- CCE Turbo node pools accept x86 VM flavors from the c3n, c4ne, c7n, c9, x2ne, s7n, s9, m3n, m7n and m9 families plus the GPU families g7, pi5e and p5e, as the CCE API reference lists them on 2026-09-25; region availability varies. (KB-GMRI, V0 recheck due, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- On 2026-11-19 in eu-de, GET /api/v3/clusterupgradepaths on the CCE endpoint offered the latest patches v1.35.3-r4, v1.34.6-r2, v1.33.10-r2, v1.32.13-r2 and v1.31.14-r32, older than the patches the CCE patch release notes name (for example v1.35.5-r10). (KB-GNKM, V3 live, checked 2026-11-19)
- GET /api/v3/addontemplates on the CCE endpoint returned 14 add-on templates on eu-de on 2026-09-25: autoscaler, cce-hpa-controller, metrics-server, nginx-ingress, gpu-beta, volcano, coredns, everest, grafana, log-agent, npd, storage-driver, node-local-dns and cie-collector. The newest supportVersions patterns reach cluster version v1.35. (KB-I7J7, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-10-05)
- On CCE, `csi-disk` is the EVS-backed block storage class, and a 10Gi PersistentVolumeClaim naming it bound in about 5 seconds. (KB-JGA2, V3 live, checked 2026-09-16)
- While no elastic IP is bound to the CCE cluster API, the external_otc endpoint cannot be authenticated against, although it resolves and serves a valid certificate. (KB-KTT2, V3 live, checked 2026-09-16)
- Pod density on a CCE Turbo node is capped by the node flavor's supplementary network interface quota rather than by the kubelet default. Documentation examples give 128 pods on a 4xlarge class node and 256 on an 8xlarge class node. (KB-NFSU, V1 docs, checked 2026-06-12)
- The CCE add-on catalogue on eu-de returned 14 add-ons at the check, and searching that response for KEDA and for Knative matched zero entries. (KB-NHCM, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-10-14)
- Application Service Mesh (ASM) is a managed service mesh compatible with Kubernetes and Istio that manages the lifecycle and traffic of microservices without changes to the application. It provides load balancing, outlier detection and fault injection, and grayscale releases such as canary and blue-green deployment. The Regions and Endpoints page lists the ASM API endpoint in eu-de. (KB-NMSH, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- GET /api/v3/projects/{project_id}/clusters on the CCE endpoint answers HTTP 200 with a payload of kind Cluster, apiVersion v3 and an items array. The call is AK/SK signed like other platform APIs. (KB-NOWE, V4 kept live, checked 2026-07-02)
- T Cloud Public (TCP) offers managed Kubernetes in Cloud Container Engine (CCE), as CCE Standard and CCE Turbo clusters, with Kubernetes v1.30 to v1.34 in commercial use per the version policy in the documentation mirror of 2026-08-31 (KB-5TID). Clusters use ECS nodes, VPC, ELB and EVS, SFS or OBS storage, and container images come from Software Repository for Containers. (KB-OOVV, V0 recheck due, docs, checked 2026-10-11)
- Ubiquitous Cloud Native Service (UCS) is a platform that centrally manages distributed Kubernetes clusters, so cloud-native applications run across regions and clouds, on CCE and CCE Turbo clusters of T Cloud Public (TCP) as well as on Kubernetes clusters of other cloud vendors, with traffic distribution between them. The service description of 09.10.2026 still lists it as expected to be available from 1 September 2026. (KB-P3JV, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- SWR organizations are created with POST /v2/manage/namespaces and removed with DELETE /v2/manage/namespaces/{name} on the swr-api endpoint. (KB-PJME, V3 live, checked 2026-11-20)
- When concurrent emptyDir-heavy pods fill nodefs on a CCE worker, the node gets `node.kubernetes.io/disk-pressure` and further pods stay Pending with an untolerated taint. `GET /api/v1/nodes/<node>/proxy/stats/summary` can report 5 percent used while the taint is still set, because the kubelet holds the condition for its transition period. (KB-QYGN, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-09-17)
- On a standard CCE cluster a LoadBalancer ingress registers only Services of type NodePort. With a ClusterIP backend the controller logs `can not found protocol port of pod <ns>/<pod>, skip add member`, the ELB pool stays empty and requests return 502. ClusterIP backends work on a CCE Turbo cluster with a dedicated load balancer. (KB-SH6U, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-16)
- The public price API carries the CCE cluster charge under sn=cce as one hourly item per cluster flavor: cce.s1.small, cce.s1.medium and, for the HA clusters, cce.s2.small to cce.s2.xlarge, six items in eu-de on 2026-09-24. Worker nodes, EIP, NAT gateway and EVS are priced under their own service names. (KB-SJJV, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-09-24)
- A CCE cluster created without a version (cce.s1.small, VirtualMachine, vpc-router) came up as v1.35 and was Available after 242 s; a node on s3.large.2 without an os field got HCE OS 2.0 with the container runtime containerd and was Active after 121 s. (KB-SNYC, V3 live, checked 2026-11-20)
- The CCE add-on catalog read on eu-de on 2026-09-25 held no keda and no knative template, so on that date no add-on offered request driven pod scale to zero. (KB-SQ4W, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-10-05)
- On 2026-11-19 in eu-de, the newest CCE add-on templates were coredns 1.30.94, nginx-ingress 6.0.24, metrics-server 1.3.132, node-local-dns 1.7.93, npd 1.19.56, volcano 1.22.5, everest 2.5.41, cie-collector 3.12.9, grafana 1.3.5, log-agent 1.7.10 and autoscaler 1.35.8. The autoscaler is behind the 1.35.29 the add-on page names; the others match the docs. (KB-TMED, V3 live, checked 2026-11-19)
- The number of master nodes in a CCE cluster is fixed when the cluster is created, so a highly available control plane has to be chosen at creation time. (KB-UOZO, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- On CCE the number of master nodes is fixed at cluster creation. Changing the cluster scale (50, 200, 1000 nodes) leaves the master count unchanged and only moves upward, so reaching a three-master HA cluster from a single-master one means a new cluster. (KB-VXRJ, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-09)
- A stale `node.kubernetes.io/disk-pressure` taint is removed with `kubectl taint node <node> node.kubernetes.io/disk-pressure-`; Kubernetes adds it again automatically while the node disk space is still near its limit. (KB-WJEE, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- The hashicorp/kubernetes provider for a CCE cluster must be configured from state written by an earlier apply, read through terraform_remote_state plus base64decode of certificate_clusters and certificate_users from opentelekomcloud_cce_cluster_v3. Providers are configured before resources created in the same apply exist. (KB-WPTN, V3 live, checked 2026-08-18)
- The CCE version policy says the console creates clusters only of the latest two commercial versions (v1.33 and v1.34) and that v1.30, v1.31 and v1.32 clusters are created only through the API, while the create-cluster API reference says the console offers the latest three versions. (KB-XVNK, V0 recheck due, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- When a CCE cluster version reaches end of service, CCE stops creating new clusters of that version and ends technical support, vulnerability fixes, patches and the CCE SLA for clusters that still run it. (KB-Z6PU, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- GET /api/v3/projects/{project_id}/quotas on cce.eu-de.otc.t-systems.com returns the CCE quota. On a test project the read showed a limit of 5 clusters per region on that date. (KB-ZZK5, V4 kept live, checked 2026-08-18)

## gpu (45)

- On 2026-11-10 a g7v.2xlarge.8 ECS from Enterprise_Windows-Server_2022_STD_amd64_uefi_prev in eu-de came up with the same NVIDIA driver 539.72 (CUDA 12.2) as the Windows Server 2025 _prev image, the A40-8Q in WDDM mode with 8192 MiB, and with the RDP GPU policies set by user_data the RDP session desktop (dwm, explorer) ran on the GPU. (KB-27DP, V3 live, checked 2026-11-10)
- The ECS documentation says G6, G7 and G7v ECS from a public image carry the NVIDIA GRID driver and the GRID licence has to be bought and configured by the customer; live on 2026-11-10 a g7v.2xlarge.8 from the Windows Server 2022 and 2025 _prev public images showed License Status Licensed (NVIDIA RTX Virtual Workstation) with a one-day lease and no licence set up by the user (KB-RHYN, KB-B7IL). An imported customer image is not covered by that observation. (KB-2DTS, V1 docs, checked 2026-10-21)
- On 2026-09-25 the eu-de public image catalog held 57 images flagged for GPU flavors, 45 of them Linux, and only six Linux ones name an NVIDIA driver in their description: Standard_Ubuntu_22.04_GPU_NV, latest and prev, with Nvidia public driver included, and Standard_RockyLinux_9_GRID in bios and uefi variants with the GRID driver file NVIDIA-Linux-x86_64-535.288.01-grid.run included. (KB-3JIJ, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- A 700B-class open MoE model in AWQ-INT4, served with the vllm/vllm-openai container on p5e.40xlarge.12 with eight H100 NVL cards, reached about 36 tokens per second with 0.11 s time to first token end to end. (KB-3QWJ, V3 live, checked 2026-05-08)
- In eu-de the vGPU flavors g7v.2xlarge.8, g7v.4xlarge.8 and g7v.6xlarge.8 carry NVIDIA A40-8Q, A40-16Q and A40-24Q profiles with 8, 16 and 24 GB of GPU memory, 8, 16 and 24 vCPU and 64, 128 and 192 GB RAM, offered only in eu-de-01, where g7v.6xlarge.8 read sellout on 2026-10-21. (KB-3VXF, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-10-21)
- DJI Terra ships no official CLI or headless API. The backend engines DJIMapGen.exe, TerraWorkingProcess.exe, terra_3dgs.exe and DJIPicMapGen.exe are driven from an Electron GUI (DJIBrowser.exe). (KB-4A7E, V1 docs, checked 2026-06-23)
- The NVIDIA Container Toolkit install guide requires an installed NVIDIA GPU driver and a supported container engine such as Docker, then the toolkit packages, and for Docker the command nvidia-ctk runtime configure --runtime=docker, which updates /etc/docker/daemon.json so that Docker can use the NVIDIA Container Runtime, followed by a restart of the Docker daemon. (KB-4C3N, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- On an eight-GPU instance, docker rm -f followed immediately by docker run --gpus all sometimes gives the new container only seven GPUs while nvidia-smi -L on the host still lists eight, and torch aborts with device >= 0 && device < num_gpus. Restarting the docker daemon between swaps restored all eight. (KB-5GI3, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-05-07)
- vLLM 0.19.1 loading AWQ-INT4 weights through the compressed-tensors MarlinLinearKernel path repacks the layout in memory at load, about 110 seconds per 5 GB shard, single threaded per worker on cold page cache (81 shards, about 2.5 hours). Raising OMP_NUM_THREADS slowed it to about 200 seconds per shard on 80 vCPU. (KB-65QI, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-05-07)
- On 2026-10-21 in eu-de only the _prev Windows Server 2022 STD public images (uefi and bios) carried __support_kvm_gpu_a40_g7v true; the _latest ones carried false, so a g7v Windows Server 2022 host starts from the previous patch level. (KB-6Z4Y, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-10-21)
- A g7v.2xlarge.8 ECS from the Windows Server 2022 _prev public image without EIP and without NAT gateway draws its NVIDIA RTX Virtual Workstation lease without internet access: the image carries C:\Program Files\NVIDIA Corporation\vGPU Licensing\ClientConfigToken\eu-de_client_configuration_token.tok of an NLS service instance, and nvidia-smi showed Licensed with a one-day expiry about 5 minutes after the boot. (KB-7ANR, V3 live, checked 2026-11-12)
- On 2026-11-10 the price API rates of the g7v Windows flavors in eu-de (R12 and R24) matched the service description of 09.10.2026 row by row, and neither carries an R36 rate for any g7v size. (KB-AC72, V3 live, checked 2026-11-10)
- The TCP service description, revision 09.10.2026, lists only the local-disk flavors i3 and d2 and all Dedicated Host types as charged in the shutdown state, so a stopped GPU ECS such as g7v is not charged for compute. (KB-AX36, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-21)
- On 2026-11-10 a g7v.2xlarge.8 ECS from the Windows Server 2022 _prev public image in eu-de also showed License Status Licensed with a lease expiry about one day ahead (2026-11-11 12:04:39 GMT) and Product Brand NVIDIA RTX Virtual Workstation, without any licence set up by the user. (KB-B7IL, V3 live, checked 2026-11-10)
- In eu-de g7v.2xlarge.8 and g7v.4xlarge.8 run on Intel Ice Lake 3.0GHz with dedicated cores and two threads per core (4 and 8 cores), one NUMA node, resource types G7V-GPU-A40-GRID_8Q and G7V-GPU-A40-GRID_16Q, and support auto recovery and cold migration but not live migration. (KB-BKPA, V3 live, checked 2026-11-10)
- GPU flavors in eu-de come with fixed RAM, and every Windows GPU flavor cheaper than g6.10xlarge.7 on pay-as-you-go has 32 to 128 GB RAM (2026-09-25). g6.10xlarge.7 itself pairs one T4 with 280 GB RAM, and p2s.8xlarge.8 and p2v.8xlarge.8 reach 256 GB with only 32 vCPU. (KB-CQ2S, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- The p5s GPU flavors carry H100 PCIe cards, not H100 NVL, so their GPU topology differs from p5e. (KB-CSNR, V3 live, checked 2026-09-25)
- The ECS user guide says certain G-series ECSs do not support remote login from the console and advises MSTSC or an own VNC server on them, and it says a login over MSTSC makes GPU acceleration fail because MSTSC replaces the WDDM GPU driver with a non-accelerated remote desktop display driver. (KB-DC2B, V1 docs, checked 2026-11-10)
- The p5e.40xlarge.12 flavor (8x H100 NVL, 94 GB each) links its GPUs as NVLink bridge pairs without an NVSwitch fabric, so traffic between pairs goes over PCIe. nvidia-fabricmanager installs but fails to start there with 'Detected Pre-NVL5 system', leaving a broken systemd unit, so leave it out. (KB-DPSI, V3 live, checked 2026-05-07)
- The stock GPU ECS image in eu-de ships NVIDIA driver 570.211.01, which serves CUDA 12.8. Container images built against CUDA 12.8, such as vllm/vllm-openai v0.19.x, run on it without a driver change. (KB-EDXJ, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-05-06)
- The flavor g6.10xlarge.7 in eu-de provides one T4 with 16 GB (about 15 GB usable framebuffer), 40 vCPU from 20 physical Xeon Gold 6266 cores at 3.0 GHz with hyper-threading, 280 GB RAM and dedicated CPU. (KB-F3C3, V3 live, checked 2026-06-23)
- No H100 GPU flavor is available in eu-nl. On 2026-09-25 the ECS flavor list of eu-nl held no p5s or p5e flavor and no flavor with an H100 GPU, while eu-de listed four p5s and three p5e flavors, all in eu-de-01. (KB-IWD5, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- The p5s flavor carries H100 PCIe cards, a different GPU topology from the p5e H100 NVL flavors. (KB-JLBD, V3 live, checked 2026-09-25)
- p5e.40xlarge.12 provides eight H100 NVL cards of 94 GB, wired as NVLink bridge pairs with no NVSwitch fabric, so cross-pair traffic falls back to PCIe. nvidia-fabricmanager installs but its unit fails to start and leaves a broken systemd unit behind. (KB-JRCC, V3 live, checked 2026-05-07)
- Reserved terms of 12, 24 and 36 months are offered for the GPU flavor p5e.10xlarge.12 in eu-de, each also as an upfront-payment variant. The service description of 09.10.2026 adds that p5s and p5e flavors are offered only on request. (KB-KWII, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- NVIDIA: the A40-8Q, A40-16Q and A40-24Q vGPU types have 8192, 16384 and 24576 MB of frame buffer for the whole VM; all sessions of a session host share it. Q-series vGPUs need a vWS licence, one per VM. The vApps licence, which NVIDIA names for RDSH and other session-based solutions, belongs to A-series vGPUs and is counted per concurrent user. The licensing guide does not say how many session users one vWS licence on a Q-series vGPU covers. (KB-L7WQ, V1 docs, checked 2026-11-12)
- In eu-de on 2026-09-25, p2s.16xlarge.8 (eight V100 of 32 GB, 64 vCPU, 512 GB RAM, end announced for 31.12.2026) and pi5e.24xlarge.4 (six L4 of 24 GB, 96 vCPU, 384 GB RAM) are orderable and take Windows Server images, so g7.12xlarge.8 (one A40 of 48 GB) is not the largest Windows-capable GPU flavor. g6.20xlarge.7 still offers two T4, 80 vCPU and 560 GB RAM. (KB-NIV7, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- On 2026-11-10 in eu-de g7v.2xlarge.8 and g7v.4xlarge.8 read normal in eu-de-01, g7v.6xlarge.8 read sellout there, and no g7v size is offered in another zone; the flavor-wide cond:operation:status of every g6, g7 and g7v size read abandon while the zone states read normal. (KB-OTPP, V3 live, checked 2026-11-10)
- On 2026-11-10 in eu-de the gold Windows images that carry __support_kvm_gpu_a40_g7v true were only the _prev builds of Windows Server 2019, 2022 and 2025 Standard (uefi and bios); no Windows _latest build carried it. (KB-OTZ6, V3 live, checked 2026-11-10)
- DJI Terra is documented as compatible with NVIDIA graphics cards only, with a minimum of 4 GB VRAM and 32 GB RAM and a recommendation of an RTX 2070 with 64 GB RAM. (KB-QATO, V1 docs, checked 2026-06-17)
- Section 3.1.1.2 of the TCP service description (revision 09.10.2026) marks the H100 flavor families p5s (NVIDIA H100) and p5e (NVIDIA H100 NVL) as only in EU-DE, and section 6.2.1.13 shows n.a. in every EU-NL column of all p5s and p5e rows. (KB-QLMG, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- On 2026-11-10 a g7v.2xlarge.8 ECS from Enterprise_Windows-Server_2025_STD_amd64_uefi_prev in eu-de came up with NVIDIA driver 539.72 (CUDA 12.2) preinstalled, the A40-8Q in WDDM mode with 8192 MiB and the display active, and with the RDP GPU policies set by user_data the desktop processes of the RDP session (dwm, explorer) ran on the GPU as C+G. (KB-RCC2, V3 live, checked 2026-11-10)
- On 2026-11-10 a g7v.2xlarge.8 ECS from the Windows Server 2025 _prev public image in eu-de showed vGPU Software Licensed Product with License Status Licensed and an expiry about one day ahead (2026-11-11 12:09:49 GMT) without any licence set up by the user, so the image draws a vGPU licence lease on its own; this differs from KB-2DTS, which says the GRID licence has to be bought and configured by the customer. (KB-RHYN, V3 live, checked 2026-11-10)
- On a g7v.2xlarge.8 ECS with Windows Server 2025 in eu-de the console Remote Login connected (encrypted) but showed only a black screen after first boot. (KB-TFQV, V3 live, checked 2026-11-10)
- A DJI Terra LiDAR reconstruction on g6.10xlarge.7 peaked at about 127 GB working set (about 135 GB including the OS) and about 3 GB of the 15 GB framebuffer, with GPU utilisation mostly low. The RAM peak is dataset-bound and stayed the same at 1, 3 and 4 computing cores. (KB-TVQH, V3 live, checked 2026-06-23)
- On 2026-09-25 in eu-de-01 only p5s.20xlarge.12 showed the status normal, while p5s.5xlarge.12, p5s.10xlarge.12, p5s.40xlarge.12 and all three p5e flavors showed sellout. (KB-USSN, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- On the same dataset and flavor the run took 15.6 h with one computing core, 9.1 h with three and 9.1 h with four, so three cores cut about 40% and a fourth core changed nothing. About 4 h of all-core compute is fixed in every run. (KB-VECE, V3 live, checked 2026-06-23)
- A stopped G6, G7, G7v or P5e ECS is not billed for vCPU, memory, image and GPU, only for its disks, but its resources are released, so a start can fail when capacity is short. (KB-VTTM, V1 docs, checked 2026-10-21)
- The GPU flavor p5e.10xlarge.12 provides two H100 NVL cards with 94 GB memory per card, 188 GB in total, with 40 vCPU and 480 GB RAM. Storage is not part of the flavor: the system disk and any EVS disks are separate resources. (KB-VZW3, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- The ECS flavor p5e.40xlarge.12 in eu-de-01 provides eight H100 NVL cards, 752 GB of GPU memory in total. (KB-W4HV, V4 kept live, checked 2026-05-06)
- None of the 139 public images in eu-de on 2026-09-25 mentions Docker, containerd, Podman, a container runtime or the NVIDIA Container Toolkit in its name or description. (KB-WCSR, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- The ECS user guide says a GPU driver has to be installed before a GPU-accelerated ECS is used, that a P-series ECS from a Windows public image has a Tesla driver installed by default and that one from a Linux public image has none and needs the driver installed after creation. (KB-WYE4, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- pi-series GPU flavors under Windows expose the card in TCC mode, so CUDA compute runs but a GPU-rendered desktop does not. (KB-XAD4, V3 live, checked 2026-06-11)
- Five stop and start cycles of a g7v.2xlarge.8 Windows Server 2022 ECS in eu-de-01 on 2026-11-12 night all succeeded: a soft stop reached SHUTOFF in 12 s (44 s the first time), a start reached ACTIVE in 12 to 13 s, and the guest reported a licensed A40-8Q vGPU 126 to 151 s after the start call; g7v.2xlarge.8 and g7v.4xlarge.8 read normal before every start. (KB-YBTN, V3 live, checked 2026-11-12)
- On GPU passthrough instances under Windows Server, the NVIDIA R570 branch driver 573.48 works, while 580.88 and 596.36 produce Code 43. That branch tops out at CUDA 12.8. (KB-Z2DO, V3 live, checked 2026-06-11)

## evs (44)

- Elastic Volume Service (EVS) provides block storage volumes that are attached to Elastic Cloud Servers as system or data disks; up to 32 volumes can be attached to one server and a volume can be expanded online when the operating system supports it. The disk types are High I/O, GP SSD, GP SSD V2, Ultra-High I/O and Extreme SSD, and the documentation states that system and data disks support encryption, snapshots and backups. The Regions and Endpoints page lists API endpoints for EVS in eu-de and eu-nl. (KB-2DY5, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- On a Linux ECS an attached EVS data disk appears as a virtio block device such as /dev/vdb. A file system is created on the device before it is mounted. (KB-3URW, V3 live, checked 2026-05-01)
- Releasing an attached EVS volume takes two calls in two different services. Detach runs through the ECS API as DELETE /v1/{project_id}/cloudservers/{server_id}/detachvolume/{volume_id} and is asynchronous, so its job must reach SUCCESS first. Only then does DELETE /v2/{project_id}/cloudvolumes/{volume_id} on the EVS API remove the volume. (KB-4V7Z, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-05-01)
- EVS shared disks on T Cloud Public (TCP) support SCSI-3 reservations and can be attached to up to 16 servers. The attached ECS instances have to sit in an anti-affinity ECS group. (KB-4WQV, V1 docs, checked 2026-08-24)
- A 430 GB AWQ-INT4 model cold-load took 50 to 180 minutes from a GPSSD volume and about 7 minutes from an ESSD volume on the same GPU instance type. (KB-56WX, V3 live, checked 2026-05-07)
- An 80 GiB restored Windows system disk reported 0 percent lazy loading at 13 s, 6 percent at 137 s and 100 percent at 438 s after the create call, while RDP answered at about 149 s. The counter is non-monotonic near the end, only the final value is reliable. (KB-5DQ4, V3 live, checked 2026-09-02)
- An EVS data disk attached to a Windows Server 2019 or 2022 ECS supports ReFS block cloning. Backup software reported fast cloning as supported after the volume was formatted ReFS with GPT, a 64 KB cluster size and integrity streams off. (KB-6Z6K, V3 live, checked 2026-04-22)
- EVS encryption happens at the volume layer, so an encrypted data disk is partitioned, formatted and mounted inside a Linux guest exactly like an unencrypted one. (KB-7ZUP, V3 live, checked 2026-06-15)
- POST /v2.1/{project_id}/cloudvolumes creates an EVS volume asynchronously and returns a job_id. That job is polled on the EVS jobs endpoint /v1/{project_id}/jobs/{job_id} rather than the ECS jobs endpoint, and the new volume id appears as entities.volume_id once the job status is SUCCESS. (KB-CZH3, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-05-01)
- Lazy-loading progress of a restored disk is read with GET /v3/{project_id}/os-vendor-volumes/{volume_id}/internal-info. The live response carries the field lazyloading_progress, while the documentation names the field loading_progress. (KB-DLKD, V3 live, checked 2026-09-02)
- The TCP service description states that up to 32 block storage devices may be assigned per Elastic Cloud Server, while the ECS user manual states 24 at creation and up to 60 after attachment (VBD max 24, SCSI max 59, VBD plus SCSI at most 60). The two sources disagree and neither number is settled. (KB-DMN5, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-02)
- A volume create request on the EVS path /v2.1/{project_id}/cloudvolumes carries availability_zone in the volume body, for example eu-de-01, and the volume is created in that AZ. (KB-FSXW, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-05-01)
- In the EVS API of TCP the volume_type value SSD is the ultra-high I/O disk type (GPSSD is General Purpose SSD, ESSD is Extreme SSD, GPSSD2 is General Purpose SSD V2, SAS is high I/O, SATA is common I/O). In the eu-de price API its per-GB record is the one with flavor vss.ssd (OTC_EL_BLST_SSD, named EVS Ultra-High I/O); vss.gpssd and vss.essd are the records of GPSSD and ESSD. A match on the word SSD in the record names hits all three. (KB-GL4O, V1 docs, checked 2026-11-08)
- An EVS disk consumed in CCE through a dynamic PV is bound to one AZ and can be mounted by one pod at a time. (KB-GZTZ, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-16)
- Data of a disk restored from CBR loads lazily from OBS after the mount. A full read took 183 seconds for 8 GiB and 1780 seconds for 81 GiB, about 22 seconds per GiB or roughly 47 MB/s, and it applies per disk. (KB-HJKW, V3 live, checked 2026-09-02)
- On a running Linux ECS with a virtio_blk system disk (ext4 on GPT), an extended EVS system disk grows online with growpart and resize2fs, without reboot. Verified from 30 GB to 100 GB while the filesystem was mounted. (KB-HNAB, V3 live, checked 2026-06-08)
- A root EVS volume on a running ECS can be extended online. After the cloud-side extend, growpart on the root device followed by resize2fs grows a mounted ext4 partition while the instance keeps running, verified from 30 GB to 100 GB on a virtio_blk GPT disk. (KB-IP25, V3 live, checked 2026-06-08)
- EVS General Purpose SSD (GPSSD) behaved like a burst-credit volume similar to AWS gp2 in a test: fresh volumes burst above baseline, and after hours of heavy reads sustained reads fell to about 21 MB/s (small random reads, about 285 IOPS). That made loading large model weights disk-bound. (KB-JKEF, V3 live, checked 2026-05-07)
- EVS GP SSD v2 bills on three separate meters on TCP: provisioned capacity, provisioned IOPS above the 3,000 IOPS included, and provisioned throughput above the 125 MiB/s included. (KB-JN3T, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-09-09)
- GET /v2/{project_id}/os-quota-sets/{project_id} with the query usage=true on the EVS endpoint in eu-de returns limit and in_use per quota, also per volume type such as gigabytes_GPSSD. (KB-KIFA, V4 kept live, checked 2026-11-03)
- On 2026-11-20 in eu-de an EVS create with volume_type SATA in eu-de-01 was refused with HTTP 400 Invalid input received, the volume type listing SATA as sold out in eu-de-01, eu-de-02 and eu-de-03; SAS disks were created in the same zone. (KB-KPZZ, V3 live, checked 2026-11-20)
- Bringing a 10 GiB GPT data volume back on a Windows Server 2022 guest took 43 s in total: 6 s to create the volume from the CBR backup, 12 s to attach it, the rest to bring it online. The volume arrives Offline under the SAN policy OfflineShared. (KB-KQUM, V3 live, checked 2026-09-02)
- Data disks under heavy write survive a crash-consistent capture by both SDRS mirroring and CBR backup: both copies reported Filesystem state clean, mounted, and passed e2fsck -fn. The file count differs between the copies, which is expected for a point-in-time capture. (KB-KUQ7, V3 live, checked 2026-09-02)
- A CBR backup can be restored into another availability zone: POST /v2/{project_id}/cloudvolumes on the EVS endpoint accepts backup_id together with a target availability_zone, volume_type and size, and creates the disk there. (KB-LXHJ, V3 live, checked 2026-08-26)
- An EVS snapshot stays bound to its source volume and goes away with it, so a copy that has to outlive the disk is taken as an IMS system disk image or a CBR backup. (KB-MDYY, V3 live, checked 2026-07-29)
- In eu-de the EVS volume listing GET /v2/{project_id}/cloudvolumes/detail returns count and volumes, and each entry carries id, name, size, volume_type, bootable and a metadata object. The metadata carries __system__encrypted on some volumes only, with value 1 or 0, and __system__cmkid only on encrypted volumes, while the top-level encrypted field read false even on encrypted volumes (checked 2026-09-25). (KB-O5MK, V4 kept live, checked 2026-09-25)
- The size of the volume actually attached to an ECS is read from the EVS cloudvolumes/detail response, matching attachments[].server_id against the instance UUID from the metadata service at /openstack/latest/meta_data.json. A guest still enumerating the old size after a clean boot points to a volume that was not extended. (KB-P3SX, V3 live, checked 2026-06-08)
- On an ECS with virtio_blk disks, the guest picks up an extended EVS volume size automatically through a virtio config-change interrupt. /sys/class/block/vda/size shows the new size before growpart is run. (KB-RGHC, V3 live, checked 2026-06-08)
- ESSD was available in eu-de-01 when checked. The volume types offered per availability zone are listed by GET https://evs.eu-de.otc.t-systems.com/v3/{project_id}/types. (KB-SOBS, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- An EVS snapshot moves from creating to available on GET /v2/{project_id}/snapshots/{snapshot_id}. For a 40 GB GPSSD root disk that took about five seconds. (KB-SY3Y, V3 live, checked 2026-07-29)
- A virtio_blk disk picks up a new volume size through a virtio config-change interrupt once the volume was really extended. The kernel view can be read from /sys/class/block/<dev>/size or with fdisk, and it already shows the new capacity when the event arrived. (KB-T4IL, V3 live, checked 2026-06-08)
- On 2026-11-10 in eu-de the EVS volume types ESSD, GPSSD, GPSSD2, SSD and SAS listed eu-de-01, eu-de-02 and eu-de-03 with no sold-out zone, SATA was sold out in all three, and co-p1 and uh-l1 were sold out in every zone they list. (KB-TI5J, V3 live, checked 2026-11-10)
- EVS Extreme SSD reaches at most 1,000 MiB/s and 128,000 IOPS with sub-millisecond latency, but its throughput is Min(1,000, 120 + 0.5 x capacity in GiB): the full rate, which loads a 430 GB model in about 7 minutes, needs a disk of about 1,760 GiB, while a disk sized just for the model gets about 320 MiB/s. Extreme SSD also has an IOPS burst token bucket (burst limit 64,000) and General Purpose SSD is capped at 250 MiB/s. (KB-TUFK, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- Creating an EVS volume from a CBR backup takes about 6 to 7 s and barely depends on volume size, measured at 8 GiB and at 81 GiB. Volume size affects the background lazy load, not the create step. (KB-UH3H, V3 live, checked 2026-09-02)
- POST /v2/{project_id}/snapshots on EVS needs force set to true when the source volume is attached to a server; without it the request for an in-use volume is refused. The call returns HTTP 202 with a snapshot id in status creating. (KB-UQLA, V3 live, checked 2026-07-29)
- On a Linux guest, reading a restored volume end to end took 183 s for 8 GiB and 1780 s for 81 GiB while the background load was still running. (KB-V2AA, V3 live, checked 2026-09-02)
- Deleting an EVS disk deletes all data of the disk including the snapshots created from it, so an EVS snapshot does not outlive its source disk as it would in plain Cinder. An EVS snapshot is not an independent copy. (KB-W5BY, V3 live, checked 2026-07-29)
- EVS volumes are triple-replicated on the platform side, so capacity is planned at net capacity: a mirrored pair of two 3.84 TB on-premises disks corresponds to about 3.84 TB of EVS, not 7.68 TB. (KB-WKHA, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- A data disk detached with DELETE /v1/{project_id}/cloudservers/{id}/detachvolume/{volume_id} is available again after a few seconds and attaches to another ECS in the same AZ with POST .../attachvolume; an EIP moves between ECSs by PUT /v1/{project_id}/publicips/{id} with port_id null and then the port of the new server. (KB-WOC5, V3 live, checked 2026-11-20)
- After hours of heavy reads a GPSSD volume drained its burst credits and sustained reads fell to about 21 MB/s (iostat -x showed about 285 IOPS at roughly 10 ms r_await), against a baseline near 150 MB/s. An ESSD volume in the same test sustained about 1 GB/s with sub-millisecond latency. (KB-XXZV, V3 live, checked 2026-05-07)
- Deleting an EVS volume also deletes the snapshots taken from it, so a snapshot lives only as long as its source disk. An IMS image or a CBR backup survives deletion of the source instance. (KB-YENF, V3 live, checked 2026-07-29)
- An EVS data volume created from the backup child of an encrypted volume together with a destination KMS key comes up encrypted at the destination, without an OS-level copy of the data. (KB-YEPK, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- The EVS path /v3/{project_id}/cloudvolumes/detail returns HTTP 404 in TCP eu-de. The same volume detail listing is served by the v2 path. (KB-YIBL, V4 kept live, checked 2026-09-25)
- An encrypted EVS data volume can be created in the target tenant straight from the backup child of an encrypted volume together with a target KMS key, with no copy step inside the guest operating system. (KB-YPPS, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-03-04)

## api (41)

- The enterprise project quota is 100 per account, readable with GET /quotas on the EPS endpoint. (KB-263E, V3 live, checked 2026-06-03)
- In eu-de the tag calls POST /v1/{project_id}/cloudservers/{id}/tags/action, POST /v2/{project_id}/cloudvolumes/{id}/tags/action, POST /v2.0/{project_id}/vpcs/{id}/tags, POST /v2.0/{project_id}/subnets/{id}/tags and POST /v2/{project_id}/images/{id}/tags all answer 204 with the lab key of a test tenant. (KB-2KUG, V3 live, checked 2026-11-19)
- Creating a DLI elastic resource pool via API worked with max_cu equal to min_cu: 16/16 returned 200, 16/64 returned DLI.0035. The pool took about 10 minutes to reach AVAILABLE. (KB-3VTJ, V3 live, checked 2026-07-28)
- A TMS call to /v1.0/predefined-tags signed with AK/SK answered HTTP 404. TMS is a global service, and the call was not repeated with a domain scoped token, so the required auth type stays unconfirmed. (KB-47NM, V3 live, checked 2026-07-17)
- Config (RMS) answers on rms.eu-de.otc.t-systems.com. GET /v1/resource-manager/domains/{domain_id}/all-resources returns a domain-wide inventory including tags and the enterprise project id, and /all-resources/summary returns counts per provider, type and region. Both need a domain-scoped token. (KB-535D, V3 live, checked 2026-07-17)
- The canonical request ends with the SHA-256 hex digest of the request body. A request sent without a body uses the digest of the empty string in that position. (KB-66EU, V3 live, checked 2026-09-25)
- In a list query, limit=200 caps the page size rather than the number of matching records, so a full page of 200 means at least 200 records exist. (KB-6G5Y, V3 live, checked 2026-09-02)
- An existing resource is moved into an enterprise project with POST /v1.0/enterprise-projects/{id}/resources-migrate, which answers 204; id 0 moves it back to the default project. The result is visible through POST /{id}/resources/filter. (KB-6OP2, V3 live, checked 2026-06-03)
- The vendor's per-service Python SDK clients fail with region lookup errors when they are pointed at eu-de, because eu-de is missing from the region list those clients ship with. (KB-7LDG, V3 live, checked 2026-09-25)
- Quota reads on eu-de: GET /v3/{project_id}/elb/quotas, /autoscaling-api/v1/{project_id}/quotas, /v2/{project_id}/os-quota-sets/{project_id} of sfs and /v3.0/OS-QUOTA/projects/{project_id} of iam answer 200, while /v2.0/quotas of nat, /v3/{project_id}/quotas of cbr and /v2/quotas of dns answer 404 APIGW.0101. (KB-C36H, V4 kept live, checked 2026-11-04)
- On 2026-09-25 a signed POST with an empty body reached request validation, not authentication, on ECS, VPC, EVS, RDS, ELB, CBR, CES, SMN, WAF, VPCEP, VPN, NAT and IMS in eu-de: each answered its own HTTP 400 error and none answered 401. The DRS job list and the KMS key list, both POST calls, answered 200, and signed creates and deletes on CES, WAF, VPC, ELB and ECS succeeded the same day. (KB-DK5M, V3 live, checked 2026-09-25)
- The Authorization header of a signed call has the form SDK-HMAC-SHA256 Access=<access key>, SignedHeaders=<names>, Signature=<hex>. The signed header names are lowercased, sorted and joined with semicolons. (KB-DVYO, V3 live, checked 2026-09-25)
- A call to a service endpoint in another region needs that region's own project_id. Using the eu-de project_id against an eu-nl endpoint returns HTTP 403, which reads like the service being absent in that region. (KB-FOCJ, V3 live, checked 2026-08-27)
- A signed call carries an X-Sdk-Date header with the UTC timestamp in YYYYMMDDThhmmssZ form. The header name is part of the signed header list, so the signature covers the request timestamp. (KB-GLEX, V3 live, checked 2026-09-25)
- The DLI SQL check, POST /v1.0/{project_id}/jobs/check-sql, answers at once without submitting a job and already refuses unsupported DDL: on 2026-09-25 it refused CREATE TABLE ... USING ICEBERG with DLI.0007 and STORED BY with DLI.0005, so it tests what DLI supports without a queue run. (KB-HZUX, V3 live, checked 2026-09-25)
- With a read-only login a signed call answers HTTP 404 on GET /v1/{project_id}/os-keypairs of ecs, GET /v2.0/vpn/ipsec-site-connections of vpc and GET /v3/{project_id}/dcaas/direct-connects of dcaas in eu-de, so these paths do not show whether such resources exist. (KB-IEQC, V4 kept live, checked 2026-11-04)
