- In the canonical request each path segment is percent-encoded and the path ends with a slash, query parameters are sorted by key with key and value percent-encoded, and the body hash is SHA256 of the raw body, or of the empty string when there is no body. (KB-IMQR, V3 live, checked 2026-08-18)
- In the vendor SDK on huaweicloudsdkcore 3.1.186, the region lookup <Service>Region.value_of("eu-de"), whose result .with_region() normally takes, raises KeyError because eu-de is not in the region table the SDK ships. A plain string passed to .with_region() raises nothing there, and build() then fails with AttributeError. (KB-LCLP, V3 live, checked 2026-09-25)
- TCP API requests are authenticated with an AK/SK signature of type SDK-HMAC-SHA256. The algorithm needs only HMAC-SHA256 and SHA-256, so an HTTP client plus a standard crypto library can sign calls. (KB-LHNK, V3 live, checked 2026-09-23)
- The enterprise project API supports list, create, query, update and enable/disable, and has no delete operation. The default enterprise project id is 0. (KB-LYSJ, V3 live, checked 2026-06-03)
- A TCP API request signed with SDK-HMAC-SHA256 carries only three headers inside the signature: Host, X-Sdk-Date and Content-Type. The string to sign is SDK-HMAC-SHA256, the X-Sdk-Date value and the SHA256 of the canonical request, joined by newlines. (KB-M5D7, V3 live, checked 2026-08-18)
- Service APIs on T Cloud Public (TCP) accept request signing with an access key and a secret key, of type SDK-HMAC-SHA256. The project id is not part of the signature: it is a segment of most service paths, and the path is signed as part of the canonical request. (KB-MH3Y, V3 live, checked 2026-09-25)
- FunctionGraph functions are created with POST /v2/{project_id}/fgs/functions on functiongraph.eu-de.otc.t-systems.com. The code of an existing function is replaced with PUT on the same path plus /{urn}/code, runtime, memory and xrole are changed with PUT /{urn}/config, and a synchronous invoke is POST /{urn}/invocations with the event as body. (KB-MUMT, V3 live, checked 2026-07-30)
- Postman substitutes {{variable}} as plain text, so a placeholder written inside quotes reaches the API as a JSON string. For a field typed Integer or Boolean the placeholder has to stand outside the quotes, as "size": {{root_disk_size}}. (KB-NBWO, V3 live, checked 2026-08-18)
- A signature computed over a URL or body different from the one actually sent returns HTTP 401, and the response carries nothing that identifies the mismatch as the cause. (KB-NFGJ, V3 live, checked 2026-08-18)
- Timestamp formats differ per service in listings: CCE returns "2026-09-16 16:14:34.249535 +0000 UTC", RDS "2026-09-13T14:13:41+0000", EIP create_time "2026-09-02 09:10:06" and VPC "2026-04-14T13:10:40" without a zone. (KB-POCS, V3 live, checked 2026-09-17)
- The TCP API gateway distinguishes two 404 texts: "method X not found" means the path is registered with other methods only, "has not been published in the environment" means the path is unknown. /v2/zones/{id}/dnssec/enable, /rotate, /keys and /v2.1/zones/{id}/dnssec returned the second. (KB-Q4H2, V3 live, checked 2026-09-10)
- In the canonical request of an SDK-HMAC-SHA256 signature the URL path is percent-encoded segment by segment and ends with a trailing slash, and the query parameters are sorted by name. (KB-R3TW, V3 live, checked 2026-09-23)
- Setting the endpoint explicitly with .with_endpoint() and the service host under eu-de.otc.t-systems.com works for the ECS, VPC, RDS, EVS and DRS clients of the huaweicloudsdkcore-based SDK. (KB-RGV4, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-02-04)
- In otcextensions 0.32.29 the service list registers drs, vpn (Classic VPN, v2) and evpn (Enterprise VPN, v5), but their proxy modules are empty stubs without resource methods, so DRS and VPN still have to be called as raw signed REST requests. (KB-ROZI, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- GET /providers on the EPS API reports an unreliable total_count under a small limit: it returned 4 with limit=5 while the real total is 44 with limit=200. (KB-SPQQ, V3 live, checked 2026-06-03)
- With code_type "inline" FunctionGraph expects base64 of the plain source file. A base64 zip is accepted at create and fails only at invoke with "failed to find module 'index' (invalid or missing encoding declaration)", which reads like a Python error. The Python runtime also wants the coding declaration on line 1. (KB-SQNC, V3 live, checked 2026-07-30)
- The contractual availability date of a service is the date carried in the current service description revision. An API endpoint answering in a region is a separate signal from that contractual date. (KB-VEDO, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- The TMS predefined-tag API uses the URI /v1.0/predefine_tags on tms.eu-de.otc.t-systems.com; the spelling predefined-tags returned 404 APIGW.0101. POST /v1.0/predefine_tags/action with action create or delete returns HTTP 204. (KB-WICU, V3 live, checked 2026-07-17)
- The EPS v1.0 API in eu-de answers GET /v1.0/enterprise-projects with 200 and the keys enterprise_projects and total_count, POST with 201 carrying enterprise_project.id, GET and PUT on /{id} with 200, and POST /{id}/action with action enable or disable with 204. (KB-WMI3, V3 live, checked 2026-06-02)
- MRS Manager users, roles and groups can be scripted over https://<manager>:28443/web/api/v2/permission/ with HTTP basic auth. The request schemas are undocumented but readable on the node under /opt/Bigdata/om-server/apache-tomcat-*/webapps/web/WEB-INF/validate/. (KB-X6L3, V3 live, checked 2026-07-28)
- With the lab key of a TCP test tenant, read-only list calls to ECS, EVS, VPC (VPCs, subnets, security groups, ports, publicips, bandwidths, peerings, route tables, quotas), NAT v2.0 and private NAT v3, VPN v5 gateways, IMS private images, CBR vaults and the OBS bucket list all answered on 2026-11-11 without a refusal. (KB-XECK, V3 live, checked 2026-11-11)
- The string to sign joins with newlines the literal SDK-HMAC-SHA256, the X-Sdk-Date value and the SHA-256 hex digest of the canonical request. The signature is HMAC-SHA256 over that string, keyed with the secret key itself. (KB-YYWK, V3 live, checked 2026-09-23)
- GET /v1.0/{project_id}/databases returning 400 DLI.0999 'Only active cluster manager can create resource domain' means DLI is still unprovisioned, without an elastic resource pool, rather than an authentication failure. (KB-Z3SM, V3 live, checked 2026-07-28)
- In the EPS resources/filter call the field region_id is mandatory when resource_type is bucket. (KB-ZF6E, V1 docs, checked 2026-07-01)
- In eu-de POST /v2.0/{project_id}/publicips/{publicip_id}/tags with the body {"tag": {"key": ..., "value": ...}} answers 204 with no body on an unbound EIP, and GET on the same path then lists the tag. (KB-ZTIQ, V3 live, checked 2026-11-11)

## cbr (41)

- A crash-consistent restore of a Windows ECS backed up during continuous writes contained one truncated file out of 37, while a comparison run with writes stopped and the volume cache flushed before the backup contained zero damaged files out of 57. (KB-3PZR, V3 live, checked 2026-09-02)
- Creating a CBR vault with consistent_level app_consistent returns HTTP 400 with error code BackupService.6126, while crash_consistent returns 200. The API enum accepts both values. Checked on a vault with object_type server. (KB-3T3I, V3 live, checked 2026-09-02)
- CBR keeps backup copies inside its own vaults. The service overview constraints page lists download of a backup to a local PC and upload of a backup to OBS as operations the service excludes. Vault locking therefore protects only the copies held in CBR. (KB-47P2, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-16)
- Removing a protected resource from a CBR vault lowers the vault's `allocated` value on its own. One vault went from 190 GB to 140 GB after a resource was removed, with only the removal call. (KB-4HSQ, V3 live, checked 2026-09-16)
- A Linux ext4 root filesystem captured while the server was running failed to boot after restore: the clone superblock read clean with errors and ext4_orphan_get reported EFSCORRUPTED. Restored from a backup of the same server taken while stopped, it booted in 66 seconds with a clean superblock. (KB-6CUR, V3 live, checked 2026-09-02)
- A Linux server restored from an enhanced CBR backup reached service in 31 to 33 seconds independent of data volume: disk handed over in 6 to 7 seconds by Instant Restore, standby start 11 seconds, attach 12 seconds, mount 3 to 5 seconds. Instant Restore requires an enhanced backup, readable as support_lld. (KB-6DKR, V3 live, checked 2026-09-02)
- The CBR documentation describes no deduplication and no calculator for storage per backup frequency. CBR stores block-level incremental-forever backups that reference unchanged blocks, and the guide says backup sizes cannot be estimated from file sizes. (KB-6EHO, V1 docs, checked 2026-10-22)
- The units in a CBR vault payload are mixed: `billing.size` and `billing.allocated` are GB, while `billing.used` is MB. Reading `billing.used` as GB turns 155588 MB into an apparent 155 TB. (KB-6IRS, V3 live, checked 2026-09-16)
- After a CBR server restore a logical volume group has to be attached again, and the CBR FAQ covers a login password that became random after a restore or after creating a server from an image and points to the ECS password reset. (KB-73MN, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- A cold rebuild from backup to a working service measured 111 s with a Debian guest and 149 to 168 s with Windows Server 2022. The Debian figure holds only for a backup taken from a stopped machine. Different phases and disk sizes, so an order of magnitude, not a controlled comparison. (KB-7DCC, V3 live, checked 2026-09-02)
- A volume restored from a CBR backup is usable at once through Instant Restore and loads its blocks lazily in the background, so full read speed comes later. This applies to every restored data volume, not only to the system volume of a machine rebuilt from an image. (KB-BNJP, V3 live, checked 2026-09-02)
- CBR backups are created with consistent_level crash_consistent. A request for app_consistent is rejected by the service with error BackupService.6126. (KB-C2ML, V3 live, checked 2026-09-02)
- When accepting a shared CBR backup, the member_id in the accept path is the destination project id. The member UUID returned in the share response is rejected with 404 BackupService.6705. (KB-C4AO, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- Deleting a CBR vault that holds backups is asynchronous in eu-de: after DELETE /v3/{project_id}/vaults/{vault_id} the vault reported billing.status deleting, its used capacity dropped to 0 after about a minute and the vault answered 404 BackupService.6105 'Vault Not Found' after about three minutes. A vault without backups was gone at the first check. (KB-CNKG, V3 live, checked 2026-10-05)
- A pilot light switchover (start the stopped standby, create one data volume from a CBR backup, attach it and bring it online) measured 31 to 33 s with a Debian guest and 66 s with a Windows Server 2022 guest. (KB-D5LC, V3 live, checked 2026-09-02)
- Per the EVS user guide a disk created from a CBR backup is encrypted or not by a choice made at creation, while the EVS API reference says a create call without __system__encrypted gives the new disk the encryption attribute of its data source. A live restore of an encrypted volume on a test tenant in eu-de on 2026-02-04 produced a target volume with the encryption flag off. (KB-DESF, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- An EVS root volume restored from a CBR backup that was taken while the machine was running failed to boot in 3 of 3 attempts on Debian with ext4. The same path on Windows Server 2022 with NTFS booted, and chkdsk reported no errors. (KB-DKE3, V3 live, checked 2026-09-02)
- An ECS restored from a CBR backup on TCP does not carry over the metadata of the source instance: the restored instance has the default security group only, empty data volume names, and no volume tags, description or agency. (KB-DOPL, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-02-04)
- The restore point of a CBR backup sits at the start of the backup run. A source counter that moved from 220 to 412 during a 304 s backup came back at 234 on the restored disk, so RPO counts from the backup start. (KB-E435, V3 live, checked 2026-09-02)
- A CBR vault keeps its resource binding after the bound ECS is deleted: on 2026-10-05 a server vault and a disk vault in eu-de still listed the server (OS::Nova::Server) and its volume (OS::Cinder::Volume) with protect_status available, while the ECS server listing no longer returned that server. A cleanup by resource therefore has to look at the vaults as well. (KB-EBMP, V3 live, checked 2026-10-05)
- In a CBR vault, size is the configured capacity in GB, billing.allocated is the sum of the sizes of the protected resources rather than the vault capacity, and billing.used is in MB. (KB-EQOS, V3 live, checked 2026-09-17)
- A CBR backup policy accepts several execution times per day, on or off the hour. With a day-based retention rule CBR keeps only the latest backup of each day, and a backup that runs past the next execution time makes CBR skip that time. (KB-GG7D, V1 docs, checked 2026-10-22)
- CBR vault backup locking keeps automatic backups locked until their retention period ends. It is described in the documentation mirror under Cloud Backup and Recovery vault management and is not named in the service description. (KB-HVJS, V0 recheck due, docs, checked 2026-09-16)
- CBR vault backup locking keeps automatic backups undeletable by any user for the whole retention period, stays enabled once switched on, leaves manual backups deletable, and still allows cross-region replication of the locked vault. (KB-I63S, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-01)
- CBR vault creation documents crash_consistent as the consistency level value, and the CBR service overview describes CBR backups as crash-consistent. (KB-IJTE, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-02)
- T Cloud Public (TCP) runs scheduled backups with a set retention period: a Cloud Backup and Recovery (CBR) backup policy runs on chosen weekdays or every 1 to 30 days at a set time and keeps backups by count or for one month, three months, six months, one year or a custom period of 2 to 99,999 days, with optional weekly, monthly and yearly long-term rules. RDS databases are backed up automatically every day by default and kept for 1 to 732 days. (KB-ITGL, V1 docs, checked 2026-10-11)
- CBR vault backup locking keeps automatic backups locked until their retention period ends, giving a second native WORM option next to OBS Object Lock. It is documented in the CBR user guide under vault management, enabling backup locking. (KB-J2C5, V0 recheck due, docs, checked 2026-09-16)
- Restoring a CBR server backup stops the server and starts it again when the restore ends unless Start the server immediately after restoration is deselected, and the backup overwrites the current disks, by default the original ones or another disk of at least the same size. (KB-K6HK, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- In a CBR vault object the billing.size field is the capacity in GB, while billing.used is the used capacity in MB (API reference: 'Used capacity, in MB'). A disk vault of size 10 with used 3084 holds about 3 GB, not more than its capacity. (KB-LGK7, V1 docs, checked 2026-10-05)
- The TCP service description, as checked on 2026-09-16, names neither CBR backup locking nor OBS WORM. (KB-MC6B, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-09-16)
- CBR backup deletion is asynchronous: delete calls return success while the backups move to state `deleting`, and a list call issued right after still counts all of them. They drain to zero in about 60 seconds. A resource removed from a vault sits at `protect_status: removing` for the same window. (KB-MYLM, V3 live, checked 2026-09-16)
- A first CBR backup of a 10 GB SAS data disk in eu-de, started with POST /v3/{project_id}/checkpoints in a disk vault, went from waiting_protect through protecting to available in about four and a half minutes. (KB-MZ2T, V3 live, checked 2026-11-20)
- Back-to-back CBR backup share and accept API calls run into a rate limit and return HTTP 429. Delays of 3 to 5 seconds between the calls were used to stay under it. (KB-PAUD, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-02-04)
- A CBR vault created with POST /v3/{project_id}/vaults protects disks when billing.object_type is disk and the resource entries use type OS::Cinder::Volume. Whole-server protection uses the resource type OS::Nova::Server instead. (KB-PP2P, V3 live, checked 2026-08-26)
- Encryption of a volume created from a CBR backup is decided when the volume is created, since per the EVS documentation the encryption attribute of a disk cannot be changed afterwards. For an encrypted backup shared into another tenant the CBR documentation also requires the key to be shared with the recipient. (KB-Q6ES, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- A server restored from a CBR backup into another project comes up without the security group bindings, resource tags and volume names of the original. Those attributes have to be set again on the restored server. (KB-RY24, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-02-04)
- A CBR backup of a Windows Server 2022 ECS taken while the guest wrote continuously to the NTFS system disk restored to a machine that booted, reported the dirty bit as NOT Dirty, logged a healthy volume event and passed a read-only chkdsk. (KB-SPKE, V3 live, checked 2026-09-02)
- A CBR vault can be shrunk: PUT /v3/{project_id}/vaults/{vault_id} with {"vault":{"billing":{"size":300}}} returned 200, the vault reported 300 GB at once and the existing backups stayed intact. The new size has to stay above billing.used. (KB-TWYK, V3 live, checked 2026-09-17)
- Cross-tenant sharing of CBR backups is rate limited. Share calls issued in quick succession return HTTP 429, and a pause of a few seconds between calls keeps the sequence inside the limit. (KB-XXAD, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-02-04)
- Cloud Backup and Recovery (CBR) provides backup and restore for Elastic Cloud Servers, Elastic Volume Service disks and SFS Turbo file systems: the customer backs up all or selected volumes of a server, sets the execution time of automated backups and their retention period, and can replicate server backups to the other region. Backups are stored in vaults, the first backup is full and later ones are incremental, and backup policies set timing, frequency and retention. The Regions and Endpoints page lists API endpoints for CBR in eu-de and eu-nl. (KB-Y4LS, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- CBR backups are incremental after the first one. A second backup of the same 81 GiB server took 243 seconds against 501 seconds for the first, which ran at roughly 3.5 seconds per GiB plus about 220 seconds fixed. (KB-YC6H, V3 live, checked 2026-09-02)

## rds-mssql (40)

- RDS for SQL Server supports instances joined to an AD domain, and the documentation marks that setup as not recommended. (KB-4SGC, V1 docs, checked 2026-08-25)
- In a DRS backup migration the database name is matched case-sensitively against the name stored inside the backup file. The names master, model, msdb, tempdb, rdsadmin and resource are refused as a source name and as a new name. (KB-5VMM, V0 recheck due, docs, checked 2026-05-20)
- A create call for RDS for SQL Server takes datastore type SQLServer with a version string such as 2017_SE, 2019_SE, 2022_SE, 2017_EE, 2019_EE or 2022_EE, and a matching flavor_ref, for example rds.mssql.x1.large.2 for a single Standard Edition instance. (KB-5VPL, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- SQL Server on TCP runs through managed RDS for SQL Server with the licence included, through the customer's own licence on a Dedicated Host or Physical ECS (service description section 3.1.4.2), or through Managed Services, whose eu-de price list carries Custom MSSQL with Standard and Enterprise licence rows as of 2026-09-25. (KB-752O, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- In the RDS for SQL Server create call the collation is validated before the KMS key: a request with a bogus disk_encryption_id UUID returns DBS.200506 when the collation is valid and DBS.200005 when it is not, and the request is rejected before an instance is created. (KB-7IPK, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-05-22)
- Backup and restore of an RDS for SQL Server instance through SSMS is not available. Backup runs through the managed service. (KB-ATOA, V1 docs, checked 2026-08-25)
- Backup of an RDS for SQL Server instance on T Cloud Public (TCP) fails once the instance holds more than 500,000 tables. (KB-B54W, V1 docs, checked 2026-08-24)
- RDS for SQL Server on TCP offers engine versions up to SQL Server 2022. (KB-BCY3, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- DRS backup migration expects the destination engine version to match the source or be newer. An Enterprise Edition source can fail against a Standard Edition destination, depending on which Enterprise-only features the source database used. (KB-BETZ, V0 recheck due, docs, checked 2026-05-20)
- RDS for SQL Server grants no super administrator (sysadmin) permission to any user, and the documentation directs applications to connect with a lower role. (KB-DQ7I, V1 docs, checked 2026-08-25)
- A primary/standby switchover on the destination RDS instance during a DRS backup migration fails the task permanently, and the task has to be created again from the start. (KB-FP7M, V1 docs, checked 2026-05-20)
- The advertised collation listing (GET /v3/{project_id}/collations) is the instance-level list offered at create time and is narrower than the accept-list of POST /v3/{project_id}/instances. On 2026-10-14 the listing held 8 values without Latin1_General_100_CI_AS, and a SQL Server 2019 SE create with that collation was accepted and the instance became ACTIVE with it. On 2026-08-24 9 values worked against 8 advertised. (KB-I4TR, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-10-14)
- In the RDS price listing the license and storage rows carry an empty opiFlavour, so they are selected by osUnit, which holds the edition such as Standard or Enterprise, and by productCategory, which is MSSQL for SQL Server rows. (KB-IVQL, V3 live, checked 2026-06-01)
- On 2026-07-06 the RDS datastore listing for sqlserver in eu-de returned six versions: 2017_SE, 2017_EE, 2019_SE, 2019_EE, 2022_SE and 2022_EE. The newest edition in that listing was 2022. (KB-JYO7, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- Pre-verification in a DRS backup migration is on by default. It reads the backup file header from OBS and checks validity, integrity, continuity and engine version compatibility before the restore starts, taking one to two minutes. (KB-LE6N, V0 recheck due, docs, checked 2026-05-20)
- On eu-de the DRS backup migration task type is driven from the console only; the public DRS API exposes no backup_migration job type. (KB-MEQU, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-05-20)
- The volume size of an RDS for SQL Server instance has to be a multiple of 10 GB; another value fails the create call with DBS.280266. (KB-NEKR, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-05-22)
- Overwrite Data set to Yes in a DRS backup migration disables HA on the destination RDS instance for the duration of the task and restores HA when the task ends. (KB-NGJR, V0 recheck due, docs, checked 2026-05-20)
- A source database with TDE enabled has to have database-level TDE turned off before DRS backup migration accepts it. (KB-NKEB, V0 recheck due, docs, checked 2026-05-20)
- Latin1_General_100_CI_AS is accepted by the RDS for SQL Server create call although the /collations listing endpoint leaves it out of its result. (KB-OI2G, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-05-22)
- After a DRS backup migration the restored .mdf occupies its allocated size, not the used size. One restore produced a 163 GB file from 73 GB of user data, so destination storage has to cover the allocated size, around 1.5 times the backup database size. (KB-P5SX, V3 live, checked 2026-05-20)
- A native SQL Server backup file restored by DRS carries database-level content only. Logins, server permissions, linked servers, SQL Agent jobs and DB Mail stay on the source side, and non-clustered indexes arrive in an invalid state. (KB-PRHV, V1 docs, checked 2026-05-20)
- On 2026-08-24 RDS for SQL Server in eu-de offered the versions 2017, 2019 and 2022, each in Standard and Enterprise edition. (KB-QLJ5, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- In the RDS for SQL Server create call the storage type is validated before the collation. A flavour whose AZ status in eu-de-01 is other than normal returns DBS.280241 Invalid storage type and reports nothing about the collation. (KB-QPF3, V3 live, checked 2026-08-24)
- The api-ref for the SQL Server collation query gives the URI as /v3/{project_id}/collations while its own example shows /v3/{project_id}/instances/{instance_id}/collations, and its sample response values are generic filler rather than the eu-de list. (KB-RKCX, V1 docs, checked 2026-08-24)
- The RDS SQL Server collation list is read with GET /v3/{project_id}/collations, a project-scoped path with no instance id, and the values arrive under the response key charSets. (KB-RSU3, V4 kept live, checked 2026-08-24)
- RDS for SQL Server offers engine versions 2017, 2019 and 2022, each in Standard and Enterprise edition. SQL Server 2025 (engine 17.00) is not offered in the API, the console or the service description. (KB-RUHF, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- Documented RDS for SQL Server limits: database names up to 64 characters from [a-zA-Z0-9_-], at most 100 databases per instance, and backup fails above 500000 tables. (KB-RYBN, V1 docs, checked 2026-08-25)
- RDS for SQL Server accepts eight collations at create time: Chinese_PRC_CI_AS, Chinese_PRC_CI_AI, Chinese_PRC_90_CI_AI, SQL_Latin1_General_CP1_CI_AS, SQL_Latin1_General_CP850_BIN2, Cyrillic_General_CI_AS, Latin1_General_100_CI_AS_KS and Latin1_General_100_CI_AS. Any other value, including legacy Latin1_General_* variants without _100_ and other European locales, returns DBS.200005 Server failure. (KB-SCS5, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-05-22)
- DRS backup migration reads the .bak from an OBS bucket in the same region as the target RDS instance and only from the bucket root. The file picker lists .bak files only, and the file name is limited to 200 characters of letters, digits, hyphen and underscore. (KB-SDB5, V0 recheck due, docs, checked 2026-05-20)
- SQL Server flavour keys in the RDS listing follow the form db.x1.<size>.<ratio>.<mode>.mssql, where mode is single, ha or rr. In that listing the hourly rate of an ha flavour was exactly twice the rate of the matching single flavour. (KB-SVWO, V3 live, checked 2026-06-01)
- RDS for SQL Server in eu-de does not accept the instance collation Latin1_General_CI_AS_KS or German_PhoneBook_CI_AS. The create answers HTTP 500 DBS.200005 Server failure, the same answer as an invented collation, while the same body with Latin1_General_100_CI_AS is accepted. Of the _CI_AS_KS variants only Latin1_General_100_CI_AS_KS is in the advertised list. (KB-TAXN, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-10-14)
- RDS for SQL Server on T Cloud Public (TCP) allows a maximum of 100 databases per instance. (KB-TQJT, V1 docs, checked 2026-08-24)
- A DRS Backup Migration restore onto RDS for SQL Server keeps the collation of the source user database. In a 162 GiB restore the database arrived as Latin1_General_CI_AS on an instance whose server collation was Latin1_General_100_CI_AS_KS. (KB-TTWD, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-05-01)
- RDS for SQL Server in eu-de offers Latin1_General_CI_AS as an instance collation: on 2026-08-24 it was both in the collation listing and accepted by the create call. (KB-UGZC, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- DRS backup migration requires each backup file to hold exactly one database. A database whose backup is spread over several files is outside what the task accepts. (KB-W72N, V0 recheck due, docs, checked 2026-05-20)
- On 2026-08-24 the collation listing in eu-de returned 8 values: Chinese_PRC_CI_AS, Chinese_PRC_CI_AI, Chinese_PRC_90_CI_AI, Cyrillic_General_CI_AS, SQL_Latin1_General_CP1_CI_AS, SQL_Latin1_General_CP850_BIN2, Latin1_General_100_CI_AS_KS and Latin1_General_CI_AS. The same listing returned 7 on 2026-05-22, with Latin1_General_CI_AS added since. (KB-WESI, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- On RDS for SQL Server the server collation is fixed at instance creation, so tempdb, model, msdb and the system catalogs keep it while a restored user database keeps its own. Statements crossing the two, such as a join to a #temp table, fail with Msg 468. (KB-YF25, V3 live, checked 2026-05-01)
- On 2026-09-25 the eu-de storage-type listing for RDS for SQL Server offered CLOUDSSD, ESSD and GPSSD2 for Enterprise and Standard versions alike and marked ULTRAHIGH unsupported in every AZ. The create API documents CLOUDSSD, ESSD and GPSSD2 as volume types. (KB-ZHA4, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- An RDS for SQL Server instance can be stopped for up to 15 days; while stopped its VM is not billed, but EIPs, storage and backups are; it starts again in the next maintenance window after 15 days, and each start triggers a full backup. (KB-ZZJS, V1 docs, checked 2026-10-22)

## vpn (40)

- GET /v5/{project_id}/vpn-connection/{id}/log on vpn.eu-de returns the negotiation log of one Enterprise VPN connection as logs with raw_message and time, also for a read-only login, while the paths .../logs and /vpn-connections/{id}/log answer 404 APIGW.0101. (KB-223Z, V3 live, checked 2026-11-04)
- On T Cloud Public (TCP) in eu-de VPN gateways are listed with GET /v5/{project_id}/vpn-gateways, which returns them under the key vpn_gateways. Per the API reference each entry carries name, id, status, flavor, vpc_id (only for gateways attached to a VPC) and an eip1 object with ip_address and bandwidth_size. (KB-37QB, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- Deleting an Enterprise VPN gateway created with eip1 and eip2 by id also released both EIPs: a DELETE on them afterwards answered 404, so a teardown must not count on releasing them separately. (KB-5BRP, V3 live, checked 2026-11-13)
- Classic VPN reached end of life in eu-de on 2025-06-01 but stays current for eu-nl, so its rate remains listed in the current price list. (KB-6AR3, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- In TCP eu-de the Enterprise VPN API on vpn.eu-de serves gateways, customer gateways and connections (GET /v5/{project_id}/vpn-connection answers HTTP 200), and IKE and IPsec policies are part of the connection object with no list path of their own. The Classic VPN paths on vpc.eu-de for connections, IKE and IPsec policies and endpoint groups answer HTTP 404, in line with the end of life of Classic VPN in eu-de on 01.06.2025. (KB-6DLN, V4 kept live, checked 2026-09-25)
- On T Cloud Public (TCP) in eu-de customer gateways are listed with GET /v5/{project_id}/customer-gateways. The response returns them under the key customer_gateways with total_count and page_info, each entry carrying name, id, id_type and id_value; the older field ip, the peer address of the remote side, is kept for compatibility but marked deprecated. (KB-6P6T, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- Creating a VPN connection whose peer subnet matches an existing VPC subnet is blocked with VPN.0219, the following subnet appears in both VPC route and peer subnet. (KB-6UE4, V3 live, checked 2026-08-26)
- POST /v5/{project_id}/vpn-connection refuses style STATIC in upper case with SYS.0400; the enum is lower case: policy, static, bgp, policy-template. (KB-7376, V3 live, checked 2026-11-12)
- An Enterprise VPN gateway (Professional1, VPC attachment, two EIPs, eu-de-01 and eu-de-02) was ACTIVE 109 s after the create call. (KB-742L, V3 live, checked 2026-11-20)
- An active-active VPN gateway in a VPC holds a port per uplink in its access subnet, named vpn.<gateway id>.gateway_ip.1.uplink and .2.uplink, with device_owner compute:<AZ> in GET /v1/{project_id}/ports, and each of its two EIPs shows that port's private IP in GET /v1/{project_id}/publicips. (KB-7SHV, V3 live, checked 2026-11-04)
- The VPN user manual carries no MTU statement. The only MTU-related numbers in the doc set are one vendor CLI example with mtu 1400 and the VPN quota lines, maximum TCP packet length 1300 bytes and MSS below 1399. (KB-7VF7, V1 docs, checked 2026-08-26)
- A customer gateway can be recreated with the same settings in another project, as its create call takes only plain values (name, id_type, id_value, bgp_asn and tags). A VPN gateway can be recreated there with the same flavor, attachment type, local subnets and BGP ASN, but its vpc_id or er_id, connect_subnet, access_vpc_id, access_subnet_id and EIP ids must point to resources that exist in the new project. (KB-AI63, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- A VPN v5 gateway carries one connection per combination of gateway EIP and customer gateway. A second create on the same pair returned error VPN.0310, VPN connection already exists between that vgw ip and cgw id; the other EIP or another customer gateway takes the next connection. (KB-ASYH, V3 live, checked 2026-08-26)
- For IPsec to another site TCP has site-to-cloud VPN, where a VPN gateway of the VPC connects to a customer gateway through IKE and IPsec tunnels. If the customer gateway reaches the Internet through a NAT device, NAT traversal has to be enabled on it. (KB-CKVZ, V0 recheck due, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- An Enterprise VPN gateway (Professional1, public, VPC attachment, two EIPs) reached ACTIVE 123 s after create, and a static policy connection with IKEv2, AES-256, SHA2-256, DH group 14 and PFS group 14 to a strongSwan 5.9 peer on an Ubuntu 24.04 ECS with an EIP reached ACTIVE 150 s after create; the VPC route to the peer subnet was set without a manual route, and TCP 389, 445 and 80 on the peer network answered from a server in the local subnet. (KB-CSLP, V3 live, checked 2026-11-12)
- Enterprise VPN (EV) establishes site-to-site IPsec tunnels, encrypted with IKE and IPsec, between a customer network and a VPC, so the VPC communicates securely with an on-premises data center. Enterprise VPN gateways are exclusive to one tenant and support several connection modes, and a VPN connection links the cloud VPN gateway with the customer gateway device on premises. The Regions and Endpoints page lists the Enterprise VPN API endpoint in eu-de. (KB-CVCM, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- A VPN v5 connection built on the standby EIP of an active-standby gateway has to carry an HA role. Without it the create returned error VPN.0001, invalid request, connection haRole is invalid. (KB-E3CC, V3 live, checked 2026-08-26)
- Enterprise VPN Basic carries a specification ceiling of 10 connection groups. (KB-EI4V, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-08-27)
- A peer subnet that collides with a subnet already in the VPC route table is rejected while the VPN v5 connection is created, with error VPN.0219 naming the CIDR block that appears in both the VPC route and the peer subnets. (KB-EZUZ, V3 live, checked 2026-08-26)
- In a VPN Enterprise Edition v5 connection the field vgw_ip takes the resource id of the gateway EIP rather than the address itself. (KB-G773, V3 live, checked 2026-08-26)
- The Enterprise VPN connection log names the failing stage in Reason: phase1 proposal mismatch for IKE, authentication fail for the pre-shared key or identity and phase2 proposal or pfs mismatch for the child SA, so the log of each connection of a gateway shows its own stage. (KB-GOWP, V3 live, checked 2026-11-04)
- An Enterprise Edition VPN gateway on T Cloud Public (TCP) with network type Public network is bound to a group of two EIPs (active EIP and active EIP 2 in active-active mode, active and standby EIP in active/standby mode), and its interconnection subnet needs four or more assignable IP addresses. (KB-IES2, V1 docs, checked 2026-11-12)
- The contractual price row sn=vpn, named Virtual Private Cloud VPN, is Classic VPN. Classic VPN reached end of life in eu-de on 2025-06-01 and stays current for eu-nl, which is why the row is still in the live document. (KB-JJEO, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- An Enterprise VPN connection with ike_version v2 reports a failed child negotiation in its console log as IPSec tunnel negotiation fails with Reason phase2 proposal or pfs mismatch, with the status DOWN in GET /v5/{project_id}/vpn-connection, and the log shows the internal gateway address 172.16.0.17 as local address and not the EIP. (KB-JYRR, V3 live, checked 2026-11-04)
- The VPN v5 connection path is singular. POST /v5/{project_id}/vpn-connection creates the connection and PUT /v5/{project_id}/vpn-connection/{connection_id} updates peer_subnets in place. Customer gateways use the plural path /v5/{project_id}/customer-gateways and answer 201 on create. (KB-KUEP, V3 live, checked 2026-08-26)
- DELETE /v5/{project_id}/customer-gateways/{id} answers 400 when it comes right after the DELETE of the VPN connection that uses the customer gateway (seen in two runs); the same call answered 204 a few minutes later, once the connection was gone. (KB-MFC3, V3 live, checked 2026-11-21)
- When a VPN gateway on T Cloud Public (TCP) is associated with an Enterprise Router and uses BGP or a policy-based configuration, configuring customer subnets is optional. (KB-NQRJ, V1 docs, checked 2026-08-26)
- An Enterprise VPN connection in style policy (local 10.98.1.0/24, peer 10.99.0.0/16, IKEv2, AES-256, SHA2-256, DH and PFS group 14) to a strongSwan peer on an Ubuntu 24.04 ECS with an EIP was ACTIVE 165 s after the peer ECS was created when the peer's rightsubnet equalled the gateway's local subnet exactly; ping worked both ways and strongSwan showed ESP in UDP with 10.99.0.0/16 === 10.98.1.0/24. With a wider rightsubnet (10.98.0.0/16) the connection took 918 s to show ACTIVE and no ping got through. On Ubuntu 24.04 the strongswan package brings neither ipsec nor swanctl into the PATH of cloud-init runcmd at that point, yet the tunnel comes up from /etc/ipsec.conf. (KB-OANA, V3 live, checked 2026-11-21)
- An Enterprise VPN gateway holds a downlink port per instance besides each uplink port, named vpn.<gateway id>.gateway_ip.1.downlink and .2.downlink, and a VPC route to the remote subnet shows in GET /v1/{project_id}/routetables/{id} as type eni with one of these downlink port ids as nexthop. (KB-PDOW, V3 live, checked 2026-11-04)
- In eu-de GET /v5/{project_id}/vpn-connections (plural) on vpn.eu-de.otc.t-systems.com answers HTTP 404 APIGW.0101 with and without a vgw_id query parameter, and so do /v2.0/{project_id}/vpn/ipsec-site-connections and /v2.0/{project_id}/vpn/vpnservices on vpc.eu-de.otc.t-systems.com. The documented connection list is GET /v5/{project_id}/vpn-connection (singular), which answers HTTP 200. (KB-Q3BZ, V4 kept live, checked 2026-09-25)
- Enterprise VPN comes in three sizes: Basic at 100 Mbit/s, Professional 1 at 300 Mbit/s and Professional 2 at 1 Gbit/s. (KB-RNFZ, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- A VPN Enterprise Edition v5 gateway is created with POST /v5/{project_id}/vpn-gateways on the eu-de VPN endpoint. connect_subnet, the interconnection subnet, is mandatory and needs at least four free IP addresses. The gateway takes about three to five minutes to reach ACTIVE. (KB-SDV3, V3 live, checked 2026-08-26)
- An instance reached through an EIP with NAT needs no security group rule for protocol 50: IKE NAT detection payloads move the IPsec tunnel to UDP 4500 encapsulation, so ESP travels inside UDP. (KB-STJH, V3 live, checked 2026-07-29)
- For an Enterprise Edition VPN on T Cloud Public (TCP), a customer subnet cannot be part of the existing subnets of the VPC and cannot be a destination in the VPC route table. A subnet stretched over an Enterprise Switch L2 connection therefore cannot be used as a VPN customer subnet, only as a local subnet. (KB-TFJA, V1 docs, checked 2026-08-26)
- GET /v5/{project_id}/vpn/quotas counted vpn_connection used 1 while GET /v5/{project_id}/vpn-connection listed 2 connections of one active-active pair. (KB-U4C6, V3 live, checked 2026-11-04)
- Enterprise VPN on TCP is specified at 100 Mbit/s for the Basic edition with a ceiling of 10 connection groups, 300 Mbit/s for Professional 1 and 1 Gbit/s for Professional 2. (KB-VJ4C, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-09)
- T Cloud Public (TCP) connects on-premises networks by site-to-site VPN and by dedicated lines: Enterprise VPN builds IPsec tunnels between the customer network and a VPC, and Direct Connect provides a dedicated connection through a network provider with dedicated or hosted ports and an optional redundant two-port setup. Telekom also supplies the line as EthernetConnect (layer 2), IntraSelect Cloud Connect (layer 3) or Equinix Cloud Exchange as an optional service. (KB-XSZD, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- An Enterprise VPN gateway is created with network_type public or private, and eip1 and eip2 are set only for a public gateway, so a site-to-site tunnel between two private gateways in two VPCs of one tenant needs no EIP. (KB-YCZS, V1 docs, checked 2026-11-11)
- The Enterprise Edition VPN documentation of T Cloud Public (TCP) requires Dead Peer Detection and IPsec tunnel interfaces on the customer gateway, gives 1300 bytes as the default maximum length of TCP packets through a VPN gateway and recommends a TCP MSS below 1399 on the customer gateway. (KB-YIOI, V1 docs, checked 2026-11-12)
- A route delivered by a VPN Enterprise Edition v5 connection shows up in the VPC route table with type eni and the gateway port as next hop, so a route lookup for it filters on eni. (KB-YYZZ, V3 live, checked 2026-08-26)

## cts (36)

- PUT /v3/{project_id}/tracker refuses compress_type in upper case with error CTS.0227 'The compress_type must be json or gzip'; the lower-case value gzip is accepted. (KB-2QGH, V3 live, checked 2026-09-23)
- CTS in eu-de records public NAT gateway operations under service_type VPC (deleteSnatRule, deleteNatGateway, updateFloatingip, deleteEip), not under NAT: a query with service_type=NAT for the same window returned no traces. (KB-3XKX, V3 live, checked 2026-10-06)
- CTS in eu-de does not record an ELB API call that the service rejects: a DELETE of a listener answered 409 ELB.8907 left no trace, while the successful deletes around it did. A gap in the trail can therefore hide a failed call; the client output is the evidence for errors. (KB-4QFE, V3 live, checked 2026-10-06)
- CTS writes transferred trace files under CloudTraces/{region}/{yyyy}/{mm}/{dd}/system/{SERVICE}/ when is_sort_by_service is true. The file_prefix_name is not a folder: it starts the file name, as in {prefix}_CloudTrace_{region}_{timestamp}Z_{hash}. (KB-5HUW, V3 live, checked 2026-09-23)
- CTS is a control-plane audit log covering tenant API calls, console actions and IAM events. A window with no traces therefore bounds an incident to the guest operating system. (KB-7FCF, V3 live, checked 2026-07-29)
- GET /v3/{project_id}/traces on the CTS endpoint in eu-de with trace_type=system, service_type, from and to in epoch milliseconds and limit=100 returns per trace the trace_name, resource_type, resource_name, HTTP code and trace_rating. That is enough to rebuild the order and outcome of a terraform apply or destroy, call by call, after the fact. (KB-A5RP, V3 live, checked 2026-10-06)
- The CTS trace query API GET /v3/{project_id}/traces serves about seven days of history. limit maxes out at 200 per page and meta_data.marker is passed back as next to page further into the past. (KB-AB5E, V3 live, checked 2026-07-29)
- CTS system traces are read with GET /v3/{project_id}/traces, using trace_type=system, limit, from and to as epoch milliseconds, and resource_id to filter a single resource. (KB-AK3I, V3 live, checked 2026-07-31)
- An OBS setBucketPolicy call became visible in the CTS trace query about two to three minutes after it happened: the query returned nothing at first and the trace after a little over two minutes of polling every 30 seconds. (KB-FYVH, V3 live, checked 2026-09-23)
- Cloud Trace Service keeps traces queryable through its API for 7 days in eu-de. An event older than that returns no trace, and is readable later only if an authorized OBS transfer or an enabled LTS forwarding was already active when the event happened. (KB-GJKP, V3 live, checked 2026-07-31)
- A CTS event notification with operation_type customized requires an explicit operations list naming service_type, resource_type and trace_names, and then publishes matching traces to an SMN topic. (KB-GWL5, V3 live, checked 2026-07-17)
- Cloud Trace Service (CTS) is a log audit service that records the operations performed on cloud resources through the console, through API calls and by connected services, for security analysis, change tracking, compliance audit and fault finding. Traces of the last seven days are searched in the console, all traces are stored in Object Storage Service and can be downloaded as a report, and the documentation adds transfer to Log Tank Service and key-event notification through Simple Message Notification. The Regions and Endpoints page lists the CTS API endpoint in eu-de. (KB-HWFP, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- CTS traces record control-plane API actions. In a review after a suspected guest compromise the CTS record was complete for API actions and clean, while what happened inside the guest operating system was not answerable from it. (KB-JBYB, V3 live, checked 2026-07-29)
- CTS writes hash-chained digest files only for a tracker that has OBS transfer configured together with the opt-in trace-file verification switch. The digest signature is SHA256withRSA and the file hashes are MD5. (KB-KVR7, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-01)
- GET /v3/{project_id}/traces?trace_type=system on the CTS endpoint returns the operation vocabulary of a service, including operations that have no published API path. (KB-LYUM, V3 live, checked 2026-09-10)
- CTS traces can be filtered with trace_type=system plus a from and to millisecond window, and narrowed by resource_id for one resource or by service_type, for example IAM to read the full identity trail of new users, credentials, agency grants and logins. (KB-M4UN, V3 live, checked 2026-07-29)
- T Cloud Public (TCP) keeps audit logs of management actions in Cloud Trace Service (CTS): operations on the resources of the services connected to CTS, made through the console or the API, are recorded as traces, traces of the last seven days are searched in the console, and a tracker transfers traces to an OBS bucket or an LTS log stream for long-term keeping (KB-ZICC). The 7-day query window was confirmed live in eu-de (KB-GJKP); coverage differs per service, for example SDRS wrote no traces in a live test (KB-HBF5). (KB-MF3H, V1 docs, checked 2026-10-11)
- A customized CTS key event notification on OBS setBucketPolicy with the advanced filter resource_name = {bucket} delivered a mail through its SMN topic to a confirmed email subscription when that bucket's policy was written. No access policy on the SMN topic was needed for CTS in the same account. (KB-MQNL, V3 live, checked 2026-09-23)
- The mail of a CTS key event notification states times as GMT+0100 even in German summer time: a trace at 15:14:22 UTC was shown as 16:14:22 GMT+0100. The mail carries the full trace, including the user name, the account and project ids and the source IP. (KB-MV5W, V3 live, checked 2026-09-23)
- GET /v3/{project_id}/traces?trace_type=system&service_type=VPC on cts.eu-de.otc.t-systems.com returns the system traces for EIP work. The traces createEip, batchUpdateEipTags and deleteEip appeared about 20 seconds after the corresponding API call. (KB-NPWT, V3 live, checked 2026-07-17)
- A CTS trace query filters by service_type only, so record counts over a time window measure all tenant activity rather than one procedure. In one window, 61 of 167 IMS records were queryImage. (KB-NY2U, V3 live, checked 2026-09-02)
- GET /v3/{project_id}/trackers on the CTS endpoint answers HTTP 200. A tracker carries lts.is_lts_enabled, obs_info for transfer of traces to an object storage bucket and is_support_validate for trace file integrity, and these three are off by default. (KB-O4MB, V4 kept live, checked 2026-07-02)
- A CTS trace routed to SMN and then to FunctionGraph released a non-compliant EIP about four seconds after its creation in eu-de. (KB-O6VO, V3 live, checked 2026-07-17)
- POST /v3/{project_id}/notifications refuses a notification_name with hyphens with error cts.0007 'Notification name verify failed' and accepts underscores. A customized notification with an SMN topic and an advanced filter such as resource_name = {name} or resource_id = {id} with condition AND is created with status enabled and type smn. (KB-PHG6, V3 live, checked 2026-09-23)
- Setting is_obs_created true in PUT /v3/{project_id}/tracker did not create a bucket or change obs_info in eu-de, although the call answered 200. (KB-R5XP, V3 live, checked 2026-09-23)
- A CTS trace query with resource_type=security-group-rule returned zero traces for security group rule changes made minutes earlier, while older time windows in the same tenant returned complete results. (KB-RMYI, V3 live, checked 2026-07-29)
- In a chain of CTS event notification to SMN to FunctionGraph, the remediating API call was recorded in CTS about four seconds after the event that triggered it. (KB-SLEN, V3 live, checked 2026-07-17)
- With an existing bucket in the same account and is_obs_created false, PUT /v3/{project_id}/tracker switches the trace transfer to that bucket. The tracker then shows status enabled and is_authorized_bucket false, yet trace files arrived in the bucket within about a minute with no bucket policy set. (KB-TCSN, V3 live, checked 2026-09-23)
- GET /v3/{project_id}/trackers returns the system tracker with status, lts.is_lts_enabled and obs_info fields including is_authorized_bucket and is_obs_created. Those fields show whether trace archiving to OBS or LTS is really active. (KB-TU5O, V4 kept live, checked 2026-07-31)
- CTS records OBS bucket-policy changes as setBucketPolicy and deleteBucketPolicy with resource_type bucket and the bucket name as resource_name. IAM createCredential traces carry the new access key id as resource_id and do not name the user; updateUser and login traces carry the target user's id as resource_id. (KB-UWP4, V3 live, checked 2026-09-23)
- CTS shows platform-internal actors beside the tenant's own identities: an op_service account materializes resources started from the console, and any agency created in the tenant appears as an actor too. Both are normal traffic in the audit trail. (KB-V232, V3 live, checked 2026-07-29)
- In TCP eu-de on 2026-09-25 the documented CTS tracker queries GET /v3/{project_id}/trackers and GET /v1.0/{project_id}/tracker return the tracker and the trace listing GET /v3/{project_id}/traces responds normally. A GET on the singular path /v3/{project_id}/tracker, which the API reference defines only for POST and PUT, answers HTTP 404. (KB-VKNI, V4 kept live, checked 2026-09-25)
- CTS recorded a manual switchover path across services: in a seven hour window CBR wrote 19 traces, IMS 167, ECS 129 and EVS at least 200, which is the page limit rather than a count. Each record holds actor, domain, source IP, time, resource and code. (KB-W44W, V3 live, checked 2026-09-02)
- A CTS trace carries user.name, source_ip, trace_name for the action (createServer, deleteEip, createCredential), trace_rating with values normal, warning or incident, trace_type with values ApiCall, ConsoleAction or SystemAction, and code. (KB-YVOM, V3 live, checked 2026-07-29)
- CTS holds a 7 day trace window for a control plane review. Traces can be read tenant wide, filtered by resource_id for one resource, or by service_type, for example IAM, to see new users, credentials and agency grants. (KB-YXTL, V3 live, checked 2026-07-29)
- A CTS tracker can deliver traces to an OBS bucket and to an LTS log stream. (KB-ZICC, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)

## terraform (36)

- OpenTofu 1.10.3 stores remote state on OBS through the S3 backend with the skip_ flags set. init, apply and state pull complete, and the stored state objects are byte-exact across repeated applies. (KB-23WT, V3 live, checked 2026-07-17)
- With the opentelekomcloud Terraform provider, an opentelekomcloud_networking_secgroup_v2 resource applies in about one second and destroys in about eight seconds. (KB-2NKA, V3 live, checked 2026-08-18)
- On a test tenant in eu-de a stale credential in terraform.tfvars took effect over the same variable supplied as a TF_VAR_ environment variable. Terraform variable precedence is -var, then *.auto.tfvars, then terraform.tfvars, then TF_VAR_, then the declared default. (KB-2NR4, V3 live, checked 2026-08-18)
- A Terraform `count` expression that resolves to a resource attribute fails with `Invalid count argument: The "count" value depends on resource attributes that cannot be determined until apply`. The same expression is evaluated during `terraform destroy`, so the destroy is blocked as well. Counts derived from input variables are known at plan time. (KB-33BW, V3 live, checked 2026-09-16)
- The opentelekomcloud Terraform provider 1.37.1 is licensed MPL-2.0, and hashicorp/null 3.3.0 is MPL-2.0 as well. The BUSL 1.1 licence covers the Terraform engine from version 1.6.0 onward, not the providers, with a change date four years after publication. (KB-4SPW, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-09)
- Cloud Create (former Cloud Topology Designer) is an optional design tool that lets the customer design and deploy T Cloud Public (TCP) infrastructure and the applications on it; it stores designs and configuration data in T Cloud Public and is reached at designer.otc-service.com. (KB-75AL, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- Terraform reserves count, for_each, depends_on, source, version, providers, lifecycle and provider as module meta-arguments. A module variable with one of those names fails during terraform init while terraform validate passes. The resource opentelekomcloud_asm_service_mesh_v1 carries an attribute named version, so a schema-driven generator hits this. (KB-A5BQ, V3 live, checked 2026-09-16)
- The Terraform provider opentelekomcloud 1.37.9 authenticates in eu-de with AK/SK from OS_ACCESS_KEY and OS_SECRET_KEY, the project name as tenant_name and the account id in OS_DOMAIN_ID, without the account name: with those set, terraform plan got past the provider login, while a wrong access key ends in 'Incorrect IAM authentication information: ak ... not exist' (APIGW.0301). (KB-AIBN, V3 live, checked 2026-10-06)
- After the rename to T Cloud Public the Terraform provider stays opentelekomcloud/opentelekomcloud, the API hosts stay under otc.t-systems.com and the docs stay on docs.otc.t-systems.com with sources in the opentelekomcloud-docs GitHub organisation, so existing code and identifiers stay unchanged. The docs portal pages now say T Cloud Public, while many service guides still say Open Telekom Cloud (docs mirror, checked 2026-09-25). (KB-BUGT, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- The S3 backend option use_lockfile gives no mutual exclusion on OBS. A second apply started inside the first apply's lock window acquired the lock and exited 0, and the first apply then failed to release it. (KB-BZTT, V3 live, checked 2026-07-17)
- The opentelekomcloud Terraform provider covers the resources around an enterprise switch build: VPC, subnet, ECS, VPC peering and the Enterprise Edition VPN stack through opentelekomcloud_enterprise_vpn_gateway_v5, opentelekomcloud_enterprise_vpn_connection_v5 and opentelekomcloud_enterprise_vpn_customer_gateway_v5. (KB-E3R7, V1 docs, checked 2026-08-26)
- A block whose provider schema sets min_items >= 1 fails terraform validate with "Insufficient <block> blocks" when it is emitted as a dynamic block whose for_each reads an unset variable, because the count is unknown at validate time. This covers nested required blocks such as node_config then volume in opentelekomcloud_css_cluster_v1. (KB-EOI4, V3 live, checked 2026-09-16)
- In provider opentelekomcloud the resource opentelekomcloud_ecs_instance_v1 accepts power_state, so keeping a standby instance stopped and starting it is an attribute change in the configuration rather than an imperative call. (KB-G44E, V3 live, checked 2026-09-02)
- The opentelekomcloud provider exposes enterprise_project_id as a provider-level attribute in schema version 1.36, so the enterprise project can be set once per stack. It still takes effect only on the roughly 16 resources that are enterprise-project aware. (KB-GVDH, V1 docs, checked 2026-07-17)
- The provider-level attributes of the opentelekomcloud Terraform provider v1.36, as listed by terraform providers schema -json, lack a default_tags block. Tags have to be set per resource, for example through a shared tagging module or variable checked by a policy test on the plan. (KB-HY3K, V1 docs, checked 2026-07-17)
- OpenTofu 1.10.3 can keep remote state on OBS through the S3 backend with endpoint https://obs.eu-de.otc.t-systems.com and the backend's skip_* validation flags set. init, apply and state pull work and the state objects are stored byte-exact. (KB-IOPW, V3 live, checked 2026-07-17)
- The opentelekomcloud Terraform provider reads credentials from the environment variables OS_ACCESS_KEY, OS_SECRET_KEY, OS_DOMAIN_NAME, OS_TENANT_NAME and OS_AUTH_URL, so a module whose provider block declares its own variables and one that relies on the environment can run from the same shell. (KB-J2TD, V3 live, checked 2026-08-18)
- In provider opentelekomcloud the resource opentelekomcloud_ims_image_v2 rejects the argument backup_id with "Error: Unsupported argument". It builds an image from instance_id or volume_id, that is from a running instance or an existing volume. (KB-JM5L, V3 live, checked 2026-09-02)
- In a live test with skip_s3_checksum unset, the OpenTofu 1.10.3 S3 backend wrote state to OBS intact: Content-Length matched the body and the object held no aws-chunked or x-amz-checksum framing. Keep skip_s3_checksum = true against future client changes. (KB-JNE2, V3 live, checked 2026-07-17)
- In provider opentelekomcloud the resource opentelekomcloud_evs_volume_v3 accepts backup_id together with availability_zone, so a volume in another AZ can be created from a CBR backup by the resource itself with no separate API call. Checked with terraform validate against the schema, not with an apply. (KB-JQR6, V3 live, checked 2026-09-02)
- An auto-loaded terraform.tfvars overrides a TF_VAR_ environment variable of the same name, so a credential left in a tfvars file keeps being used after the environment export is changed. (KB-L375, V3 live, checked 2026-08-18)
- The opentelekomcloud Terraform provider authenticates with an access key and secret key together with domain_name, tenant_name and auth_url. (KB-L7RI, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- In the opentelekomcloud Terraform provider, enterprise projects are set through the enterprise_project_id argument on about 16 resource types, for example opentelekomcloud_vpc_secgroup_v3, where the argument is ForceNew. (KB-LVIQ, V3 live, checked 2026-06-03)
- Terraform provider opentelekomcloud version 1.37.7, published 2026-08-27, exposes 331 resources. (KB-LYTV, V3 live, checked 2026-09-02)
- The schema of the Terraform provider opentelekomcloud 1.37.9 still lists five resource types of VBS and CSBS (opentelekomcloud_vbs_backup_v2, _vbs_backup_policy_v2, _vbs_backup_share_v2, _csbs_backup_v1, _csbs_backup_policy_v1), although both services are no longer available since 01.01.2026. A resource type in the provider schema does not make a service offered. (KB-M6LM, V3 live, checked 2026-10-12)
- Provider opentelekomcloud covers cbr_vault_v3 and cbr_policy_v3, compute_volume_attach_v2 and the SDRS set sdrs_protectiongroup_v1, sdrs_protected_instance_v1, sdrs_replication_pair_v1 and sdrs_replication_attach_v1. In cbr_policy_v3 consistent_level defaults to crash_consistent, which matches what the CBR API returns. (KB-PGPD, V3 live, checked 2026-09-02)
- Terraform's indented heredoc <<- strips the smallest indentation found on any non-empty line of the body. A nested heredoc whose interpolation and terminator lines sit at column 0 sets that baseline to 0, so the whole body keeps its indentation and the shebang arrives indented. (KB-PQ4E, V3 live, checked 2026-08-18)
- State written to OBS by OpenTofu 1.10.3 without skip_s3_checksum carries no aws-chunked or x-amz-checksum framing: Content-Length equals the body and the object reads back as valid JSON. (KB-R5TS, V3 live, checked 2026-07-17)
- The opentelekomcloud Terraform provider exposes enterprise projects through the enterprise_project_id argument on about 16 resources, among them vpc_secgroup_v3, lts_group_v2, fgs_function_v2, ces_*, private_nat_*, taurusdb_*, apigw_gateway_v2, cfw_firewall_v1 and waf_cloud_instance_v1. (KB-RE2U, V1 docs, checked 2026-06-02)
- The Terraform resource opentelekomcloud_ecs_instance_v1 resizes in place on a flavor change, stop, resize and start, with no rebuild, and it ignores the image cond:image tag like the raw API does. Verified from s7n.large.4 to s9.large.2 on an untagged image. (KB-T2RV, V3 live, checked 2026-07-21)
- On 2026-10-05 the upstream Terraform issue 36704 (skip_s3_checksum not applied to GetObject, a breaking change since Terraform 1.11.2 for S3-compatible state backends) was still open. A remote state in OBS therefore still needs Terraform pinned to 1.11.1 or OpenTofu 1.10 or newer with the skip flags and use_lockfile. (KB-TN3S, V0 recheck due, docs, checked 2026-10-05)
- The opentelekomcloud Terraform provider (schema v1.36) accepts enterprise_project_id at provider level, so one enterprise project can be set once per stack instead of on every resource. It only takes effect on resources that support enterprise projects. (KB-U52F, V1 docs, checked 2026-07-17)
- Resource Formation Service (RFS) is a managed infrastructure-as-code engine that builds and manages cloud resources such as databases, VPCs, pipelines and IAM roles from templates, in batches and in a consistent way. The documentation states that RFS fully supports Terraform, both the HCL language and providers, and deploys the resources a template specifies automatically. (KB-UJSI, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- T Cloud Public (TCP) supports infrastructure as code: Resource Formation Service (RFS) runs Terraform templates (HCL and providers) as a managed service, the opentelekomcloud Terraform provider (version 1.37.7 exposes 331 resources, KB-LYTV) drives the platform from customer pipelines, and resources are provisioned and managed fully automatically through the platform APIs, the OpenStack APIs and standard OpenStack command-line tools. (KB-V42N, V1 docs, checked 2026-10-11)
- With OpenTofu 1.10.3, use_lockfile = true on an OBS S3 backend gave no mutual exclusion in a live test: a second concurrent apply took the lock and overwrote the .tflock file. Serialize applies in the pipeline or use a backend with real locking. (KB-VSJN, V3 live, checked 2026-07-17)
- The current line of the Terraform provider opentelekomcloud/opentelekomcloud for TCP is 1.37, out since 2026-07-03; the newest version on 2026-09-25 is 1.37.9. (KB-XRV5, V0 recheck due, docs, checked 2026-09-25)

## windows (36)

- Microsoft: with Djoin.exe a computer joins an Active Directory domain without contacting a domain controller during the join: djoin /provision on a domain member creates the computer account and a base64 blob (with /machineOU for the target organisational unit), and djoin /requestODJ on the new machine applies it. (KB-2BU4, V1 docs, checked 2026-11-12)
- On a TCP Windows public image a scheduled task of type once that a #ps1_sysnative user_data creates for a few minutes later never runs: Cloudbase-Init runs its LocalScripts after the user_data, and 20-set-TimeZone moves the time zone from UTC to Central European time, so the start time lies an hour in the past. A task at system start or a detached Start-Process works. (KB-2ICQ, V3 live, checked 2026-11-18)
- A restored Windows server answered RDP after 235 seconds on Server 2019 and 269 seconds on Server 2022 at first boot. A cold rebuild of the same shape took 152 seconds. (KB-2IE5, V3 live, checked 2026-09-02)
- A Windows Server 2022 g7v ECS in eu-de created with key_name of the user's own RSA key and no adminPass accepted RDP with NLA as Administrator using the password read in the console with Get Password and the private key saved as a .pem file; no password change was demanded. (KB-2ODE, V3 live, checked 2026-11-10)
- A Windows Server 2019 ECS on s7n.large.4 in eu-de (public image Enterprise_Windows-Server_2019_STD_amd64_bios_latest) shows no VM Generation ID device, and a domain controller promoted on it has no msDS-GenerationId. A copy of that DC made from a private image kept the same invocation id, so the AD virtualization safeguards do not act on TCP. (KB-3Q3A, V3 live, checked 2026-11-18)
- The attack surface reduction rule that blocks credential stealing from LSASS is documented as an equivalent of RunAsPPL LSA Protection rather than a layer added on top of it. (KB-4LLB, V1 docs, checked 2026-06-11)
- Microsoft: FSLogix containers need a storage provider that supports SMB and works with the built-in Windows SMB client (Azure page blobs only with Cloud Cache). FSLogix may be used in any data center or cloud when the user holds an eligible licence, among them an RDS CAL or Microsoft 365 E3/E5/Business. The sizing example is 10 IOPS per user in steady state and 50 IOPS at sign-in and sign-out. On TCP this means a Windows SMB file server on ECS, since SFS has no SMB (KB-7TPW). (KB-4MLV, V1 docs, checked 2026-11-12)
- The Windows Server 2022 _prev public image on a g7v ECS reported Remaining Windows rearm count 1000 and License Status Licensed in slmgr /dlv, so Sysprep for a private image is not blocked by the rearm limit the IMS guide warns about. (KB-4T4V, V3 live, checked 2026-11-12)
- Software can be installed on a Windows ECS from an ISO held in OBS: the ISO is pulled into the guest with rclone, mounted with the PowerShell cmdlet Mount-DiskImage -PassThru, installed from the mounted drive letter and released with Dismount-DiskImage. (KB-4VOL, V3 live, checked 2026-06-17)
- LiDAR360 from GreenValley uses a node-locked license key with online activation through its License Manager. The key binds to the machine, and moving it requires a return or deactivation on the old machine before activation on the new one. (KB-7DW6, V3 live, checked 2026-06-17)
- netsh interface ipv4 show config on a German Windows Server 2019 prints the DHCP line as 'DHCP aktiviert: Nein' for a static NIC and for Loopback Pseudo-Interface 1. (KB-BTN6, V3 live, checked 2026-11-20)
- Microsoft: Entra join of Windows Server is listed only for Windows Server 2019 and newer virtual machines running in Azure, so a Windows Server ECS on TCP cannot be Entra joined; it can be Entra hybrid joined through a join to the on-premises Active Directory domain. Hybrid joined devices need periodic line of sight to a domain controller. The supported device list of the hybrid join pages names Windows Server 2016, 2019 and 2022 and did not name Windows Server 2025 on 2026-11-12. (KB-CEF2, V1 docs, checked 2026-11-12)
- Over SMB from a Windows file server ECS to another ECS in the same subnet (both s7n.xlarge.2, SSD system disks), listing a tree of 20,000 files of 1 to 8 KB took 0.8 s and copying it with robocopy /MT:16 took about 15 s. (KB-D3EU, V3 live, checked 2026-11-12)
- Microsoft: Windows 10 and Windows 11 Enterprise multi-session may not be run in production outside the Azure Virtual Desktop service. Multi-session desktops on TCP therefore run on Windows Server with the Remote Desktop Session Host role. (KB-D5J7, V1 docs, checked 2026-11-12)
- On Windows Server 2025 an ECS that is reachable only over RDP cannot complete a forced first-logon password change. The server requires NLA by default and the client stops with error 0x1207, password must be changed before logging on. (KB-EDZM, V3 live, checked 2026-06-11)
- A restored Windows server brings its data disk up Offline because of the SAN policy OfflineShared. Setting san policy=OnlineAll before the full-ECS image is created fixes this once in the image instead of at each restore. (KB-F535, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-02)
- Windows Server 2022 public images on TCP activate against kms.eu-de.otc-service.com:1688 from a server without EIP or NAT gateway: SoftwareLicensingProduct showed LicenseStatus 1 about 7 minutes after create. (KB-H27T, V3 live, checked 2026-11-12)
- FSLogix profile containers work on TCP with an SMB share on a Windows Server ECS: a domain user logged on to one session host got a VHDX on the share (164 MB), and a registry setting written there was present at the next logon on a second host; NTFS rights by AD group let the user write only to its group's project folder. (KB-IGMP, V3 live, checked 2026-11-12)
- Microsoft: every user or device that connects to a Windows Server Remote Desktop Session Host needs an RDS CAL. A licensing grace period of 120 days needs no licence server. Per-user CALs cannot be tracked in a workgroup, so a workgroup session host uses per-device CALs. A Windows Server 2025 session host takes only Windows Server 2025 RDS CALs; a Windows Server 2022 session host takes 2022 or 2025 CALs. (KB-IN2Y, V1 docs, checked 2026-11-12)
- The TCP public image Enterprise_Windows-Server_2022_STD_amd64_bios_latest sets the local account lockout threshold to 10 invalid logons (net accounts), so a script that retries an SMB logon of a local user with a wrong password can lock that user out; net accounts /lockoutthreshold:0 turns the lockout off on a lab host. (KB-ML5B, V3 live, checked 2026-11-22)
- When the KMS server is unreachable at the first boot of a Windows public image, the Cloudbase-Init local script 50-set-KMShost.ps1 ends with exit code 1002 (run again at the next boot) and leaves KeyManagementServiceName unset; slmgr /ato then fails with 0x8007232B until slmgr /skms kms.eu-de.otc-service.com:1688 is set or the server boots again with KMS reachable. (KB-MQWD, V3 live, checked 2026-11-13)
- A Windows Server 2025 ECS (public image _prev build, eu-de) created with adminPass and a #ps1_sysnative user_data that runs net user Administrator /logonpasswordchg:no still demanded a password change at the first RDP logon, so that user_data line does not clear the flag Cloudbase-Init sets with the password. (KB-O2MW, V3 live, checked 2026-11-10)
- Client Windows images such as Windows 10 and Windows 7 may only be used to create ECS on a Dedicated Host. The service description additionally limits bring-your-own Microsoft Windows and SQL Server licences to Physical and Dedicated Hosts. (KB-O4J5, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-06-10)
- A Windows Server 2022 ECS whose security group allows egress only to TCP 80 and 443, DNS and TCP 1688 to 100.125.0.0/16 is neither activated nor time-synced: kms.eu-de.otc-service.com resolves to 198.19.58.127 outside 100.125.0.0/16, and w32tm falls back to the Local CMOS Clock because UDP 123 to ntp01 and ntp02.eu-de.otc-service.com (100.125.x) is closed. Egress TCP 1688 to 198.19.58.127 and UDP 123 to 100.125.0.0/16 fixed both. (KB-OD4P, V3 live, checked 2026-11-13)
- On Windows Server 2025 ECS in eu-de created with adminPass, neither a first-boot user_data command nor a delayed startup task from user_data removed the demand to change the password at first logon, so RDP with NLA kept failing with 0x1207. (KB-OE2P, V3 live, checked 2026-11-10)
- On a German Windows Server 2019 (de-de evaluation ISO 17763.3650, UI de-DE) the guide blocks for the VirtIO boot start run unchanged: Get-NetIPInterface shows Dhcp Disabled, a scheduled task with -User SYSTEM writes the DriverDatabase entries (LastTaskResult 0), and the registry check prints 0, 0, 7 times True, 48 and viostor as on English Windows; only sc.exe answers in German (ERFOLG, FEHLER 1060). (KB-PDWJ, V3 live, checked 2026-11-20)
- Two copies of one Windows Server 2019 domain member running at the same time on TCP (same name, other address) both keep a working secure channel until one of them changes the machine password. After the change, the copy that still holds the old password keeps its open channel only until it restarts; after the restart Test-ComputerSecureChannel is False and nltest /sc_query answers ERROR_ACCESS_DENIED. Run as SYSTEM from a startup task, nltest /sc_change_pwd changed the password while the channel worked, and Reset-ComputerMachinePassword answered access denied. (KB-QEO2, V3 live, checked 2026-11-18)
- A Windows Server 2022 ECS on s7n.xlarge.2 (BIOS image) exposes COM1 as the serial console: Cloudbase-Init logs there and lines a PowerShell script writes to COM1 with System.IO.Ports.SerialPort at 115200 appear in os-getConsoleOutput within a minute, which gives a result channel from a guest without network access to the session. (KB-SU4Y, V3 live, checked 2026-11-12)
- A g7v.2xlarge.8 ECS from the Windows Server 2022 _prev public image with key_name in eu-de-01 (launched 11:38:34 UTC) had its encrypted Administrator password at the os-server-password call when read at 11:50 UTC, so Cloudbase-Init posts the password on that image as well. (KB-U2BF, V3 live, checked 2026-11-10)
- On a fresh Windows Server 2022 ECS a scheduled task that ran Install-WindowsFeature AD-Domain-Services about 3 minutes after create, while Cloudbase-Init was still running, returned in 3 s without installing (state Available, no error shown); the same call about 10 minutes after create installed the role in 295 s, so role installs in first-boot automation need a check of InstallState and a retry. (KB-WPVI, V3 live, checked 2026-11-12)
- Windows Server 2022 and Windows Server 2025 are offered as public images and run on the pi5e GPU family with NVIDIA L4. (KB-X52S, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- A g7v.2xlarge.8 ECS from the Windows Server 2025 _prev public image with key_name in eu-de-01 had its encrypted Administrator password available at the os-server-password call about 6 minutes after launch (launched 11:33:57, first non-empty answer 11:39:43 UTC); the answers before were empty. (KB-XRKJ, V3 live, checked 2026-11-10)
- A Windows Server 2022 ECS without EIP joins an Active Directory domain whose controller is another ECS in the same subnet by offline domain join: djoin /provision on the controller, the blob fetched over the subnet, djoin /requestODJ /localos and one restart; the host then showed PartOfDomain True, a working secure channel and AES-256 Kerberos tickets, about 2 minutes from blob to restart. (KB-YOMD, V3 live, checked 2026-11-12)
- Two Windows Server 2019 domain controllers of one domain, prepared on Hyper-V Generation 2 by the VirtIO steps of the guide (driver package, boot start, DriverDatabase entries, DHCP) and exported one after the other, run on TCP (s7n.large.4, UEFI images) with their old addresses as DHCP ports. Each copy keeps its invocation id and shows no event 2095. The copy exported first and started alone fails replication with 8524 until the second arrives; then both replicate all five naming contexts in both directions without failures, the first copy receives the user made on the second after its export, users made on TCP replicate both ways, and a soft reboot keeps it clean. (KB-YQS7, V3 live, checked 2026-11-19)
- Microsoft: the Entra private network connector for Entra Private Access runs on Windows Server 2016 or later, can run on a VM in any cloud that reaches the resources, opens only outbound connections on ports 80 and 443 to the Microsoft service and needs no inbound port. Microsoft recommends at least two connectors per connector group. So on TCP a connector ECS needs outbound Internet (NAT gateway or EIP) and no inbound security group rule from the Internet. (KB-ZX2B, V1 docs, checked 2026-11-12)
- Microsoft: for the Entra hybrid join the organisational unit of the computer objects must be in the sync scope of Microsoft Entra Connect and each device needs HTTPS access to three Microsoft addresses for device registration and sign-in (a fourth for seamless SSO). A session host on TCP without EIP therefore needs a NAT gateway path for the hybrid join, while the domain join itself needs none. (KB-ZZGH, V1 docs, checked 2026-11-21)

## ces (33)

- In eu-de on 2026-09-25 the 4-hour and 24-hour rollups (period 14400 and 86400) reached back 235 days to the creation of the oldest measured resource, beyond the 155 days of the 1-hour rollups. The docs give no retention for these two periods, so whether Cloud Eye keeps a full year could not be proven. (KB-2FSY, V3 live, checked 2026-09-25)
- On T Cloud Public (TCP) in eu-de a CES resource group is created with POST /V1.0/{project_id}/resource-groups. The body carries group_name and a resources array, where each entry names a namespace such as SYS.ECS and a dimensions list with a name like instance_id and the resource id as value. (KB-3GQL, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- On T Cloud Public (TCP) in eu-de the CES v2 call for a single resource group returned only the group metadata, without the resources list that the V1.0 path returned for the same group. (KB-4475, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- GET V1.0/{project_id}/resource-groups/{group_id} returns the group together with its full resources array. The v2 path for the same group returns only the group metadata. (KB-4Y5B, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- On T Cloud Public (TCP) in eu-de the same resource group create request sent to the CES v2 path returned HTTP 400, while the V1.0 path accepted it. (KB-54E2, V3 live, checked 2026-09-25)
- CES paths written with a lowercase v1 version segment return HTTP 404 in TCP eu-de. The version segment that resolves is the capital-V form V1.0. (KB-5OSP, V4 kept live, checked 2026-09-25)
- A CES resource group is created with POST /V1.0/{project_id}/resource-groups, which answered HTTP 201 on 2026-09-25. The v2 resource-groups path takes its own body (group_name, type, tags) and answered HTTP 200 the same day, while a V1.0-shaped body sent to v2 is refused with HTTP 400 ces.0014 "Some content in message body is not correct". (KB-6AI5, V3 live, checked 2026-09-25)
- On T Cloud Public (TCP) in eu-de the members of a CES resource group are read with GET /V1.0/{project_id}/resource-groups/{group_id}. The response returns them under the key resources, each entry carrying namespace, dimensions as name and value pairs, and status. (KB-6HWY, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- A CES alarm rule references its resources by ID and its notification target by SMN topic, so the resource group members and the SMN topic exist before the alarm rule is created. CES dimensions carry resource IDs, so ECS and RDS names are resolved to IDs first. (KB-74D3, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- Pushed data is read back with GET /V1.0/{project_id}/metric-data and the parameters namespace, metric_name, dim.0 to dim.2, from, to, period (1, 300, 1200, 3600, 14400 or 86400) and filter. On 2026-09-25 this GET answered HTTP 200 with datapoints on ces.eu-de.otc.t-systems.com for the system metric SYS.ECS cpu_util. (KB-BUCK, V3 live, checked 2026-09-25)
- In TCP eu-de on 2026-09-25 GET /v2/{project_id}/alarms lists the CES alarms of a project under alarms with a count, each with name, type, namespace, policies and resources. GET /V1.0/{project_id}/alarms lists the same alarms under metric_alarms. (KB-CBMO, V3 live, checked 2026-09-25)
