- The CES metrics list path is case sensitive. Written with capital V1.0 it returns the metric list, written as v1 the API gateway answers HTTP 404. (KB-E3AA, V4 kept live, checked 2026-09-25)
- On T Cloud Public (TCP) in eu-de, CES creates an alarm with POST /V1.0/{project_id}/alarms on ces.eu-de.otc.t-systems.com, the version segment written with an uppercase V. The body carries metric (namespace such as SYS.ECS, dimensions, metric_name), condition, alarm_type and alarm_enabled. (KB-F3UI, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- An application adds its own metric data with POST /V1.0/{project_id}/metric-data on the Cloud Eye endpoint: the body is an array of entries, each with metric (namespace, metric_name and up to three dimensions), ttl of 1 to 604,800 seconds, collect_time in UNIX milliseconds and value, plus optional unit and type. The call answers 201 without a body, while each data point is deleted once its ttl expires. (KB-FEHS, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- Cloud Eye (CES) monitors the status of cloud resources in real time and raises alarms: monitoring starts automatically for resources such as Elastic Cloud Servers, the customer sets alarm rules on metrics and views them on dashboards. The documentation adds an agent for Elastic Cloud Servers that collects about 40 metrics such as CPU, memory and disk usage at 60-second granularity, and alarm notifications by email, SMS or HTTP/HTTPS. The Regions and Endpoints page lists API endpoints for Cloud Eye in eu-de and eu-nl. (KB-FK5P, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- In eu-de on 2026-09-25 the Cloud Eye metric-data API returned raw data back to 2 days, 5-minute rollups back to 10 days, 20-minute rollups back to 20 days and 1-hour rollups back to 155 days, so the 5-minute rollups lived longer than the documented 6 days. (KB-K6SW, V3 live, checked 2026-09-25)
- A metric-data POST may not exceed 512 KB and every collect_time must lie between three days before and ten minutes after the current time, with 20 seconds of margin at each end. Data should be sent more often than the aggregation period, otherwise the rollups get gaps. (KB-KUTY, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- T Cloud Public (TCP) monitors resources and raises alarms with Cloud Eye (CES): metrics of cloud services are collected automatically, an agent adds operating system metrics for servers, alarm rules on thresholds trigger notifications through Simple Message Notification topics by email, SMS or HTTP/HTTPS, and dashboards show the data. Application-level monitoring comes from Application Operations Management and Application Performance Management. (KB-L4FW, V1 docs, checked 2026-10-11)
- A CES metric listing in eu-de on 2026-09-25 returned about 1520 metrics, the count moving slightly between reads, in the namespaces SYS.ECS, SYS.ELB, SYS.EVS, SYS.FunctionGraph, SYS.NAT, SYS.OBS, SYS.RDS and SYS.VPC. No SYS.CCE namespace was among them. (KB-M42H, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- In a CES alarm body, alarm_type RESOURCE_GROUP targets a resource group and its dimensions carry names without values, MULTI_INSTANCE targets named instances and its dimensions carry name and value, EVENT.SYS marks a system event alarm whose dimensions can be empty. (KB-MLR6, V3 live, checked 2026-09-25)
- The Cloud Eye docs give retention periods of two days for raw metric data, 6 days for 5-minute rollups, 20 days for 20-minute rollups and 155 days for 1-hour rollups. The console lets you pick a custom time range of up to six months. (KB-OSCN, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- Cloud Eye can dump cloud service monitoring data in real time to a DMS for Kafka topic, where it is read on the Kafka console or with an open-source Kafka client. An account can create at most 20 dump tasks and Kafka is the only destination type. (KB-P2MX, V0 recheck due, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- Custom metrics show up in the Cloud Eye console under Custom Monitoring once data has been added through the API. Alarm rules can be created on them from that page. (KB-QDU7, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- A CES alarm carries its notification targets in alarm_actions and ok_actions, entries of type notification whose notificationList holds an SMN topic URN. The V1.0 body also sets alarm_action_enabled; a notification time window is documented only in the v2 alarm API, as notification_begin_time and notification_end_time. (KB-REPW, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- In TCP eu-de the CES v2 metric list and the v2 single alarm GET return HTTP 404, while V1.0 serves both. Resource group creation works on both paths, each with its own body (KB-6AI5). (KB-SNTC, V3 live, checked 2026-09-25)
- CES metric listing runs on the V1.0 path: GET /V1.0/{project_id}/metrics with limit 1000 answered HTTP 200 and returns meta_data.total. CES alarm calls use the same V1.0 path. (KB-TMYE, V4 kept live, checked 2026-07-02)
- A CES alarm is bound to a resource group through the field metric.resource_group_id in the alarm body. (KB-TO6W, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- GET /V1.0/{project_id}/resource-groups/{group_id} returns the members of a CES resource group in a resources array, each entry carrying the metric namespace and the dimensions of the resource. (KB-UI56, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- The CES metric list is returned by GET /V1.0/{project_id}/metrics with a capital V in the version segment. The same path written with a lowercase v returns HTTP 404. (KB-VFOS, V4 kept live, checked 2026-09-25)
- On a test tenant in T Cloud Public (TCP) eu-de on 2026-09-25 the v2 and V1.0 CES alarm lists returned the same two alarm ids. (KB-W3FA, V3 live, checked 2026-09-25)
- On T Cloud Public (TCP) in eu-de CES alarms are listed with GET /V1.0/{project_id}/alarms, where the version segment is the literal string V1.0. The response returns metric_alarms plus a meta_data object with total and marker, and the marker value is sent back as the start parameter to fetch the next page. (KB-X4W2, V3 live, checked 2026-09-25)
- POST /v2/{project_id}/alarm-templates creates a custom CES alarm template, HTTP 201 on 2026-09-25. Its batch delete needs delete_associate_alarm in the body next to template_ids: without it the call answers HTTP 400 ces.0014. (KB-XQ7F, V3 live, checked 2026-09-25)
- CES alarm rules are created with POST /V1.0/{project_id}/alarms and listed with GET /V1.0/{project_id}/alarms. In eu-de on 2026-09-25 GET /v2/{project_id}/alarms listed the same alarms as the V1.0 path. (KB-YCXE, V3 live, checked 2026-09-25)

## network (32)

- The Meraki vMX 26.1.4 KVM image does not ask a metadata service at 169.254.169.254 for user data: under QEMU with a network it sent no request in 15 minutes, while a control guest did. (KB-3H4S, V3 live, checked 2026-10-01)
- With the MTU raised on one side only, a path MTU bisect returned 1276 bytes, which is not a tunnel limit. It is the far end veth still at 1300 plus the bridge allowance of mtu plus hard_header_len plus VLAN_HLEN, 18 bytes. (KB-3UAP, V3 live, checked 2026-08-26)
- In eu-de the subnets of a just deleted ECS (two NICs) were deleted with 204 about 2 s after the ECS delete job reported SUCCESS and its EIP was released; no wait for the ports was needed. (KB-3VRG, V3 live, checked 2026-11-11)
- GET /v1/{project_id}/cloudservers/{server_id} in eu-de lists a security group once per NIC: an ECS with two NICs in the same group returned that group twice in server.security_groups. Deduplicate the names before counting the groups of a server. (KB-4SOA, V3 live, checked 2026-11-11)
- From a Windows ECS behind a public NAT gateway with egress limited to TCP 80 and 443, the Microsoft Entra sign-in endpoint on 443 answered, SMTP 587 and TCP 53 to the internet were refused, the public source address was the NAT EIP, and the Microsoft Entra private network connector installer (21 MB, version 1.5.5130.0, valid Microsoft signature) downloaded from the Microsoft download host in 13 s. (KB-6S2N, V3 live, checked 2026-11-13)
- Ubuntu 24.04 ECS without an EIP, in a subnet with an SNAT rule of a public NAT gateway, reached https://www.debian.org with HTTP 200 during cloud-init. (KB-6WXR, V3 live, checked 2026-11-20)
- A small public NAT gateway (spec 1) created with POST /v2.0/nat_gateways in a new VPC reached ACTIVE 17 s after the create, faster than the two to five minutes seen before, and its SNAT rule answered PENDING_CREATE on create. (KB-CXZO, V3 live, checked 2026-11-13)
- At boot the Meraki vMX 26.1.4 KVM image mounts /dev/sr0 on /mnt/cd. With an ISO labelled cidata on a CD-ROM it logs 'Successfully mounted CDROM for VMX platform', without one 'Failed to mount CDROM for VMX platform'. (KB-D3EJ, V3 live, checked 2026-10-01)
- On 2026-10-07 the public Cisco Meraki documentation and product pages describe the vMX only as a virtual machine (AWS, Azure, GCP, Alibaba Cloud, KVM, ESXi, Private Cloud on UCS). A container or Kubernetes edition of the vMX is not described there; ask Cisco for its name and status. (KB-D6SI, V0 recheck due, docs, checked 2026-10-07)
- The Meraki vMX 26.1.4 KVM image reads its token only from the CD-ROM device /dev/sr0. The same cidata ISO on a hard disk is not used. (KB-EEL6, V3 live, checked 2026-10-01)
- In eu-de GET /v1/{project_id}/cloudservers/limits reported maxSecurityGroups 10 with totalSecurityGroupsUsed 1, while GET /v1/{project_id}/quotas of VPC reported securityGroup 26 of 100 in the same project: for the security group quota read the VPC quota call, not the ECS limits. (KB-GCPK, V3 live, checked 2026-11-11)
- The managed Secure Mail Gateway service relays outbound mail through the operator's smarthost, requires an SPF entry for the sending domain, and its documentation states that mailing per customer can be limited to 100 mails per minute. (KB-HFAR, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-16)
- On 2026-11-13 the eu-de project of a TCP test tenant had quotas of 20 VPCs and 20 public IPs (raised from 10 each), with 9 VPCs and 10 public IPs in use before the lab run. (KB-HIDR, V3 live, checked 2026-11-13)
- In eu-de PUT /v1/{project_id}/publicips/{publicip_id} with {"publicip": {"port_id": ...}} binds an existing unbound EIP with dedicated bandwidth to the first NIC port of an ECS; the EIP showed status ACTIVE on the first GET about 1 s later. (KB-HVYK, V3 live, checked 2026-11-11)
- The Meraki vMX 26.1.4 KVM image runs a virtio-net NIC as wan0, takes its address by DHCP on it and boots with virtio_net.gso=0 on its kernel command line. (KB-IU6Q, V3 live, checked 2026-10-01)
- The Cisco Meraki vMX on AWS takes its authentication token from EC2 user data: the setup guide has the token entered in the user data field under Advanced Details when the instance is launched. The same guide says the vMX on Azure, GCP and Alibaba Cloud also gets the token from a user-data field in the VM configuration. (KB-JQLW, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-30)
- On a TCP test tenant on 2026-11-11 POST /v1/{project_id}/security-group-rules answered 201 for TCP 8080 from each of the 15 Cloudflare IPv4 ranges in one security group (no rule quota hit), and an ECS web UI on 8080 then loaded through the Cloudflare proxy with an origin rule to port 8080 and SSL Full against its self-signed certificate. (KB-KNL2, V3 live, checked 2026-11-11)
- The Content Delivery Network service of the platform is built on Akamai. Its user guide index carries the title "Content Delivery Network (Akamai)" and the API reference in the mirror is a TODO placeholder. (KB-KXZ7, V1 docs, checked 2026-06-08)
- ARP traverses a VXLAN tunnel between Linux VTEPs by flood and learn. The remote MAC appears in bridge fdb show for the remote VTEP destination, so a stretched subnet works with no L3 route anywhere. (KB-M3JN, V3 live, checked 2026-08-26)
- The Meraki vMX 26.1.4 KVM image boots only with its disk on an AHCI (SATA) controller. Under QEMU it boots on q35 with SATA and on i440FX with an added AHCI controller, but not from IDE or virtio-blk. (KB-NAOF, V3 live, checked 2026-10-01)
- A new EIP appears in GET /v1/{project_id}/antiddos with protect_type default and status normal, traffic_pos_id 99 and enable_L7 false, the same values as GET /v1/{project_id}/antiddos/default-config; GET /v2/{project_id}/antiddos/query_config_list answered APIGW.0101. (KB-NZB2, V3 live, checked 2026-11-20)
- Two VPCs of one tenant peered with POST /v2.0/vpc/peerings plus a peering route each way let a server in one VPC reach SMB 445 and RDP 3389 in the other within 3 minutes of creation, and a guest address held as a two-sided virtual IP (VIP port pair = server address, server port pair = VIP) in the source subnet reached the peered VPC as its source address. (KB-OBZD, V3 live, checked 2026-11-19)
- On 2026-11-20 the eu-de project of a TCP test tenant was empty (no ECS, no VPC, no EIP; one default security group) with quotas of 10 VPCs, 10 public IPs, 5 VPN, 50 load balancers, 100 ECS instances and 800 vCPUs. (KB-QBLY, V3 live, checked 2026-11-20)
- GET /v1/{project_id}/security-group-rules/{rule_id} on the VPC v1 API in eu-de needs the full rule UUID: an 8-character prefix of an existing rule answered 404 VPC.0611 SecurityGroupRuleNotFound. Read the full id from GET /v1/{project_id}/security-groups/{sg_id}. (KB-RQA5, V3 live, checked 2026-11-11)
- A change of primary_dns with PUT /v1/{project_id}/vpcs/{vpc_id}/subnets/{subnet_id} reached a server created after it: its resolv.conf listed the new primary DNS first. Lines a Debian 12 guest writes to /dev/ttyS0 are readable through POST /v2.1/{project_id}/servers/{id}/action with os-getConsoleOutput, so probes need no guest login. (KB-TGRP, V3 live, checked 2026-11-19)
- In eu-de GET /v1/{project_id}/publicips/{publicip_id} also returns a tags array, and POST /v2.0/{project_id}/publicips/{publicip_id}/tags/action with action create answers 204 on an unbound EIP; GET /v2.0/.../tags then lists the tags sorted by key. (KB-TICK, V3 live, checked 2026-11-11)
- Section 6.5.1 of the TCP service description prices network traffic under exactly three items: VPN, internet inbound and internet outbound. (KB-TJXA, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-09-11)
- Jumbo frames on the underlay are only needed to keep a guest MTU of 1500, which requires 1550 on the wire. A guest clamped to 1450 runs over a normal 1500 underlay. Over IPsec subtract about 62 bytes of ESP overhead, about 78 bytes with NAT-T. (KB-TZV7, V1 docs, checked 2026-08-26)
- On 2026-11-10 the eu-de region project of a TCP test tenant had used 10 of 10 VPCs and 26 of 30 public IPs in GET /v1/{project_id}/quotas, so test subnets of a lab run there go into an existing VPC. (KB-UPO4, V3 live, checked 2026-11-11)
- A SonicWall SMA 8200v virtual appliance (firmware 12.5.0-02002, KVM qcow2 build) running as an ECS in eu-de terminated a Connect Tunnel SSL VPN session from a Linux client in another tenant over the internet. The client got an address from a pool inside the VPC subnet and reached RDP on another ECS in that subnet. (KB-VDM3, V3 live, checked 2026-04-15)
- With Linux VTEPs over VPC peering in eu-de a 1500 byte underlay carries an inner IP MTU of 1450. The largest payload passing with DF set is 1422 bytes, which is exactly 1500 on the wire, because the VXLAN headers take 50 bytes. (KB-XUSG, V3 live, checked 2026-08-26)
- The NAT Gateway API of TCP eu-de is /v2.0/nat_gateways and /v2.0/snat_rules without a project id in the path; POST /v2/{project_id}/nat_gateways answers APIGW.0101. A small gateway (spec 1) was ACTIVE within 5 s and an SNAT rule for a subnet with an EIP within 5 s. (KB-XZEO, V3 live, checked 2026-11-20)

## waf (29)

- The CloudWAF release note of 20 March 2025 says it only works with a dedicated load balancer created on or after 27 January 2025. (KB-2MGI, V0 recheck due, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- Dedicated WAF has resources dedicated per instance, can be spread over up to three availability zones and is reached through an internet-facing ELB. It comes as Professional WI-100 with 100 Mbit/s and 2,000 QPS or Enterprise WI-500 with 500 Mbit/s and 10,000 QPS, each with 60,000 WAF-to-server connections. (KB-2SIL, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- When a protected domain is added in Cloud WAF with POST /v1/{project_id}/waf/instance, the certificate is referenced by the field certificate_id in snake_case. The spellings certid, cert_id and certificateid each returned WAF.1001 "invalid request". (KB-5A7O, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-05-26)
- From 1 October 2026 Classic Mode gets no regular updates, patches or bug fixes. Technical support continues on a best-effort basis without any obligation to provide bug fixes or security patches and continued operation is at the customer's own risk. (KB-BKQH, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- The table in section 3.9.6 gives Cloud Mode the same metrics as Classic Mode: 300 Mbit/s, 10,000 QPS and 6,000 WAF-to-server connections per domain name. Classic Mode is connected by a CNAME DNS record change, while the access mode cell via internet-facing ELB spans both the Dedicated and the Cloud rows. (KB-BLS4, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- The WAF ignore rule call answers "Rule already exists" when the same path and rule combination is already whitelisted in that policy. (KB-BS7A, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-02-04)
- Web Application Firewall (Dedicated) (WAF) is a web application firewall whose resources are dedicated per instance: it monitors, filters and blocks HTTP and HTTPS traffic to web servers with customer-set policies and logging, can be spread over up to three availability zones and is reached through an internet-facing Elastic Load Balancer. The Regions and Endpoints page lists API endpoints for dedicated WAF in eu-de and eu-nl. (KB-CE5J, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- WAF events are listed with GET /v1/{project_id}/waf/event on waf.eu-de.otc.t-systems.com, with the from and to parameters given in epoch milliseconds; the API reference pages with offset and limit (and marker), while a live test on 2026-02-04 used pageindex and pagesize. The response key is items next to total, and each event holds id, host, url, rule, action, sip, payload and time. (KB-D4HX, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- After a dedicated WAF domain is deleted, the engine port named WAF_ENGINE_<id> with device_owner compute:<az> stays ACTIVE in its subnet. While it is there, deleting the security group returns 409 VPC.0602 SecurityGroupInUse and deleting the subnet returns 409 VPC.0209. (KB-ELHG, V3 live, checked 2026-09-16)
- Dedicated WAF accepts POST /v1/{project_id}/waf/policy without a WAF instance in eu-de (HTTP 200) and deletes such a policy with HTTP 204. A global protection whitelist rule on such a policy was refused with WAF.1001 on 2026-09-25, even with the documented example body. (KB-GA75, V3 live, checked 2026-09-25)
- A WAF ignore rule for a false alarm needs the event_id field in the request body next to the path; the API reference marks both as mandatory and gives the rule back only in the response. A body sent without event_id was answered with HTTP 400 Bad request in a live test on 2026-02-04. (KB-JADS, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- Web Application Firewall (Cloud Mode) (WAF) monitors, filters and blocks HTTP and HTTPS traffic to web servers; the customer creates, configures and monitors it, activates protection policies and logs their activity. The documentation names protection against SQL injection, cross-site scripting, web shells, command and code injection, file inclusion, CC attacks, malicious crawlers and CSRF, and a Cloud Mode instance provides the ELB access mode, in which WAF inspects traffic inside the gateway of a dedicated application load balancer (KB-TJGQ). Cloud Mode is generally available in eu-de and eu-nl since 3 March 2026 (KB-KFC4). (KB-K346, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- Cloud Mode (CloudWAF) is generally available in EU-DE and EU-NL without whitelisting since 3 March 2026. Cloud Mode and Dedicated Mode are both created and managed in the console under Web Application Firewall. (KB-KFC4, V0 recheck due, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- T Cloud Public (TCP) offers a web application firewall as Web Application Firewall in Cloud Mode and in Dedicated Mode: it monitors, filters and blocks HTTP and HTTPS traffic to web servers with customer-set protection policies and logs its activity. Dedicated Mode has its own resources per instance across up to three availability zones and is reached through an internet-facing load balancer. (KB-KIAP, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- DELETE /v1/{project_id}/waf/instance/{domain_id}?keepPolicy=false returns 204 and removes the WAF domain together with its bound policy. A separate delete of that policy afterwards answers 404 WAF.3001. (KB-KJ6E, V3 live, checked 2026-09-16)
- POST /v1/{project_id}/waf/certificate on waf.eu-de.otc.t-systems.com returns WAF.1605 "invalid certificate content format" for a well-formed multi-line PEM. With every newline removed from the certificate content and from the private key, the same request returns 200 and a certificate id. (KB-KYXU, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-05-26)
- On 2026-09-25 the Classic WAF API on waf.eu-de still answered GET /v1/{project_id}/waf/instance. The dedicated WAF API answers on its own endpoint premium-waf.eu-de and its path /v1/{project_id}/premium-waf/instance is not served on waf.eu-de. (KB-LAFN, V3 live, checked 2026-09-25)
- WAF Dedicated host create names the same certificate reference certificateid, without the underscore, so a request body that works for Cloud WAF carries the wrong field name for Dedicated. (KB-MNON, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- On eu-de no tenant API removes a dedicated WAF engine after its domain has been deleted. (KB-NAYN, V3 live, checked 2026-09-16)
- An ec-256 ECDSA certificate returned WAF.1605 from the Cloud WAF certificate upload even after the newlines were stripped, while an RSA certificate sent the same way was accepted. (KB-NQ7B, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-05-26)
- The WAF API reports the protection state of a domain in the field protect_status, where 0 means protection is off, 1 means protection is on and -1 means WAF is bypassed. (KB-OJXA, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- The successors of Classic Mode are Cloud Mode and Dedicated Mode. The service description names Cloud Mode as its strategic replacement and the release notes of 1 June 2026 recommend starting the migration now. (KB-ORQX, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- A WAF ignore rule is created with POST /v1/{project_id}/waf/policy/{policy_id}/ignore on waf.eu-de.otc.t-systems.com. The API reference lists path and event_id as the body fields, both required, and returns rule, event_type and the id of the new masking rule in the response. (KB-OX3H, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- Web Application Firewall - Modes and Metrics (WAFMM) sets the access modes and capacity of the WAF offers: the classic shared mode is connected by a CNAME DNS record change, while the access mode cell via internet-facing ELB spans the Dedicated and Cloud rows (KB-BLS4). Cloud Mode handles 300 Mbit/s, 10,000 QPS and 6,000 WAF-to-server connections per domain name; Dedicated Mode comes as Professional WI-100 (100 Mbit/s, 2,000 QPS) or Enterprise WI-500 (500 Mbit/s, 10,000 QPS), each with 60,000 WAF-to-server connections. (KB-PMPA, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- The WAF event list endpoint /v1/{project_id}/waf/event takes the from and to window as epoch milliseconds and pages its result with offset and limit per the API reference (pageindex and pagesize were used in a live test on 2026-02-04). The event_id that an ignore rule requires is taken from this list. (KB-SQUD, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- Event IDs for a WAF ignore rule come from `GET /v1/{project_id}/waf/event?from={ms}&to={ms}`, which takes the time window as millisecond timestamps. (KB-SVH6, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- A Cloud Mode instance provides the ELB access mode of WAF: WAF is built into the gateway of a dedicated load balancer with an application (HTTP/HTTPS) specification, inspects the traffic there and does not forward it itself. (KB-TJGQ, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- WAF Classic Mode is no longer bookable from 1 June 2026 and reaches End-of-Maintenance and Support on 1 October 2026. (KB-WNNJ, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- The WAF ignore rule call POST /v1/{project_id}/waf/policy/{policy_id}/ignore requires path and event_id in the body per the API reference, which lists rule only as a response field. A body without event_id returned HTTP 400 Bad Request in a live test on 2026-02-04. (KB-YECU, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)

## nat (28)

- A public NAT gateway handles TCP, UDP and ICMP only. It does not support application layer gateway (ALG) functions or the ESP and AH protocols used by IPsec and GRE tunnels. (KB-2C3A, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- An EIP used by an SNAT rule shows the private IP of the NAT gateway port as private_ip_address in GET /v1/{project_id}/publicips, and the port has device_owner network:nat_gateway. The EIP can be moved from an ECS NIC to a NAT gateway SNAT rule and keeps its address. (KB-2VYR, V3 live, checked 2026-11-04)
- In eu-de a public NAT gateway of spec 1 created with POST /v2.0/nat_gateways (router_id = VPC id, internal_network_id = subnet id) was ACTIVE about 14 s later, and an SNAT rule on an existing unbound EIP was ACTIVE about 12 s after its POST /v2.0/snat_rules; the EIP was DOWN with no port again right after the SNAT rule and the gateway were deleted. (KB-3PMT, V3 live, checked 2026-11-19)
- DNAT rules are listed with GET /v2.0/dnat_rules?nat_gateway_id=... on the NAT endpoint in eu-de. The response key is dnat_rules, and each rule holds id, floating_ip_address, external_service_port, private_ip, internal_service_port and protocol. (KB-4O52, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- Private NAT gateways are not available in eu-nl. On 2026-09-25 four private NAT v3 list paths answered 404 APIGW.0101 in eu-nl but 200 in eu-de, and section 6.5.7.2 of the service description shows n.a. for EU-NL for all four sizes. (KB-5RPQ, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- Section 6.5.7.1 of the TCP service description prices NAT Gateway by an hourly rate that depends only on the gateway size. (KB-6JVL, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-09-11)
- NAT gateway quotas cannot be changed online. A higher quota is requested from customer service by phone or email with the domain name, project name, project ID and the quota needed. (KB-6OE4, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- The service description lists five public NAT gateway types with their maximum SNAT connections and SNAT connections per second: Micro 1,000 and 100, Small 10,000 and 1,000, Medium 50,000 and 5,000, Large 200,000 and 10,000, Extra-Large 1,000,000 and 30,000. (KB-BFOA, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- A NAT gateway is created with POST /v2.0/nat_gateways on nat.eu-de.otc.t-systems.com, where router_id is the VPC id and internal_network_id the subnet id, plus spec. The call returns HTTP 201 and the status reaches ACTIVE in about two to five minutes. (KB-D3LI, V3 live, checked 2026-07-17)
- NAT gateway calls sit on the v2.0 path, https://nat.eu-de.otc.t-systems.com/v2.0/nat_gateways, and not on the usual /v2/{project_id} shape used by other services. (KB-DCXL, V3 live, checked 2026-08-18)
- SNAT rules are listed with GET /v2.0/snat_rules?nat_gateway_id=... on the NAT endpoint in eu-de. The response key is snat_rules, and each rule holds id, network_id (the subnet), floating_ip_address, status and cidr. (KB-ECCJ, V3 live, checked 2026-09-25)
- An SNAT rule uses an elastic IP that is already allocated in the same project. The address has to exist before the rule is created. (KB-FL2M, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- By default an SNAT connection of a public NAT gateway times out after 900 seconds over TCP and after 300 seconds over UDP. (KB-G2UW, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- The NAT gateway creation API on TCP documents specification type 0, micro, as supporting up to 1,000 SNAT connections. The micro size is absent from the UMN specification table. (KB-GKVF, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-09)
- A SNAT rule on a NAT gateway is bound either to a subnet through network_id or to a CIDR block through cidr; source_type 0 (VPC) allows either and source_type 1 (Direct Connect or Cloud Connect) allows only cidr. A subnet-based rule rebuilt in another project has to reference the subnet ID that exists in that project. (KB-IYYV, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- An SNAT rule for a CIDR that arrives over an enterprise router instead of a local subnet needs source_type set to 1 in the snat_rule body of POST /v2.0/snat_rules, together with nat_gateway_id, cidr and floating_ip_id. The call returns HTTP 201. (KB-JSIY, V3 live, checked 2026-07-17)
- NAT Gateway (NG) provides network address translation for servers in a VPC. A public NAT gateway lets servers in a private subnet reach the internet through a shared Elastic IP without being reachable from the internet (SNAT), and DNAT rules publish chosen ports; it comes in sizes from Micro to Extra-Large. A private NAT gateway connects a subnet with on-premises data centers or other VPCs through a transit IP address. The Regions and Endpoints page lists API endpoints for NAT Gateway in eu-de and eu-nl. (KB-KOA3, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- Creating an SNAT rule automatically adds a 0.0.0.0/0 route of type nat to the route table of the gateway's own VPC, so that route is not added by hand. (KB-NTOE, V3 live, checked 2026-07-17)
- NAT gateways are listed with GET /v2.0/nat_gateways on nat.eu-de.otc.t-systems.com, without a project_id in the path. The same listing under /v2/{project_id}/ returned HTTP 404. Each entry in nat_gateways holds name, id, status, router_id (the VPC id), spec and internal_network_id. (KB-O3F5, V4 kept live, checked 2026-09-25)
- The NAT gateway spec field is a string, and the value 0 (micro) exists besides 1 small, 2 medium, 3 large and 4 extra-large. (KB-OYWW, V3 live, checked 2026-09-17)
- A private NAT gateway takes at most 20, 50, 200 or 500 SNAT and DNAT rules in total for the Small, Medium, Large and Extra-large types. Each subnet takes one SNAT rule and SNAT and DNAT rules cannot share a transit IP address. (KB-P2XH, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- Rules on one public NAT gateway can share an EIP but different NAT gateways need different EIPs. A VPC can have several public NAT gateways and an SNAT rule cannot share an EIP with a DNAT rule whose port type is All ports. (KB-QGLV, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- NAT gateways are listed with GET /v2.0/nat_gateways on nat.eu-de.otc.t-systems.com, which returns HTTP 200 and carries no project id in the path. The variant /v2/{project_id}/nat_gateways returned HTTP 404. (KB-SCTZ, V4 kept live, checked 2026-07-17)
- Each VPC subnet takes one SNAT rule and a public NAT gateway has no limit on the number of SNAT rules. A public NAT gateway takes at most 200 DNAT rules with one DNAT rule per server port and one EIP per port. (KB-SX6T, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- If a server has its own EIP and its subnet also uses a public NAT gateway, the server sends its traffic through its own EIP. With a DNAT rule and an EIP on the same server, replies also leave through the server EIP and traffic fails when inbound and outbound EIPs differ, so the NAT FAQ advises against the combination. (KB-TH5Q, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- Public NAT gateways in eu-de are listed with GET /v2.0/nat_gateways on the NAT endpoint, without a project id in the path. GET /v2/{project_id}/nat_gateways answers 404 APIGW.0101, so a scripted inventory that uses that path counts zero NAT gateways and misses them. (KB-WD7O, V4 kept live, checked 2026-10-06)
- The NAT gateway list under /v2/{project_id}/nat_gateways returns HTTP 404 in TCP eu-de. The path that resolves is /v2.0/nat_gateways, without the project id in the path. (KB-XDZ7, V4 kept live, checked 2026-09-25)
- On 2026-11-12 the eu-de project of a TCP test tenant had no public NAT gateway and no Enterprise VPN gateway while its publicIp quota stood at 10 of 10, so a NAT gateway or a public VPN gateway there needs an existing EIP freed or a higher quota first. (KB-XMCL, V3 live, checked 2026-11-12)

## kms (27)

- A KMS grant of the default EVS key evs/default to another tenant was rejected, while the same grant on a custom KMS key was accepted. (KB-2RBG, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- The eu-de price API lists KMS keys under two records: OTC_KMS_UD_D (KMS Default Masterkey) with a zero hourly rate and OTC_KMS_UD_C (KMS Customer Masterkey) with an hourly rate, so only keys the customer creates are billed. (KB-3V62, V3 live, checked 2026-11-20)
- The console offers RSAES_OAEP_SHA_256 as wrapping algorithm while the get-parameters-for-import API also lists RSAES_PKCS1_V1_5 and RSAES_OAEP_SHA_1. The downloaded wrapping key expires after 24 hours. (KB-4WN4, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- A KMS grant on the default EVS key evs/default is refused, while the same grant on a user created KMS key is accepted. (KB-7M7Y, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- Symmetric imported material can be deleted by hand or given an expiry time (when expiry is chosen the console default is 24 hours after import). KMS deletes expired material within 24 hours while the key and its metadata stay in status Pending import and data encrypted with it cannot be decrypted until the same material is imported again. (KB-C3TF, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- In eu-de the KMS call POST /v1.0/{project_id}/kms/list-key-detail does not exist (HTTP 404 APIGW.0101). POST /v1.0/{project_id}/kms/list-keys with a limit in the body returns both the key ids and key_details, including creation_date in milliseconds, so it serves as the detailed listing. (KB-DMLV, V3 live, checked 2026-10-05)
- A disabled key or a key pending deletion is not rotated. If such a key is enabled again after one rotation period has passed, KMS rotates it within 24 hours. (KB-FPPV, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- The service description charges keys with imported material (BYOK) the same as symmetric, asymmetric and HMAC customer master keys. (KB-HUHI, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- Key Management Service (KMS) centrally creates, manages and backs up cryptographic keys for data encryption and protects them in hardware security modules (HSM); it works with keys that KMS generates and with keys the customer imports (bring your own key). KMS is integrated with services such as Object Storage Service, Elastic Volume Service and Relational Database Service, provides AES-256 keys for their encryption, and supports encryption and decryption, signing and verification and granular access control. The Regions and Endpoints page lists API endpoints for KMS in eu-de and eu-nl. (KB-HYWV, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- To bring your own material you create a custom key with key material source External (API field origin set to external). You then fetch a wrapping key and an import token, encrypt your material with the wrapping key and import it. (KB-JGGC, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- Imported asymmetric material (RSA_2048, RSA_3072, RSA_4096, EC_P256 and EC_P384) is permanently valid. It cannot be deleted by hand or given an expiry time and is removed only by deleting the whole key. (KB-KUNZ, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- T Cloud Public (TCP) offers key management with customer-managed and customer-supplied keys in Key Management Service (KMS): customers create their own keys in KMS or import their own key material (bring your own key), for example AES-256 symmetric keys or HMAC keys, and services such as OBS, EVS and RDS encrypt data with these keys. Keys with imported material behave differently from KMS-generated ones, for example in rotation (KB-UTXY, KB-RMY5). (KB-KUZK, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- A cross-domain KMS grant for EVS encryption works when grantee_principal_type is set to domain. With the default value user the grant fails. (KB-N72X, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-02-04)
- Automatic rotation changes only the key material while key ID, alias, description and permissions stay. Data is not re-encrypted and KMS keeps all versions: it encrypts with the latest and decrypts with the version that encrypted the data. (KB-RJRS, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- A key with imported material cannot be rotated. The key stays bound to its material, so after deletion or expiry only the same material can be imported again. (KB-RMY5, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- Rotation needs an enabled key whose origin is KMS. Asymmetric keys can only be rotated manually while default keys and keys with imported material cannot be rotated. (KB-SWEF, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- The platform supplies default customer master keys named evs/default and obs/default, so disk encryption and OBS default encryption work without creating a key first. Keys are listed with POST /v1.0/{project_id}/kms/list-keys filtered by key_state. (KB-UKFK, V3 live, checked 2026-06-15)
- Keys whose material KMS generated differ from keys with imported material: their material cannot be deleted by hand or given an expiry time and the symmetric ones can be rotated. (KB-UTXY, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- KMS rotates a custom symmetric key automatically once rotation is enabled for it. Rotation is off by default and the period is 30 to 365 days with 365 as default. (KB-W26T, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- Material can be imported for every key spec KMS offers: AES_256, HMAC_256, HMAC_384, HMAC_512, RSA_2048, RSA_3072, RSA_4096, EC_P256 and EC_P384. (KB-WXCI, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- Cloud Secret Management Service (CSMS) is a service for managing credentials (secrets): users and applications create, retrieve, update and delete secrets centrally throughout their lifecycle instead of hardcoding them or keeping them in plain-text configuration. The service description of 09.10.2026 announces CSMS as expected to be available from 1 April 2027, and on 2026-09-25 its API was not yet reachable in eu-de (KB-GUHJ). (KB-X6WM, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- A disabled custom key is still billable. The KMS user guide says it stops incurring charges if it is deleted. (KB-XC4T, V0 recheck due, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- Rotation is managed with the POST calls enable-key-rotation, disable-key-rotation, update-key-rotation-interval and get-key-rotation-status under /v1.0/{project_id}/kms/. The status call returns key_rotation_enabled, rotation_interval, last_rotation_time and number_of_rotations. (KB-XE5C, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- KMS cannot export a customer master key: its FAQ says keys can only be created and used in KMS and the API has no export call, only get-publickey for the public key of an asymmetric key. For imported material the docs tell you to keep your own backup so that it can be imported again after an accidental deletion. (KB-YJ57, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- For manual rotation, the only kind for asymmetric keys, the user guide names two methods (a new key B that replaces key A or a modified key A) with an example that swaps in a new custom key on the OBS console. The KMS FAQ says keys with KMS-generated material cannot be updated. (KB-ZE73, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- A KMS grant that gives another tenant access to an encrypted volume has to set grantee_principal_type to domain. (KB-ZFVZ, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- The service description bills customer master keys per active key version. Each rotation, automatic or on demand, creates an additional version that is billed. (KB-ZMQL, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)

## rds (27)

- On 2026-11-20 in eu-de GET /v3/{project_id}/flavors/mysql?version_name=8.0 returned 138 flavors, from 1 vCPU with 2 GB RAM up to 64 vCPUs with 512 GB RAM. (KB-2E3H, V3 live, checked 2026-11-20)
- GET /v3/{project_id}/flavors/{engine}?version_name={version} returns the flavors for an RDS engine version under the key flavors. On 2026-09-16 in eu-de it returned 138 flavors for mysql 8.4 and 144 for postgresql 18.
On 2026-10-19 the state is: 1:flavors.0.spec_code has rds.mysql. (KB-2NF7, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-10-19)
- T Cloud Public (TCP) offers managed relational databases: Relational Database Service (RDS) for MySQL, PostgreSQL and Microsoft SQL Server (Standard and Enterprise Edition) as single or primary/standby instances, TaurusDB as a MySQL-compatible distributed database, and Distributed Database Middleware for sharding MySQL. On 2026-09-16 the RDS engine listing in eu-de returned MySQL 8.4, 8.0 and 5.7 and PostgreSQL 13 to 18 (KB-TMQ5), and on 2026-08-24 it offered SQL Server 2017, 2019 and 2022 (KB-QLJ5). (KB-33UY, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- The RDS API reference documents the storage type change as POST /v3/{project_id}/instances/{instance_id}/action with a change_volume body that names the target volume_code; it lists no disk-type path. (KB-4HOO, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- On 2026-09-16 in eu-de the RDS datastore listing returned mariadb 10.5 with HTTP 200, while the flavors call for that engine version returned zero flavors. A datastore listing that shows a version is not proof that the version can be ordered.
On 2026-10-19 the state is: 2:flavors.0.spec_code lacks rds. (KB-6SR2, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-10-19)
- Changing the storage class of an RDS instance from CLOUDSSD to ESSD is an online operation on T Cloud Public (TCP) in eu-de. It took about 90 seconds, caused no failover and did not interrupt a psql session polling every second. (KB-7JAC, V3 live, checked 2026-03-10)
- Encryption at rest on an RDS instance is selected at create time by passing the UUID of a KMS key in disk_encryption_id. (KB-A3NA, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- An RDS flavor resize between the x1 and n1 families took about 9 minutes and triggered a failover. The standby is resized first, then the roles switch, then the former master is resized, and a client polling every second kept its connection. (KB-B6OL, V3 live, checked 2026-03-10)
- In eu-de RDS instances are listed with GET /v3/{project_id}/instances using limit and offset and come back under the response key instances next to total_count. Each entry carries name, id, status, datastore with type and version, volume with size, private_ips at instance level and nodes with id, name, role, status and availability_zone; a primary/standby entry also carries ha with replication_mode. (KB-BAWG, V4 kept live, checked 2026-09-25)
- The public price API accepts sn=rds with rn=eu-de and returns records under response.result.rds; the read on 2026-06-01 returned about 461 records. priceAmount is a string holding an hourly rate, while R12, R24, R36 and the RU12, RU24, RU36 with-upfront fields hold flat monthly reserved rates written with a thousands separator. (KB-BH5O, V3 live, checked 2026-06-01)
- On 2026-11-19 in eu-de, the RDS datastore listings returned MySQL 5.7, 8.0 and 8.4, PostgreSQL 13 to 18, and SQL Server 2017, 2019 and 2022 each as SE and EE. This matches the RDS user guide; the API reference appendix, which stops at MySQL 8.0 and PostgreSQL 17, is behind. (KB-CUSB, V3 live, checked 2026-11-19)
- In eu-de, GET /v3/{project_id}/datastores/mariadb returns HTTP 200 with version 10.5, while GET /v3/{project_id}/flavors/mariadb?version_name=10.5 returns zero flavors. MariaDB 10.5 is listed in the catalogue without being orderable in that region.
On 2026-10-19 the state is: 2:flavors.0.spec_code lacks rds. (KB-FBFG, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-10-19)
- GET /v3/{project_id}/datastores/{engine} returns HTTP 400 with error code DBS.280238 for the engine name oracle and for an invented engine name. (KB-G73U, V3 live, checked 2026-09-16)
- An RDS instance deleted and created again in the same subnet usually gets the same IP address back while that subnet is sparsely used. (KB-GOG6, V3 live, checked 2026-02-04)
- A manual AZ failover of an HA RDS instance, PUT /v3/{project_id}/instances/{instance_id}/failover with an empty JSON body, completed in about 13 seconds and stayed transparent to a client polling every second. (KB-H7UG, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-03-10)
- In an RDS create call the subnet_id must be the VPC subnet ID. Passing the neutron subnet ID makes the call fail with error DBS.200501. (KB-HSF7, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- The RDS instance describe call returns a created field with the creation time exact to the second, so an instance creation time can be read from the service API without CTS. (KB-ISMZ, V3 live, checked 2026-07-31)
- Point-in-time recovery of RDS for MySQL into an existing instance: GET /v3/{project_id}/instances/{id}/restore-time gives the window, and POST /v3/{project_id}/instances/recovery with source type timestamp and the target instance id completed as a job in 520 s for an empty MySQL 8.0 instance. (KB-J6L6, V3 live, checked 2026-11-20)
- Relational Database Service (RDS) provides managed relational databases with MySQL, PostgreSQL or Microsoft SQL Server (Standard and Enterprise Edition), with tools for automated deployment, maintenance, monitoring, backup and recovery. Each engine runs as a single instance or as a synchronous primary/standby (HA) pair with optional read replicas, and MySQL and PostgreSQL come in general purpose and dedicated flavors. The documentation describes automated backups in a one-hour time window on the days of the backup cycle (every day by default), kept for a retention period of 1 to 732 days (default 7) for all three engines. The Regions and Endpoints page lists API endpoints for RDS in eu-de and eu-nl. (KB-JQJN, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- An RDS for MySQL 8.0 HA instance (rds.mysql.n1.medium.2.ha, CLOUDSSD) created with availability_zone eu-de-01,eu-de-02 put the master in eu-de-01 and the standby in eu-de-02; a manual backup completed in 81 s, a read replica (rds.mysql.n1.medium.2.rr) was ACTIVE after 543 s, and an EIP was bound and unbound with PUT /v3/{project_id}/instances/{id}/public-ip. (KB-PV37, V3 live, checked 2026-11-20)
- On 2026-10-05 in eu-de RDS for MySQL 8.4 and RDS for PostgreSQL 18 could be ordered: the flavors call returned 138 MySQL 8.4 and 144 PostgreSQL 18 flavors in single, HA and replica mode, 78 of each with at least one AZ in state normal. The datastore listing showed MySQL 8.4, 8.0, 5.7 and PostgreSQL 13 to 18. (KB-QKU4, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-10-05)
- PUT /v3/{project_id}/instances/{id}/backups/policy with keep_days, start_time and period accepts 732 days and refuses 733 with DBS.201106 Invalid backup retention days; without period it is refused with DBS.280251 whatever the value. (KB-TPQ3, V3 live, checked 2026-11-20)
- The RDS create call takes a chosen private IP address in the data_vip field; fixed_ip is not a documented request field. Without data_vip the address is assigned from the subnet. (KB-TPVJ, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- For RDS for MySQL 8.0 in eu-de the storage types GPSSD2, CLOUDSSD and ESSD are normal in all three AZs while ULTRAHIGH and COMMON are unsupported; a create with ULTRAHIGH is refused with DBS.280241. (KB-WU42, V3 live, checked 2026-11-20)
- RDS engine versions are read with GET /v3/{project_id}/datastores/{engine}. The engine name in the path is case-insensitive, and the versions come back under the camelCase response key dataStores, each entry with id and name. (KB-YBOK, V4 kept live, checked 2026-09-16)
- An HA RDS instance is created by sending ha with mode Ha and a replication_mode that fits the engine (MySQL async or semisync, PostgreSQL async or sync, SQL Server sync) and by listing one AZ per node in availability_zone, for example eu-de-01,eu-de-02. (KB-ZEBY, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- RDS and DBS create calls take the VPC subnet id and answer DBS.200501 when the neutron subnet id is sent, which is the opposite of the dedicated ELB create. (KB-ZT4C, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-05-26)

## backup (25)

- Veeam Backup & Replication ships no hypervisor plug-in for the OpenStack/KVM platform that TCP runs on, so nothing that needs hypervisor integration can be delivered through it there. (KB-2FA3, V0 recheck due, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- The Veeam 13 Plug-in for Universal Hypervisor API supports only VergeOS and Platform9, whose vendors built the integration. Platform9 is OpenStack based, so Veeam can cover an OpenStack platform this way, but no such plug-in exists for TCP. (KB-2IMZ, V0 recheck due, docs, checked 2026-10-09)
- Veeam sizing guidance for an object-storage repository is roughly 250 TB per bucket, so a 2 PB repository on OBS is laid out over about eight buckets. (KB-2T3L, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-02)
- Veeam Backup & Replication 13 exports disks from VMware vSphere and Hyper-V VM backups and from Veeam Agent backups to VMDK, VHD or VHDX, and it can write them to any server added to its backup infrastructure. (KB-6ACV, V1 docs, checked 2026-10-09)
- A manual CBR backup (one checkpoint, crash consistent, full) of two running g7v.2xlarge.8 Windows ECS with 100 GB plus 250 GB ESSD each was available for both servers 9 minutes after the checkpoint call, without stopping the servers. (KB-BGBL, V3 live, checked 2026-11-13)
- PUT /v3/{project_id}/policies/{policy_id} on CBR changes an existing backup policy in place, name, operation_definition and trigger together: a weekly policy became daily with the pattern FREQ=DAILY;INTERVAL=1;BYHOUR=22;BYMINUTE=00 and timezone UTC+01:00, keeping its id and its vault association. (KB-BTCX, V3 live, checked 2026-11-16)
- A Veeam Backup & Replication 13 disk export in VMDK format writes two files, a descriptor and a content file, and outside an ESXi datastore the disk is thick provision lazy zeroed. A VHD or VHDX export writes one file, which fits the one-file-per-image import of IMS. (KB-BUR2, V1 docs, checked 2026-10-09)
- The Veeam Backup & Replication 13 workloads list (updated 2026-07-31) names Amazon Web Services, Microsoft Azure and Google Cloud as the only public clouds and has no OpenStack entry, so TCP, like other smaller public clouds, is not on the official Veeam support list. (KB-C7WI, V0 recheck due, docs, checked 2026-10-09)
- Veeam Backup & Replication 13 application-aware processing needs VMware Tools in the guest OS of a VMware VM. So in a Veeam recovery to TCP the tools stay on the source VMs and are removed on the restored ECS, not before the backup. (KB-CEC6, V1 docs, checked 2026-10-09)
- In AWS Backup only full-management resource types (S3, EFS, EKS and DynamoDB with advanced features) produce Backup-owned artefacts that restore into AWS only. EBS, RDS, Aurora, FSx and standard DynamoDB keep native snapshots with first-party export paths, including RDS snapshot export to Parquet in S3 and DynamoDB export to S3 as JSON or Ion. (KB-D6KV, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-01)
- Veeam v13 Universal CDP protects virtual, physical and cloud guests with an in-guest agent, so continuous replication out of a TCP instance to an on-premises vSphere target works. The guest must use UEFI boot, GPT partitioning and a supported operating system. (KB-G66F, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-02)
- The Veeam Backup & Replication 13 cmdlet Start-VBRRestoreVirtualDisks takes as -Server the Windows host to which the disks are exported, so a scripted disk export on TCP needs a Windows ECS as export target, although the user guide also accepts a Linux server for the console route. (KB-HMHD, V1 docs, checked 2026-10-09)
- Veeam v13 documentation excludes bucket lifecycle rules on a bucket used as a Veeam object-storage repository, so tiering of that bucket is driven by Veeam rather than by an OBS lifecycle policy. (KB-KVWG, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-02)
- After the Veeam backup server is lost, a new Veeam server reaches backups in an object storage repository once that repository is added and rescanned. The backups then show under Backups > Object Storage (Imported). (KB-LFZT, V1 docs, checked 2026-10-09)
- Veeam Backup & Replication 13.0, from the Veeam Data Platform Premium 13.0 installer ISO dated 2026-03-10, installed and ran in standalone mode (backup server, proxy and repository on one host, bundled PostgreSQL configuration database, 30-day evaluation licence) on a Windows Server 2022 ECS built from the eu-de public image Enterprise_Windows-Server_2022_STD_amd64_bios_latest, flavor s3.2xlarge.2 (8 vCPU, 16 GB) as read back on 2026-10-09. (KB-MJY3, V3 live, checked 2026-04-22)
- Veeam Backup & Replication 13 does not accept the backup server as the target of a disk export (the user guide gives security reasons), so a recovery chain that exports disks on TCP needs a second Windows or Linux server added to the Veeam infrastructure as the export target. (KB-NTSU, V1 docs, checked 2026-10-09)
- Veeam v13 SureBackup has a "Backup verification and content scan only" mode that runs without a virtual lab, so it works against a backup repository hosted on TCP. (KB-OIH7, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-02)
- POST /v3/{project_id}/vaults/{vault_id}/removeresources on CBR also deletes the backups of the removed server: its only backup turned to status deleting 20 s after the call, while the vault still listed the server in resources at that moment. (KB-PYHJ, V3 live, checked 2026-11-16)
- The Veeam Backup & Replication 13 PowerShell reference lists Add-VBRAmazonS3CompatibleRepository, the cmdlet that adds an S3 compatible object storage repository such as OBS, for the Enterprise, Enterprise Plus and Veeam Universal License editions only, not for Standard. (KB-S7MW, V1 docs, checked 2026-10-09)
- A CBR backup policy is attached to a vault with POST /v3/{project_id}/vaults/{vault_id}/associatepolicy and the body policy_id; PUT on that path answers 404 APIGW.0101. A weekly policy is created with POST /v3/{project_id}/policies, operation_definition max_backups and timezone, and trigger.properties.pattern FREQ=WEEKLY;BYDAY=SA;BYHOUR=22;BYMINUTE=00. (KB-T7WV, V3 live, checked 2026-11-16)
- A Veeam perpetual per-socket licence covers only VMs running on the licensed source hosts. A workload moved to TCP leaves that socket licence behind and needs an instance-based licence. (KB-TJYJ, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-02)
- After POST .../vaults/{vault_id}/removeresources the CBR vault still listed the removed server and its allocated size for at least 80 s; about one minute later resources showed only the remaining server and billing.allocated had dropped by that server's disk size (700 to 350 GB). (KB-VO5W, V3 live, checked 2026-11-16)
- In Veeam the storage-class selector exists only on the Amazon S3 repository type. An OBS bucket is added as the "S3 compatible with data archiving" repository type to get an archive tier. (KB-X7SP, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-02)
- Veeam Backup & Replication 13 consumes licences only to back up data. A restore needs no licence, so restoring VMs to TCP after a disaster uses none. Backing up the restored VMs on TCP with Veeam afterwards does need licences. (KB-YQRM, V1 docs, checked 2026-10-09)
- Each Veeam step of a disk-export recovery to TCP has a Veeam Backup & Replication 13 PowerShell cmdlet: Add-VBRAmazonS3CompatibleRepository adds the OBS bucket as an S3 compatible repository, Sync-VBRBackupRepository rescans it and Start-VBRRestoreVirtualDisks exports disks of VMware or Hyper-V backups as VMDK, VHD or VHDX. (KB-ZDJ2, V1 docs, checked 2026-10-09)

## sdrs (25)

- A planned SDRS switchover has as a precondition that all servers in the protection group are stopped, and the documented procedure stops them as one of its steps. (KB-2DJW, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-02)
- SDRS uses storage-based synchronous replication. It ensures disk data consistency but cannot ensure application consistency. (KB-3DVW, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-02)
- SDRS commits to an RTO within 30 minutes, excluding DNS, security group and customer script steps. (KB-6SBH, V1 docs, checked 2026-08-26)
- GET /v1/{project_id}/sdrs/quotas returns the SDRS quotas. In eu-de the listing showed a quota of 10 server groups (protection groups) and 100 replication pairs. (KB-AYIP, V4 kept live, checked 2026-08-26)
- In a controlled A/B on two identical s3.large.2 servers with the same heavy write load on the root filesystem, both the SDRS failover arm and the CBR restore arm produced a server that did not come up. One run per arm, and the superblock evidence exists only for the CBR arm. (KB-CIYG, V3 live, checked 2026-09-02)
- An EVS disk from an s9 server replicates under SDRS, fails over and comes back byte-exact, although the s9 server itself is refused as a protected instance. Attaching the replica while the pair exists returns Ecs.0205. DELETE /replications/{id} with delete_target_volume false removes the pair and keeps both volumes. (KB-EKA5, V3 live, checked 2026-08-26)
- POST /v1/{project_id}/protected-instances creates an SDRS protected instance from a server group id and a server id. The optional flavorRef sets the flavour of the DR-site server and defaults to the production flavour. (KB-F2T4, V3 live, checked 2026-08-26)
- On two test tenants in eu-de, SDRS operations produced zero CTS traces while other services wrote traces in the same window. The CTS documentation lists SDRS as a supported service with 24 trace names. (KB-HBF5, V3 live, checked 2026-09-02)
- POST /v1/{project_id}/replications creates an SDRS replication pair from a server group id, a volume id and a name, and the request carries no flavour field at all. Deleting the pair with delete_target_volume false keeps both volumes. (KB-HQXJ, V3 live, checked 2026-08-26)
- SDRS protection group actions go to POST /v1/{project_id}/server-groups/{id}/action with the action keys start-server-group, stop-server-group, failover-server-group and reverse-server-group. An action key of failover alone was answered with HTTP 400 SDRS.0206, invalid action. (KB-HRVR, V3 live, checked 2026-08-26)
- An SDRS DR drill runs from the disk snapshots of the DR site servers, not from a live replica, and it leaves protection running. The drill server inherits the specification of the DR side. Matching addressing is required only when an existing VPC is reused, error SDRS.1909. (KB-KPGI, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-02)
- An SDRS failover does not start the DR site server. The server is started by hand afterwards, which took 22 seconds to reach ACTIVE. Switchover behaves the same way. (KB-MMA3, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-02)
- Creating an SDRS protection group with POST /v1/{project_id}/server-groups is asynchronous. The call returns a job_id, and GET /v1/{project_id}/jobs/{job_id} reports status SUCCESS or FAIL and carries the new server group id under entities. (KB-MN6X, V3 live, checked 2026-08-26)
- SDRS.1353 is an AZ inventory condition, not a generation gate. SDRS builds the DR server in the paired AZ, and every s7n and m7n size plus every c7n.*.2 size is absent from eu-de-02, while c7n.large.4, which is offered there, protects with no extra parameter. (KB-NMLI, V3 live, checked 2026-08-26)
- SDRS replication and recovery from a CBR backup deliver the same consistency class, crash-consistent. The difference is the age of the copy: replication is current at the moment of the failure, a backup is as old as its last run. (KB-OXBI, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-02)
- When an SDRS failover is confirmed, the production servers show a blue screen of death or are forcibly stopped, so the resulting copy is crash-consistent by definition. (KB-OXLO, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-02)
- Storage Disaster Recovery Service (SDRS) replicates the block storage data of servers to another availability zone, so that after an outage of one availability zone the servers are started with current data in the other zone. A DR drill function tests the disaster recovery at any time. The Regions and Endpoints page lists the SDRS API endpoint in eu-de. (KB-QJ3S, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- POST /v1/{project_id}/protected-instances/{id}/resize changes flavours: production_flavorRef affects the production server, dr_flavorRef the DR server, flavorRef both, and when flavorRef is present the other two are ignored. The servers being changed must be SHUTOFF and the protection group available or protected. (KB-ROOO, V3 live, checked 2026-08-26)
- GET /v1/{project_id}/active-domains lists the SDRS replication domains with their local and remote replication clusters, which is how the usable AZ pair is read. In eu-de the listing showed one domain, local cluster eu-de-02 and remote cluster eu-de-01. (KB-SWH2, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-10-14)
- SDRS failover with dr_type migration moves the network identity. The old production server ends SHUTOFF with no addresses and the DR server comes up with the same private IP and the same EIP. Binding a new EIP returns VPC.0511 PortInUse, and reachability takes 40 to 60 seconds. (KB-UC5C, V3 live, checked 2026-08-26)
- An SDRS drill created with POST /v1/{project_id}/disaster-recovery-drills needs a drill VPC different from the protection group VPC, otherwise error SDRS.1907, and that VPC has to carry the same subnet CIDR block, otherwise error SDRS.1909. (KB-WGOJ, V3 live, checked 2026-08-26)
- SDRS on T Cloud Public (TCP) answers under https://sdrs.eu-de.otc.t-systems.com/v1/{project_id}. The same paths called against the eu-nl SDRS host returned HTTP 404 with error APIGW.0101. (KB-WVBD, V3 live, checked 2026-08-26)
- Across one full SDRS lifecycle CTS recorded 1018 traces over 9 hours across 11 service types and none from SDRS, with an active tracker and with ECS, EVS, VPC and CBR logging normally. None of the 24 documented SDRS trace names appeared. (KB-X6YB, V3 live, checked 2026-08-26)
- In eu-de, creating an SDRS protected instance from an s9, m9 or c9 ECS returned 400 SDRS.1353, also when a supported DR-site flavour was named explicitly. (KB-XGSC, V3 live, checked 2026-08-26)
- An SDRS-protected server resizes in place from s3 to s7n with POST /protected-instances/{id}/resize and body {"resize":{"production_flavorRef":"s7n.large.2"}}. The protected instance stays protected, the DR server stays s3 and replication stays active at 100 percent with no re-seed. Failover, reprotect and planned failback of the asymmetric pair work. (KB-XHYZ, V3 live, checked 2026-08-26)

## dns (24)

- A signed parent zone's denial of DS stays in resolver caches after a DS is published. Measured caps on the public resolvers: Google 1800 s, Quad9 about 3600 s, Cloudflare honours the parent SOA MINIMUM in full. (KB-34BU, V3 live, checked 2026-09-10)
- Private DNS zones on TCP resolve the cloud-internal namespace. (KB-3DUD, V1 docs, checked 2026-07-17)
- A private DNS zone is created with POST /v2/zones, zone_type private and router.router_id of a VPC with router_region eu-de, and is ACTIVE at once; an A record set in it is ACTIVE at once too. (KB-3QS6, V3 live, checked 2026-11-20)
- GET /v2/zones/{zone_id}/dnssec on the eu-de DNS endpoint returns 200 with status DISABLE, or the key object with flag 257, key_tag, digest, digest_algorithm SHA256, digest_type 2, signature ECDSAP256SHA256, signature_type 13, ksk_public_key, ds_record, created_at, updated_at and status ENABLE. (KB-4KAY, V3 live, checked 2026-09-10)
- In eu-de on 2026-09-25, GET /v2/zones/{zone_id}/nameservers returned ns1.open-telekom-cloud.com and ns2.open-telekom-cloud.com for all four public zones of a test project, and a domain hosted in the TCP DNS service is delegated to these two name servers at its registrar. An NS lookup of such a domain names the platform. (KB-5KZD, V3 live, checked 2026-09-25)
- DNSSEC is absent from the DNS api-ref, and the opentelekomcloud Terraform provider 1.37.8 ships no DNSSEC resource. (KB-5SWB, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-10)
- On a TCP public zone with DNSSEC enabled, the parent side returns no authenticated denial of DS for a delegated child zone, so validating resolvers cannot resolve names below such a delegation, wherever the child is hosted. Keep child names in the parent zone, or leave DNSSEC off while a delegation is needed. (KB-5T6U, V3 live, checked 2026-09-10)
- Enabling DNSSEC works on a subdomain public zone on TCP, although the console banner says DNSSEC can be enabled only for top-level domains and the UMN says DNSSEC supports only primary domain names. (KB-6PPX, V3 live, checked 2026-09-10)
- In eu-de the regional service endpoint for OMS has no DNS record, while the DRS endpoint in the same region resolves and its gateway answers HTTP 404 for an unknown path. (KB-7QD4, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- Domain Name Service (DNS) is an authoritative DNS service in which the customer configures DNS zones and records for internal and external communication. It provides public zones that resolve domain names on the internet, private zones that resolve names to private IP addresses inside VPCs, and reverse resolution of IP addresses to domain names. The Regions and Endpoints page lists API endpoints for DNS in eu-de and eu-nl. (KB-ABGI, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- TCP DNS rejects DNSKEY as a record set type and refuses both AXFR and IXFR, so a second provider's ZSK cannot be published and a signing secondary cannot be fed. RFC 8901 multi-signer is not possible with TCP DNS as a participant. (KB-CWDY, V3 live, checked 2026-09-10)
- CTS logs DNSSEC switching as the operations enableDnssecConfig and disableDnssecConfig with resource_type dnssec and trace_type ConsoleAction, while a scripted record-set create appears with trace_type ApiCall. (KB-DR5A, V3 live, checked 2026-09-10)
- A DNS zone object carries no dnssec field, and a dnssec key in the zone-create body is accepted and ignored. (KB-H2BV, V3 live, checked 2026-09-10)
- A zone delegated to TCP DNS validates end to end when its parent is hosted off-platform and publishes the DS: the child is signed by TCP DNS and resolvers report it fully validated. (KB-IGAE, V3 live, checked 2026-09-10)
- Re-keying a signed TCP public zone is a disable and re-enable of DNSSEC, which mints a brand new KSK with a different key tag and no overlap with the old one. The documented order is to remove the DS at the parent first, then disable, otherwise the zone goes bogus instead of dropping to unsigned. (KB-LS5E, V3 live, checked 2026-09-10)
- On T Cloud Public (TCP) in eu-de the DNS v2 call GET /v2/zones?type=private returns HTTP 200. The response carries the keys zones, links and metadata.total_count, so private zones come from the same v2 zones path as public ones, selected by the type query parameter. (KB-MRFV, V3 live, checked 2026-07-02)
- Each of the nine EIPs of a test project in eu-de answered a reverse DNS lookup on 2026-09-25 with a default name that starts with ecs-, carries the address with dashes and ends in reverse.open-telekom-cloud.com, while the DNS user guide shows the pattern ecs-xx-xx-xx-xx under a compute domain. The guide says a PTR record created in the DNS service replaces this default and deleting it brings the default back. (KB-NIB4, V3 live, checked 2026-09-25)
- A signed TCP public zone that delegates nothing beneath it works. The platform does not insert a child DS automatically when parent and child are both public zones in the same tenant. (KB-POVU, V3 live, checked 2026-09-10)
- The TCP DNS signer is online and signs per RRset at write time, separately on each nameserver. The RRSIG validity window runs from 30 days before signing to 730 days after, so a published window reads 760 days wide and one zone carries several windows at once. (KB-RCXE, V3 live, checked 2026-09-10)
- Default DNS quota on TCP is 50 public zones and 500 record sets. (KB-TWRY, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-10)
- The DNS DNSSEC resource is registered read-only. POST, PUT, PATCH and DELETE on /v2/zones/{id}/dnssec return 404 APIGW.0101 with the text "method POST not found", both with AK/SK signing and with IAM tokens. (KB-W7XD, V3 live, checked 2026-09-10)
- The DNS fine-grained action list on TCP has no action for the DNSSEC operation, so it cannot be separated from zone administration in an IAM policy. The audit service records the operation. (KB-XFW6, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-10)
- Wildcard records and underscore-prefixed names resolve and validate on a DNSSEC-signed TCP public zone. A wildcard answer carries an RRSIG with the correct label count plus the NSEC proving the queried name does not exist. (KB-XWFC, V3 live, checked 2026-09-10)
- A DNS check on 2026-09-25 showed the eu-de endpoint hosts rms, tms, cfw and the Cloud Connect host cc.eu-de resolving, while the host config.eu-de did not resolve. In the check of 2026-07-17 cc.eu-de had no record yet. (KB-ZH7A, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-09-25)

## dataarts (23)

- The CDM API is rate limited to 5 requests per minute and answers APIGW.0308 above that rate. (KB-22H2, V3 live, checked 2026-07-30)
- Incremental file migration in CDM is the destination option toJobConfig.duplicateFileOpType set to SKIP, not a separate incremental flag. In an OBS to OBS test an empty target copied 28 files, an unchanged rerun copied 0 and a run after one new file copied 1. REPLACE recopies everything. (KB-5HTE, V3 live, checked 2026-07-30)
- In eu-de the DataArts Studio endpoint prefixes dayu, dayu-dlf and cdm all resolve to the same address. (KB-6IFY, V3 live, checked 2026-07-30)
- DataArts Factory calls honour only the header named workspace, and it must be inside the AK/SK signature. The variants workspaceId, instance-id and dayu-workspace are ignored and the call answers 200 with an empty job list, while a wrong workspace id answers 400 DLF.0819 Workspace does not exists. (KB-6IM3, V3 live, checked 2026-07-30)
- The endpoint prefixes dataartsstudio, dataarts, dgc, dlf and dayu-cdm have no DNS record in eu-de. (KB-D4PF, V3 live, checked 2026-07-30)
- The service description, revision 09.10.2026, offers DataArts Studio in eu-de as a Starter version without data governance and as an Enterprise version with Data Architecture, Data Quality, Data Catalog, Data Security and Data Service, while its table lists the Data Service (DLM) flavors as n.a. for EU-DE. On the test tenant on 2026-09-25 only the Factory and Migration APIs answered; the documented paths of the other modules returned APIGW.0101. (KB-FEC6, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- The DataArts Factory error DLF.3025 "The schedule parameter is invalid" is commonly caused by a missing processType field, which is a different field from the schedule parameters. (KB-GBHW, V3 live, checked 2026-07-30)
- DataArts Factory in eu-de rejects the node types MRSHetuEngine and PRESTO with DLF.2013 The processor type parameter is invalid, and HetuEngineSQL as a node with DLF.2014 The job object type is invalid, so a HetuEngine script can be authored but not scheduled natively. (KB-KAFE, V3 live, checked 2026-07-30)
- A DataArts Factory job schedule needs cron.startTime with an ISO 8601 offset, and cron.expressionTimeZone in the GMT+2 form rather than an IANA zone name such as Europe/Berlin. (KB-LGPE, V3 live, checked 2026-07-30)
- A DataArts Studio instance, a workspace and a Migration (CDM) cluster have to be created in the console in eu-de. Everything below those three objects is API-driven. (KB-LONI, V3 live, checked 2026-07-30)
- A DataArts Factory job body requires processType with value BATCH or REAL_TIME. Omitting it returns DLF.3025 The schedule parameter is invalid, which points at the wrong field. (KB-MZN5, V3 live, checked 2026-07-30)
- DataArts Migration skips files that were already transferred when toJobConfig.duplicateFileOpType is set to SKIP, which covers incremental file loads. (KB-O5QK, V3 live, checked 2026-07-30)
- DataArts Factory job listing honours only the workspace request header. With workspaceId, instance-id or dayu-workspace instead, the call answers HTTP 200 with {"jobs":[],"total":0} for the default scope. (KB-PNCE, V3 live, checked 2026-07-30)
- DataArts Studio in eu-de is torn down in the console only: read, delete and stop on a single Factory job answer 404 APIGW.0101, and the deletion is a console chain of jobs, data connection, migration clusters and instance. A service the service description lists can have functions without an API, while an API can still answer for a service no longer offered (BMS). (KB-QSZB, V3 live, checked 2026-07-31)
- DataArts Studio (DS) is a data lifecycle management platform for data integration, data development, data governance and data services, from data ingestion to the delivery of data services. The Starter version covers the initial setup of data lake projects and ETL tasks without governance features; the Enterprise version adds data architecture, data quality, data catalog, data security and data service features. The Regions and Endpoints page lists the DataArts Studio endpoints in eu-de. (KB-R52P, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- DataArts Studio provides CDM for batch data movement and Factory for orchestration, and MRS provides Kafka and Flink for streaming pipelines. (KB-SWZZ, V0 recheck due, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- The DataArts CDM job failure "Run fail.Execute rest api job fail." after about 135 seconds points to a missing network route between the CDM agent and the target, for example when the two sit in different VPCs. (KB-TL3G, V3 live, checked 2026-07-30)
- A minute-level cron in a DataArts Factory job needs both a range and an interval. The expression 0 0-59/2 * * * ? is accepted, 0 0/10 * * * ? returns DLF.1006 and 0 */10 * * * ? returns DLF.3025. (KB-TML3, V3 live, checked 2026-07-30)
- On eu-de, HetuEngine is scheduled from DataArts Factory with a generic RESTAPI node that calls the Trino statement API. (KB-UCCW, V3 live, checked 2026-07-30)
- Once a CDM cluster exists, its links and jobs are API-driven in eu-de: GET /v1.1/{project_id}/clusters and /datastores, POST /v1.1/{project_id}/clusters/{cluster_id}/cdm/link (connector-name obs-connector) and /cdm/job, PUT /cdm/job/{name}/start, GET /cdm/job/{name}/status. (KB-V65H, V3 live, checked 2026-07-30)
- In eu-de the DataArts Factory node types HiveSQL, SMN and RESTAPI ran successfully, HiveSQL verified against an MRS cluster. (KB-XHDJ, V3 live, checked 2026-07-30)
- On 2026-09-25 the CDM flavor listing in eu-de (GET /v1.1/{project_id}/datastores/{datastore_id}/flavors) returned five flavors for the current cluster version 2.10.0.300: cdm.small (2 vCPU / 4 GB), cdm.medium (4/8), cdm.large (8/16), cdm.xlarge (16/32) and cdm.4xlarge (64/128). (KB-XI6O, V0 recheck due, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- A DataArts Factory job in eu-de has no run-on-demand API. A job runs when its schedule fires, or it is started from the console. (KB-XXDO, V3 live, checked 2026-07-30)

## eip (22)

- An EIP is ordered with POST /v1/{project_id}/publicips on vpc.eu-de.otc.t-systems.com, with publicip.type 5_bgp and a bandwidth object carrying size, share_type PER and charge_mode traffic. The call returns HTTP 200 and DELETE /v1/{project_id}/publicips/{publicip_id} returns HTTP 204. (KB-3OQ3, V3 live, checked 2026-07-17)
- In the EIP list GET /v1/{project_id}/publicips an EIP bound to a load balancer shows status ELB with an empty port_id, and a VPN-bound one shows status VPN. Only port-bound EIPs show ACTIVE, and DOWN means unbound. (KB-4T6L, V3 live, checked 2026-09-17)
- The default publicIp quota in the VPC quota response is 10 per project in eu-de. (KB-67YF, V3 live, checked 2026-08-25)
- An unbound elastic IP is rebound by reading port_id from interfaceAttachments[0] of GET /v1/{project_id}/cloudservers/{server_id}/os-interface, then sending PUT /v1/{project_id}/publicips/{publicip_id} on the VPC endpoint with publicip.port_id set. The rebind answered HTTP 200. (KB-7IM4, V3 live, checked 2026-06-17)
- EIP traffic is billed on outbound internet traffic only, in volume tiers; inbound internet traffic carries no charge. The service description of 09.10.2026 lists no billing element for bandwidth size and the bandwidths checked live in eu-de on 2026-09-25 all use charge_mode traffic, although the v3 EIP API reference still lists bandwidth and 95peak_plus as charge_mode values. (KB-ABSZ, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- GET /v1/{project_id}/publicips returns the elastic IPs allocated in a project and is the check for whether a given public address is still held. (KB-CFUN, V4 kept live, checked 2026-09-16)
- A RIPE database lookup on 2026-09-25 of all nine EIPs of a test project in eu-de returned inetnum objects maintained by the operator's network information centre: seven with a netname that carries the operator's name and the platform's name as description, two with netnames of the operator's general address space. So the whois of these nine addresses named the operator for all of them and the platform for seven. (KB-DBXO, V3 live, checked 2026-09-25)
- ECS create returns Ecs.0013 Insufficient EIP quota once the EIP quota is reached. An EIP that is bound to no port still counts against that quota and is still billed. (KB-FHCJ, V3 live, checked 2026-06-17)
- Elastic IP (EIP) provides static public IP addresses that connect resources such as Elastic Cloud Servers or load balancers with the internet, for inbound and outbound UDP, TCP and ICMP traffic. Outbound SMTP is opened on ports 465 and 587 only towards the Secure Mail Gateway, and the Mail BGP EIP type carries outbound SMTP on port 25. The Regions and Endpoints page lists EIP under the VPC API endpoints in eu-de and eu-nl. (KB-FQSW, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- Anti-DDoS (AD) protects the public IP addresses of the Elastic IP service against distributed denial-of-service attacks: it monitors the traffic from the internet to each EIP and restricts attack traffic once the thresholds set in the defense policy are exceeded. The documentation adds cleaning of network-layer and application-layer DDoS traffic, immediate alarms on a detected attack and monitoring reports. The Regions and Endpoints page lists API endpoints for Anti-DDoS in eu-de and eu-nl. (KB-HWYV, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- Public IP addresses on TCP register in the operator's address space (AS3320), so WHOIS and traceroute against a backend address show the operator even when a proxy on another provider fronts the service with a custom domain and certificate. (KB-HZEY, V1 docs, checked 2026-05-21)
- A released EIP returns to the shared address pool and can be allocated to a different tenant. A DNS A record left pointing at that address then resolves to an address under another tenant's control. (KB-L2QZ, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- Ordinary 5_bgp elastic IPs cannot be used for email services; the Mail BGP (5_mailbgp) type, assigned after the account administrator grants permission, is for outbound SMTP on port 25. Per the service description of 09.10.2026, ports 465 and 587 pass the central firewall only toward the Secure Mail Gateway Service, although the EIP user guide lists 25, 465 and 587 for Mail BGP. (KB-LU3H, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- An elastic IP that is allocated and bound to nothing keeps billing until it is released: the service description bills the Elastic IP by the hour as an element of its own, separate from traffic, with no exception for an unbound address. (KB-MZEP, V0 recheck due, contract, checked 2026-10-14)
- Resource tags on an EIP are written with POST /v2.0/{project_id}/publicips/{publicip_id}/tags/action carrying action create and a tags array, which returns HTTP 204. The same v2.0 tags/action shape works on vpcs/{vpc_id}. (KB-N44O, V3 live, checked 2026-07-17)
- The elastic IP type, for example Mail BGP, is set when the EIP is assigned and stays fixed for the life of that EIP. (KB-OSK3, V1 docs, checked 2026-09-16)
- On T Cloud Public (TCP) in eu-de, an allocated but unattached elastic IP reports status DOWN with no port binding.
